# OAGW docs vs. implementation: decisions for review

This document lists every place where the OAGW gear's documentation, scenarios or e2e tests disagree with its code, and the decision taken for each one. Nothing has been fixed yet. The review comes first: once each decision is confirmed or challenged, the fixes go into the PRs listed under [Proposed fix PRs](#proposed-fix-prs).

- **Baseline:** upstream `main` at [`66da80e57`](https://github.com/constructorfabric/gears-rust/commit/66da80e5715e10c08c6fafd5e9832137389a3355). Every link and line number points at this commit.
- **Scope:** docs in `gears/system/oagw/docs` (PRD, DESIGN, DECOMPOSITION, feature docs, ADRs, schemas), scenarios in `gears/system/oagw/scenarios`, e2e tests in `testing/e2e/suites/oagw`, and the code in `gears/system/oagw/oagw` and `gears/system/oagw/oagw-sdk` with its Rust tests.

## How to review

1. Find your area in the [Summary for reviewers](#summary-for-reviewers) or the [Decision index](#decision-index).
2. Each item has its own heading with its ID. Read the decision, then check it against the quoted docs and code. Every quote and snippet is verbatim from the baseline, and every link opens the exact lines.
3. Reply per ID, for example:
   - `M-01: agree`
   - `R-03: disagree, because descendants must never raise an inherited limit`
4. Items under [Mechanical doc fixes](#mechanical-doc-fixes) only bring docs in line with the code. They need less scrutiny, but you can reply to them by ID in the same way.

Outcome codes and priorities are explained in the [glossary](#glossary). Where a decision contradicts an accepted ADR or the PRD, the item says so, and it is listed under [Needs the most attention](#needs-the-most-attention).

## Summary for reviewers

There are **120 items**: 117 from the areas §1–§12, plus ADR-0010, CFG-HTTP and CFG-VAL. 36 are [mechanical doc fixes](#mechanical-doc-fixes) that sit in tables; the other 84 have their own heading.

### Counts per outcome

An item with a combined outcome (for example `FUTURE` + `FIX-CODE`) counts under each of its codes, so the "includes" column adds up to more than 120.

### Needs the most attention

#### P0: security and data correctness

- [M-01](#m-01-any-authenticated-caller-can-create-change-and-delete-its-tenants-upstreams-and-routes): any authenticated caller can create, change and delete its tenant's upstreams and routes. There is no per-operation permission check.
- [P-02](#p-02-percent-encoded--in-the-proxy-path-reaches-the-upstream-unchanged): percent-encoded dot-segments (`%2e%2e`, `..%2f`) pass path normalisation and reach the upstream, so a route prefix can be escaped.
- [R-04](#r-04-an-ancestors-private-auth-rate-limit-and-plugins-reach-descendants-that-omit-the-field): an ancestor's `private` auth, rate limit and plugins leak to descendants that omit the field, including the ancestor's credential reference.
- [R-05](#r-05-descendants-and-routes-can-bypass-an-ancestors-enforced-plugins-and-rate-limit): enforced ancestor plugins and limits can be bypassed on four paths, including a route with default sharing.
- [R-07](#r-07-scopeip-trusts-client-supplied-x-forwarded-for-and-buckets-are-never-evicted): `scope=ip` rate limits trust a client-supplied `X-Forwarded-For`, and buckets are never evicted.

#### Decisions that need product sign-off (they change or defer a PRD requirement)

None of these outcomes carries the `PRD` code, but each one changes PRD text or marks a PRD **MUST** as planned.

- [M-02](#m-02-the-plugin-management-api-plugins-does-not-exist-but-the-docs-describe-it-and-tick-its-entity-and-repository): the plugin management API (PRD §4.1) is marked planned.
- [PLG-01](#plg-01-six-cataloged-plugins-basic-bearer-timeout-cors-logging-metrics-have-no-implementation-but-some-docs-still-promise-them): Basic and Bearer auth, which the PRD lists as supported, are marked planned.
- [PLG-08](#other-doc-only-fixes-plugins): API-key placement in the query string is marked planned.
- [PLG-09](#other-doc-only-fixes-plugins): the PRD's "by UUID reference" wording for secrets changes to `cred://` names.
- [PLG-10](#plg-10-a-missing-secret-gives-500-while-an-unreachable-credential-store-or-idp-gives-401-and-the-status-differs-by-plugin): the PRD says every auth-plugin failure is 401. The decision maps outages to 503, bad IdP responses to 502 and misconfiguration to 500.
- [R-10](#r-10-strategy-queue-and-degrade-are-accepted-but-behave-as-reject-the-queue-block-is-dropped): the `queue` and `degrade` rate-limit strategies in a PRD MUST list are marked planned.
- [R-11](#r-11-circuit-breaker-concurrency-limit-and-backpressure-fields-are-silently-accepted-and-ignored): circuit breaker, concurrency limits and backpressure (a PRD MUST) are marked planned.
- [R-18](#status-markers-checkbox-sync): the PRD priority marker for rate limiting and its scope list change.
- [STR-09](#str-09-grpc-and-webtransport-configs-are-accepted-but-grpc-traffic-never-works-and-webtransport-is-proxied-as-plain-https): WebTransport (a p1 PRD MUST) and gRPC are marked planned.
- [O-01](#o-01-operators-get-6-of-the-12-documented-metrics-pushed-over-otlp-there-is-no-metrics-endpoint): the PRD's `/metrics` endpoint is dropped. Metrics are pushed over OTLP.
- [O-04](#o-04-no-audit-log-config-change-log-or-log-sampling-and-the-trace-breaks-at-oagw): audit logging and "100% of requests logged" (a PRD MUST) are marked planned.

#### Decisions that amend or annotate an accepted ADR, or deviate from a DESIGN principle

- [R-03](#r-03-under-an-inherit-ancestor-a-child-can-raise-its-own-limit-above-the-ancestors): ADR-0004 (accepted) needs an amendment. An `inherit` child with permission may raise its own limit.
- [R-02](#r-02-a-child-cannot-be-created-under-an-enforce-parent-that-has-an-allocated-budget), [R-17](#r-17-rate-limit-headers-do-not-follow-the-ietf-draft-the-docs-cite), [R-10](#r-10-strategy-queue-and-degrade-are-accepted-but-behave-as-reject-the-queue-block-is-dropped): ADR-0004 gains the "child rate limit required under an allocated budget" rule, new rate-limit headers and a planned marker on the strategy enum.
- [PLG-13](#plg-13-route-level-cors-is-ignored-unless-sharing-inherit-and-a-descendants-own-private-or-enforce-cors-is-dropped): ADR-0006 (accepted, CORS) gets a "deferred" note. CORS is taken out of OAGW for now.
- [A-02](#a-02-the-documented-multi-layer-config-cache-l1l2redis-does-not-exist): ADR-0007 and ADR-0008 (accepted, config caching) get "deferred until persistence" notes.
- [P-10](#p-10-rate-limiting-runs-last-after-credential-lookups-token-fetches-and-guards): ADR-0003's execution order changes (rate limiting moves before the auth plugin).
- [PLG-16](#other-doc-only-fixes-plugins): ADR-0003 gets a status note. External plugins are planned.
- [M-04](#m-04-the-upstream-protocol-field-accepts-any-string-and-is-never-used), [M-06](#other-doc-only-fixes-management-api), [P-18](#p-18-the-query-string-is-re-encoded-headoptions-cant-be-routed-and-the-host-port-rule-ignores-the-scheme): ADR-0002 gets a note on `protocol`, a corrected example and a wording fix.
- [P-07](#p-07-error-type-ids-field-names-and-resource-identifiers-dont-match-what-oagw-sends): the ADR-0013 appendix gets the canonical error types.
- [ADR-0010](#adr-0010-the-resource-identification-adr-is-still-proposed-and-describes-alias-pools-tenant-bindings-and-list-filters-that-were-never-built), [STR-10](#str-10-adr-0015-describes-a-rust-client-library-that-does-not-exist), [A-04](#adr-statuses): ADR-0010 is revised and accepted, ADR-0015 is superseded by a new SDK ADR, and ADR-0001's status goes from `superseded` back to `accepted`.
- [CFG-HTTP](#cfg-http-the-mini-chat-helm-chart-turns-on-plaintext-http-upstreams-against-the-https-only-constraint): `WONTFIX` on the mini-chat chart's `allow_http_upstream: true`, an exception to DESIGN's HTTPS-only principle.

#### Breaking changes for clients and operators

Responses, headers and status codes:

- [P-03](#p-03-a-common-suffix-pool-called-without-x-oagw-target-host-round-robins-instead-of-returning-400): a derived common-suffix alias with several endpoints and no `X-OAGW-Target-Host` gets 400 instead of round-robin. Needs a release note.
- [R-17](#r-17-rate-limit-headers-do-not-follow-the-ietf-draft-the-docs-cite): `x-ratelimit-*` headers are replaced by the IETF `RateLimit` / `RateLimit-Policy` headers.
- [M-12](#m-12-a-disabled-upstream-silently-falls-through-to-an-ancestors-upstream-instead-of-returning-503): a disabled upstream returns 503 instead of silently falling through to an ancestor's upstream.
- [M-07](#m-07-list-endpoints-ignore-filter-and-other-unknown-query-parameters-and-return-the-unfiltered-list-with-200): `$filter`, `$orderby` and unknown list parameters get 400 instead of 200 with the unfiltered list.
- [M-01](#m-01-any-authenticated-caller-can-create-change-and-delete-its-tenants-upstreams-and-routes): subjects without the upstream/route permissions start getting 403 on management calls.
- [P-01](#p-01-a-route-prefix-matches-mid-segment-so-v1chatty-reaches-the-upstream-as-v1chatty): a path that only matched mid-segment (`/v1/chatty` under route `/v1/chat`) gets 404.
- [P-02](#p-02-percent-encoded--in-the-proxy-path-reaches-the-upstream-unchanged): paths with encoded dot-segments or encoded `/` get 400.
- [P-06](#p-06-an-oversized-body-gets-400-out_of_range-from-oagw-or-a-plain-text-413-from-the-api-gateway), [P-08](#p-08-unreachable-tls-failed-and-http-only-violating-upstreams-get-different-statuses-than-documented): oversized bodies get 413 instead of 400; network, TLS and protocol failures get 502 instead of 503 (PR [#4911](https://github.com/constructorfabric/gears-rust/pull/4911)).
- [U-3](#u-3-a-response-phase-guard-that-rejects-with-5xx-makes-the-client-get-503-instead-of-the-documented-502): a 5xx response-phase guard rejection gets 502 instead of 503.
- [PLG-03](#plg-03-an-unknown-plugin-id-gives-401-auth-500-guard-or-is-silently-skipped-transform-not-the-documented-503), [PLG-10](#plg-10-a-missing-secret-gives-500-while-an-unreachable-credential-store-or-idp-gives-401-and-the-status-differs-by-plugin): unknown plugins and credential failures get new status codes (for example, an unknown transform no longer returns 200).
- [STR-06](#str-06-every-rejected-websocket-upgrade-returns-the-same-gateway-error-the-upstreams-own-401403404-is-lost): a refused WebSocket upgrade returns the upstream's own status and body instead of a gateway 503.
- [P-07](#p-07-error-type-ids-field-names-and-resource-identifiers-dont-match-what-oagw-sends): error details name the field `x-oagw-target-host` instead of `x-target-host`, and not-found errors carry the alias.
- [A-06](#a-06-docs-put-every-oagw-path-under-apioagwv1-but-the-gear-serves-oagwv1-and-error-instance-values-are-built-inconsistently): the problem `instance` no longer includes the query string.
- [P-04](#p-04-the-rest-handler-buffers-the-whole-request-body-before-checking-proxyinvoke), [P-10](#p-10-rate-limiting-runs-last-after-credential-lookups-token-fetches-and-guards): a caller without `proxy:invoke` gets 403 before any body check; over-limit calls get 429 before the auth plugin and guards run.

Configurations accepted today that will be rejected at write time (400):

- [PLG-13](#plg-13-route-level-cors-is-ignored-unless-sharing-inherit-and-a-descendants-own-private-or-enforce-cors-is-dropped): any `cors` block on an upstream or route. OAGW also stops answering CORS preflights.
- [STR-09](#str-09-grpc-and-webtransport-configs-are-accepted-but-grpc-traffic-never-works-and-webtransport-is-proxied-as-plain-https): gRPC match rules and `grpc` / `wt` endpoint schemes.
- [R-10](#r-10-strategy-queue-and-degrade-are-accepted-but-behave-as-reject-the-queue-block-is-dropped), [R-11](#r-11-circuit-breaker-concurrency-limit-and-backpressure-fields-are-silently-accepted-and-ignored), [R-12](#r-12-rate-limit-values-are-never-validated-cost-0-turns-limiting-off): `strategy: queue|degrade`, `circuit_breaker` / `concurrency_limit`, and zero or impossible rate-limit values.
- [M-04](#m-04-the-upstream-protocol-field-accepts-any-string-and-is-never-used), [M-09](#m-09-documented-validation-rules-are-ticked-x-but-missing-or-different-in-the-code), [M-10](#m-10-credential-references-are-resolved-at-write-time-for-secret_ref-only-and-the-docs-describe-a-different-check), [M-11](#m-11-unknown-or-wrong-type-plugin-ids-are-accepted-on-write-and-only-fail-or-are-silently-skipped-at-proxy-time): unknown `protocol`, invalid paths, methods, ports, tags or aliases, inaccessible OAuth2 refs, and unknown, catalog-only or wrong-kind plugin IDs.
- [P-08](#p-08-unreachable-tls-failed-and-http-only-violating-upstreams-get-different-statuses-than-documented): `http` endpoints when `allow_http_upstream` is false.

Routing, merge and rate-limit behaviour:

- [PLG-02](#plg-02-route-plugins-with-the-default-sharing-private-replace-the-upstreams-plugins-instead-of-running-after-them): a route's plugins run after the upstream's instead of replacing them.
- [R-04](#r-04-an-ancestors-private-auth-rate-limit-and-plugins-reach-descendants-that-omit-the-field), [R-05](#r-05-descendants-and-routes-can-bypass-an-ancestors-enforced-plugins-and-rate-limit): descendants stop inheriting `private` fields and can no longer drop enforced plugins or limits.
- [R-06](#r-06-requests-with-a-different-merged-limit-reset-the-shared-bucket-so-callers-bypass-limits), [R-08](#r-08-scoperoute-on-an-upstream-level-limit-acts-as-one-global-bucket), [R-09](#r-09-a-shared-budget-with-scopetenant-is-not-shared-and-budgettotal-is-ignored-at-runtime), [R-13](#r-13-the-min-merge-copies-the-whole-config-of-the-lower-rate-side), [R-14](#r-14-a-route-cannot-set-only-a-cost-that-draws-on-the-upstream-budget): bucket keys, pool sharing and merged limits change, so callers see 429 at different points.
- [R-07](#r-07-scopeip-trusts-client-supplied-x-forwarded-for-and-buckets-are-never-evicted): IP-scoped limits key on the real client. Deployments behind a proxy must configure its CIDR.
- [P-11](#p-11-with-the-default-passthrough-the-upstream-never-sees-the-clients-x-request-id-accept-or-trace-headers), [P-18](#p-18-the-query-string-is-re-encoded-headoptions-cant-be-routed-and-the-host-port-rule-ignores-the-scheme), [P-19](#p-19-x-forwarded--and-x-real-ip-reach-the-upstream-under-passthrough-all): upstreams receive `Accept`, `X-Request-ID` and trace headers by default, and the raw query string; `X-Forwarded-*` is stripped.

SDK and operators:

- [U-5](#u-5-sdk-callers-get-gateway-failures-in-two-different-ways-as-err-or-as-an-ok-503504-response): SDK callers get gateway failures as `Err` instead of `Ok` with a 503/504 response.
- [STR-13](#str-13-the-sdk-sse-parser-does-not-follow-the-w3c-rules-it-claims-to-follow): the SDK SSE parser stops yielding truncated final events and `id`/`retry`-only blocks.
- [CFG-VAL](#cfg-val-oagw-starts-with-a-zero-proxy-timeout-body-limit-or-token-cache-size-instead-of-refusing-the-config): OAGW refuses to start when `proxy_timeout_secs`, `max_body_size_bytes` or `token_cache_capacity` is 0.
- [SCH-01](#sch-01-types-registry-holds-empty-placeholder-schemas-for-the-oagw-types-so-it-checks-nothing-about-upstreams-routes-or-plugins): provisioned upstream and route instances that don't match the real schemas fail validation.
- [O-03](#o-03-gateway-rejected-requests-are-missing-from-requests_total-and-the-in-flight-and-rate-limit-gauges-are-skewed), [STR-08](#str-08-stream-aborts-are-never-counted-as-streamaborted-and-streaming-metrics-stop-when-the-headers-arrive): metrics count gateway errors and cover whole streams.

## Glossary

| Term | Meaning |
|---|---|
| Upstream | An external service that OAGW proxies to: endpoints, auth, rate limit, plugins, headers. Clients call it as `/oagw/v1/proxy/{alias}/…`. |
| Route | A match rule under an upstream (method + path prefix). A proxy request must match an enabled route. |
| Alias | The upstream's name in the proxy URL. Derived from the hostname (plus `:port` for a non-default port); an explicit alias is allowed only for IP endpoints. |
| Ancestor / descendant | Tenants form a hierarchy. A descendant tenant can use, or shadow, an upstream that an ancestor defines under the same alias. |
| CP / DP | Control Plane: management of upstreams and routes (storage, validation, alias resolution, hierarchy merge), in `src/domain/services/management/`. Data Plane: the proxy itself (auth, guards, transforms, rate limiting, the Pingora-based HTTP client). The DP calls the CP to resolve a request. |
| GTS | Global Type System, the platform's type-registry ID scheme, e.g. `gts.cf.core.oagw.upstream.v1~<uuid>`. OAGW registers its types and its plugin IDs with types-registry, and resources are addressed by GTS IDs on the wire. |
| Sharing modes | Per-field setting (`auth`, `rate_limit`, `plugins`, `cors`) that controls what descendants see. From [`PRD.md:328-332`](https://github.com/constructorfabric/gears-rust/blob/66da80e5715e10c08c6fafd5e9832137389a3355/gears/system/oagw/docs/PRD.md#L328-L332): [`private`](https://github.com/constructorfabric/gears-rust/blob/66da80e5715e10c08c6fafd5e9832137389a3355/gears/system/oagw/docs/PRD.md#L330) “Not visible to descendants (default)”; [`inherit`](https://github.com/constructorfabric/gears-rust/blob/66da80e5715e10c08c6fafd5e9832137389a3355/gears/system/oagw/docs/PRD.md#L331) “Visible; descendant can override if specified”; [`enforce`](https://github.com/constructorfabric/gears-rust/blob/66da80e5715e10c08c6fafd5e9832137389a3355/gears/system/oagw/docs/PRD.md#L332) “Visible; descendant cannot override”. |
| `X-OAGW-Error-Source` | Response header that says who produced an error: `gateway` (OAGW itself) or `upstream` (passed through from the upstream). Defined in [`ADR-0013:59-60`](https://github.com/constructorfabric/gears-rust/blob/66da80e5715e10c08c6fafd5e9832137389a3355/gears/system/oagw/docs/ADR/0013-error-source-distinction.md#L59-L60). |
| Catalog-only plugin | A plugin whose GTS ID is registered but which has no runtime implementation: `basic`, `bearer`, `timeout`, `cors`, `logging`, `metrics` ([`DESIGN.md:396`](https://github.com/constructorfabric/gears-rust/blob/66da80e5715e10c08c6fafd5e9832137389a3355/gears/system/oagw/docs/DESIGN.md#L396): “**Catalog-only identifiers** (reserved GTS ids, not resolvable via a plugin registry”). |
| Feature docs `F0001`–`F0008` | The eight delivery-tracking docs in `gears/system/oagw/docs/features/`: 0001 domain foundation, 0002 management API, 0003 plugin system, 0004 proxy engine, 0005 tenant hierarchy, 0006 rate limiting, 0007 streaming, 0008 observability. Their checkboxes are delivery status. |
| INDEX | The scenario catalogue, [`scenarios/INDEX.md`](https://github.com/constructorfabric/gears-rust/blob/66da80e5715e10c08c6fafd5e9832137389a3355/gears/system/oagw/scenarios/INDEX.md). |
| Future-facing marker | A note on a scenario or doc step saying the behaviour is planned, not built. |
| *Likely* | Strong evidence, but not traced end to end. Everything else was read in the code. |
| P0 / P1 / P2 | Priority of a `FIX-CODE` item. **P0**: security or data correctness. **P1**: a contract a client will hit. **P2**: the rest. Doc-only items have no priority. |

### Outcome codes

| Code | Meaning |
|---|---|
| `FIX-CODE` | Change the code, and add a test. |
| `FIX-DOC` | Change the docs, scenarios or tests to match the code. |
| `ADR` | Write a new ADR or amend one, then apply the resulting `FIX-*`. |
| `PRD` | Change the requirement. Needs product sign-off. |
| `FUTURE` | Mark as planned (future-facing), untick any checkbox, don't build now. |
| `WONTFIX` | Accept the divergence, with a rationale. |
| `DONE` | Already resolved. |

Combined outcomes (for example `FUTURE` + `FIX-CODE`) mean both apply: typically "the feature is planned; until then, reject its config at write time".

## Why the docs drifted

Most items trace back to one of these causes. Items link here instead of repeating the story.

| Cause | What happened | Evidence |
|---|---|---|
| **Bulk checkbox sync** | One docs commit ticked many feature-doc steps `[x]` that were never built: DB and migration steps, OData, CRUD permission checks, protocol validation, gRPC matching, plugin-table lookups and the "503 PluginNotFound" path. Some rate-limit steps were ticked the same way when rate limiting landed. | [`2aed1705e`](https://github.com/constructorfabric/gears-rust/commit/2aed1705ef781a87695c9e29f9b09838ccf419f5) "docs(oagw): sync feature TODOs with implementation" (2026-03-25); [`a9b28ca4a`](https://github.com/constructorfabric/gears-rust/commit/a9b28ca4ad491a4f27d458d9f2c8addb04c54846) (2026-04-08). |
| **Design-era docs** | The design docs and scenarios were written before the code and never reconciled. They describe DB storage, Starlark, plugin CRUD, gRPC, caching, a circuit breaker, an audit log and more. | Design proposal commits [`14ef3e26e`](https://github.com/constructorfabric/gears-rust/commit/14ef3e26e3d0660cccbe8f7e8463d5fa3254c0e3), [`2078ff1da`](https://github.com/constructorfabric/gears-rust/commit/2078ff1da266a327572515c50cd9a51e8dbf4744), [`141f08c64`](https://github.com/constructorfabric/gears-rust/commit/141f08c649b048bdf4b4c9e7add110efb09054c6) (2026-01-22); first implementation [`a989f9ba7`](https://github.com/constructorfabric/gears-rust/commit/a989f9ba7d464064a7aeb73e490ee61669f5682e) (2026-02-11). |
| **Partial doc syncs** | Later syncs fixed some docs but not all. The built-in plugin lists were fixed in ADR-0003, DESIGN and PRD, but not in F0003, DECOMPOSITION, the plugin-execution flow, INDEX or the scenarios. The CP/DP roles were fixed in the ADRs but not in the scenario flows. | [`e8158be6b`](https://github.com/constructorfabric/gears-rust/commit/e8158be6bbdef9bbcc5605b25bfb4f5181ddd0fc) (2026-08-19); [`107095d44`](https://github.com/constructorfabric/gears-rust/commit/107095d44a22f4fc2de6040a9bd5b5e8f7005ede) (2026-08-10). |
| **Canonical-errors migration** | The error types moved to the platform's canonical `cf.core.err.*` types. No doc was changed, so every error table still shows the old `cf.oagw.*` types. | [`10d7e329c`](https://github.com/constructorfabric/gears-rust/commit/10d7e329c40e69c5421c1bf0dbdfdc250f49626e) "migrate oagw to use canonical errors" (2026-05-01). |
| **No traceability checks** | Nothing validates the OAGW docs against its code. The validator config excludes OAGW's code, tests and feature docs. | [`artifacts.toml:61-67`](https://github.com/constructorfabric/gears-rust/blob/66da80e5715e10c08c6fafd5e9832137389a3355/.cf-studio/config/artifacts.toml#L61-L67): “Ignore oagw codebase and features — code traceability markers reverted, pending re-add.” |
| **e2e config sidesteps constraints** | The e2e config allows plaintext upstreams, turns SSRF protection off and sets a 2 s proxy timeout, so HTTPS-only and SSRF are never exercised end to end. | [`e2e-local.yaml:527-532`](https://github.com/constructorfabric/gears-rust/blob/66da80e5715e10c08c6fafd5e9832137389a3355/config/e2e-local.yaml#L527-L532) (snippet below). |

[`e2e-local.yaml:529-532`](https://github.com/constructorfabric/gears-rust/blob/66da80e5715e10c08c6fafd5e9832137389a3355/config/e2e-local.yaml#L529-L532)

```yaml
      proxy_timeout_secs: 2
      allow_http_upstream: true # Only for testing - do not enable in production without proper security controls
      ssrf_policy:
        enabled: false # Disabled for e2e tests (mock upstreams use 127.0.0.1)
```

## How decisions were made

When the code and a document disagree, the default answer depends on the kind of document:

| Document | Role | Default when it disagrees with the code |
|---|---|---|
| PRD | Requirement (what and why) | The code is wrong, unless the requirement was deliberately dropped. Then change the PRD explicitly, with product sign-off. |
| ADR, `accepted` | Recorded decision | The code is wrong, unless a newer decision exists. Then write a superseding ADR or add a status note; don't rewrite history. |
| ADR, `proposed` | Undecided draft | The ADR is stale: mark it superseded or rejected, or accept a revised version. |
| DESIGN | How it is built; its principles are normative | Descriptive parts: the doc is stale. Principles (tenant scope, credential isolation, HTTPS-only): the code is wrong. |
| DECOMPOSITION, feature docs | Delivery tracking; checkboxes are status | The doc is wrong. Mechanical fix. |
| Scenarios | Client-visible acceptance behaviour | Settle the behaviour first, then fix whichever side is wrong. Unbuilt features get a future-facing marker. |
| Schemas, OpenAPI, README, config docs | Reference contract | The doc is wrong (it must match the wire), unless the wire value itself is the bug. |
| Tests | Evidence of what the code does | Not an authority. Strengthen them once the behaviour is decided. |

The procedure for each item:

1. **Classify it:** the doc over-claims something unbuilt; the doc is stale after an intentional change; the code has a bug against a clear spec; two specs contradict each other; or only a test is missing.
2. **Find the highest authority** that covers the behaviour: security principle > PRD > accepted ADR > DESIGN principle > feature doc > scenario > code comment.
3. **Check the evidence of intent.** A commit that says the change was deliberate **and** a test that asserts it means the behaviour is intentional. If the only evidence is the bulk checkbox sync, or nothing tests it, treat it as a bug or as unbuilt.
4. **Apply the overrides.** Security: fix the code whatever the docs say. Client-visible contract changes: weigh the breakage and flag breaking changes. Unbuilt features: mark them planned; don't build them.

## 1. Upstream error responses: are they preserved?

**Short answer:** yes. Upstream status, headers and body are preserved on the normal path, with the exceptions below. The first exception ([U-2](#u-2-a-large-request-whose-upstream-answers-early-eg-413-before-reading-the-body-gets-a-gateway-503-instead-of-the-upstreams-response)) is a real bug.

The probe results quoted in [U-1](#other-doc-only-fixes-proxy) and [U-2](#u-2-a-large-request-whose-upstream-answers-early-eg-413-before-reading-the-body-gets-a-gateway-503-instead-of-the-upstreams-response) came from a throwaway probe test that is not in the repo, so reviewers can't re-run it. The linked code is the evidence you can check.

Mechanical doc fixes in this area: [U-1](#other-doc-only-fixes-proxy) (see [Mechanical doc fixes](#mechanical-doc-fixes)).

### U-2: A large request whose upstream answers early (e.g. 413 before reading the body) gets a gateway 503 instead of the upstream's response

- **Decision:** `FIX-CODE`, P1. If writing the request into the proxy bridge fails, read and relay any upstream response that has already arrived.
- **What the docs say:**
  - [`F0004:151`](https://github.com/constructorfabric/gears-rust/blob/66da80e5715e10c08c6fafd5e9832137389a3355/gears/system/oagw/docs/features/0004-cpt-cf-oagw-feature-proxy-engine.md#L151)
    > 2. [x] - `p1` - **RETURN** upstream response as-is with `X-OAGW-Error-Source: upstream` - `inst-proxy-31b`
  - [`ADR-0013:60`](https://github.com/constructorfabric/gears-rust/blob/66da80e5715e10c08c6fafd5e9832137389a3355/gears/system/oagw/docs/ADR/0013-error-source-distinction.md#L60) (status: accepted)
    > `X-OAGW-Error-Source: upstream` — Error returned by the upstream service, passed through unchanged
  - Rejecting on `Content-Length` before reading the body is a common upstream pattern (nginx, ALB). The docs make no exception for it.
- **What the code does:**
  - [`handlers/proxy.rs:164-175`](https://github.com/constructorfabric/gears-rust/blob/66da80e5715e10c08c6fafd5e9832137389a3355/gears/system/oagw/oagw/src/api/rest/handlers/proxy.rs#L164-L175): the REST handler reads the whole request body into memory, so every REST call takes the buffered bridge path.
  - [`service.rs:1016`](https://github.com/constructorfabric/gears-rust/blob/66da80e5715e10c08c6fafd5e9832137389a3355/gears/system/oagw/oagw/src/infra/proxy/service.rs#L1016): the bridge to Pingora is a 64 KiB in-memory pipe.

    ```rust
    let (client_io, server_io) = tokio::io::duplex(65_536);
    ```
  - [`service.rs:1161-1168`](https://github.com/constructorfabric/gears-rust/blob/66da80e5715e10c08c6fafd5e9832137389a3355/gears/system/oagw/oagw/src/infra/proxy/service.rs#L1161-L1168): the buffered path writes the entire request first and starts reading the response only afterwards. When the upstream answers early and closes, Pingora relays the response and ends the session, the write fails, and the response already waiting in the read half is dropped.

    ```rust
    let mut client_io = client_io;
    client_io
        .write_all(&wire)
        .await
        .map_err(|e| DomainError::DownstreamError {
            detail: format!("failed to write to proxy bridge: {e}"),
    ```

    The response is read only after that, at [`service.rs:1173-1174`](https://github.com/constructorfabric/gears-rust/blob/66da80e5715e10c08c6fafd5e9832137389a3355/gears/system/oagw/oagw/src/infra/proxy/service.rs#L1173-L1174).
  - [`service.rs:1117-1131`](https://github.com/constructorfabric/gears-rust/blob/66da80e5715e10c08c6fafd5e9832137389a3355/gears/system/oagw/oagw/src/infra/proxy/service.rs#L1117-L1131): the SDK streaming path has the same effect. A body-write failure is reported on `abort_rx`, and the `biased` select checks that branch before the response.

    ```rust
    tokio::select! {
        biased;
        // …
        Ok(reason) = abort_rx => {
            Err(DomainError::DownstreamError {
                detail: format!("streaming request body failed mid-stream: {reason}"),
    ```
  - [`error.rs:243-247`](https://github.com/constructorfabric/gears-rust/blob/66da80e5715e10c08c6fafd5e9832137389a3355/gears/system/oagw/oagw/src/api/rest/error.rs#L243-L247): `DownstreamError` becomes a 503 `service_unavailable`.
  - Probe results (upstream reads the headers only, sends 413, closes):

    | Request body | Result |
    |---|---|
    | 1 KiB, 60 KiB, 200 KiB | `413` from upstream, preserved |
    | 1 MiB, 8 MiB (buffered/REST path) | `Err(ServiceUnavailable)`. Over REST this is 503 with `X-OAGW-Error-Source: gateway`, or 502 after PR [#4911](https://github.com/constructorfabric/gears-rust/pull/4911). |
    | 1 MiB (SDK streaming path) | same `Err(ServiceUnavailable)` |

    The threshold sits between 200 KiB and 1 MiB here. It depends on kernel socket buffers plus the 64 KiB pipe.
  - Related, not verified: [`handlers/proxy.rs:152-159`](https://github.com/constructorfabric/gears-rust/blob/66da80e5715e10c08c6fafd5e9832137389a3355/gears/system/oagw/oagw/src/api/rest/handlers/proxy.rs#L152-L159) rejects a request whose `Content-Length` exceeds `max_body_size` before reading the body. Whether a client that is still sending then sees a connection reset instead of that error response has not been checked.
- **Evidence of intent:** none. No test sends an early upstream response. The e2e mock's `/error/{code}` and `/status/{code}` are GET-only ([`mock_upstream.py:295-306`](https://github.com/constructorfabric/gears-rust/blob/66da80e5715e10c08c6fafd5e9832137389a3355/testing/e2e/suites/oagw/mock_upstream.py#L295-L306)), and the mock reads the full request body before it answers ([`mock_upstream.py:46-52`](https://github.com/constructorfabric/gears-rust/blob/66da80e5715e10c08c6fafd5e9832137389a3355/testing/e2e/suites/oagw/mock_upstream.py#L46-L52)).
- **Why this decision:** ADR-0013 (accepted) says upstream responses pass through unchanged. The bridge breaks that by treating a request-write failure as fatal instead of reading a response that may already be there.
- **Impact:** clients that upload large bodies to upstreams that answer early will get the upstream's status (e.g. 413) instead of a gateway 503. Not breaking. Follow-ups:
  - Buffered path at [`service.rs:1166`](https://github.com/constructorfabric/gears-rust/blob/66da80e5715e10c08c6fafd5e9832137389a3355/gears/system/oagw/oagw/src/infra/proxy/service.rs#L1166) and SDK streaming path (the header write at [`service.rs:1039`](https://github.com/constructorfabric/gears-rust/blob/66da80e5715e10c08c6fafd5e9832137389a3355/gears/system/oagw/oagw/src/infra/proxy/service.rs#L1037-L1042) and the body-write failure at [`service.rs:1127-1131`](https://github.com/constructorfabric/gears-rust/blob/66da80e5715e10c08c6fafd5e9832137389a3355/gears/system/oagw/oagw/src/infra/proxy/service.rs#L1127-L1131)): don't turn a request-write error into `DownstreamError` while a response is available.
  - Add a test with an upstream that sends 413 after the headers and closes, with 1 MiB and 8 MiB bodies, on both paths.
  - Extend the e2e mock with a POST early-response endpoint.
- **Decided together with:** [P-06](#p-06-an-oversized-body-gets-400-out_of_range-from-oagw-or-a-plain-text-413-from-the-api-gateway) (its note on the `Content-Length` precheck points here).

### U-3: A response-phase guard that rejects with 5xx makes the client get 503 instead of the documented 502

- **Decision:** `FIX-CODE`, P2. A response-phase guard rejection with a 5xx status returns 502 `bad_gateway`, per ADR-0017, instead of being masked as 503.
- **What the docs say:**
  - [`ADR-0017:91-92`](https://github.com/constructorfabric/gears-rust/blob/66da80e5715e10c08c6fafd5e9832137389a3355/gears/system/oagw/docs/ADR/0017-required-headers-guard-plugin.md#L91-L92) (status: accepted)
    > Request phase → status 400, error_code REQUIRED_HEADER_MISSING … Response phase → status 502, error_code REQUIRED_HEADER_MISSING
- **What the code does:**
  - [`service.rs:227-237`](https://github.com/constructorfabric/gears-rust/blob/66da80e5715e10c08c6fafd5e9832137389a3355/gears/system/oagw/oagw/src/infra/proxy/service.rs#L227-L237): response-phase guards run before the response is returned; a rejection turns the upstream response into an error.

    ```rust
    execute_guard_responses(
        &self.guard_registry,
        // …
    )
    .await?;
    ```
  - [`error.rs:449-458`](https://github.com/constructorfabric/gears-rust/blob/66da80e5715e10c08c6fafd5e9832137389a3355/gears/system/oagw/oagw/src/api/rest/error.rs#L449-L458): `guard_rejected_to_canonical` maps every 5xx guard status to 503 `service_unavailable`.

    ```rust
    500..=599 => {
        // …
            "OAGW guard plugin returned 5xx — masking as service_unavailable",
        );
        CanonicalError::service_unavailable()
            .with_retry_after_seconds(RETRY_AFTER_GUARD_SECS)
    ```
  - [`domain/error.rs:103-107`](https://github.com/constructorfabric/gears-rust/blob/66da80e5715e10c08c6fafd5e9832137389a3355/gears/system/oagw/oagw/src/domain/error.rs#L103-L107): the `GuardRejected` error carries a status, code, detail and instance but no phase, so the same mapping serves request-phase and response-phase guards.
- **Evidence of intent:** the comment at [`error.rs:443-448`](https://github.com/constructorfabric/gears-rust/blob/66da80e5715e10c08c6fafd5e9832137389a3355/gears/system/oagw/oagw/src/api/rest/error.rs#L443-L448) explains the masking: the canonical `service_unavailable` schema cannot carry the plugin's status or code. [`guard_rejected_5xx_passes_through`](https://github.com/constructorfabric/gears-rust/blob/66da80e5715e10c08c6fafd5e9832137389a3355/gears/system/oagw/oagw/src/api/rest/error.rs#L997-L1007) asserts a guard 503 comes back as 503 (no phase involved). No test covers a response-phase rejection end to end; the e2e guard tests never configure `required_response_headers`.
- **Why this decision:** ADR-0017 is an accepted ADR and states 502 for the response phase. A code comment has less weight.
- **Impact:** clients get 502 `bad_gateway` instead of 503 `service_unavailable` when a response-phase guard rejects with 5xx. Breaking for clients that key on this 503 (its `Retry-After` hint comes from the `service_unavailable` mapping). Follow-ups: change `guard_rejected_to_canonical` ([`error.rs:449-461`](https://github.com/constructorfabric/gears-rust/blob/66da80e5715e10c08c6fafd5e9832137389a3355/gears/system/oagw/oagw/src/api/rest/error.rs#L449-L461)); align with the 502 mapping in PR [#4911](https://github.com/constructorfabric/gears-rust/pull/4911); add a response-phase rejection test (none today).

### U-4: `on_error` transforms never run for upstream error responses, and metrics count those responses as successes

- **Decision:** `FIX-DOC` (+ `FIX-CODE` via O-03), P2 (metrics). `on_error` covers gateway-originated errors only; upstream error responses go through `on_response`. Error metrics are fixed under [O-03](#o-03-gateway-rejected-requests-are-missing-from-requests_total-and-the-in-flight-and-rate-limit-gauges-are-skewed).
- **What the docs say:**
  - [`F0004:149-150`](https://github.com/constructorfabric/gears-rust/blob/66da80e5715e10c08c6fafd5e9832137389a3355/gears/system/oagw/docs/features/0004-cpt-cf-oagw-feature-proxy-engine.md#L149-L150)
    > 31. [x] - `p1` - **IF** upstream returns error response - `inst-proxy-31`
    > 1. [x] - `p1` - Execute transform plugins: `on_error` phase - `inst-proxy-31a`
  - [`flows/plugin-execution.md:336-378`](https://github.com/constructorfabric/gears-rust/blob/66da80e5715e10c08c6fafd5e9832137389a3355/gears/system/oagw/scenarios/flows/plugin-execution.md#L336-L378)
    > Transform plugins operate on error responses too: … Upstream returns 500 error … Transform plugins (post-error) execute
- **What the code does:**
  - [`service.rs:864-877`](https://github.com/constructorfabric/gears-rust/blob/66da80e5715e10c08c6fafd5e9832137389a3355/gears/system/oagw/oagw/src/infra/proxy/service.rs#L864-L877): `on_error` transforms run only when the bridge returns an error.

    ```rust
    // 10b. Execute transform error plugins on upstream failures.
    match upstream_result {
        Ok(resp) => Ok(resp),
        Err(err) => {
            execute_transform_errors(
    ```
  - [`service.rs:239-241`](https://github.com/constructorfabric/gears-rust/blob/66da80e5715e10c08c6fafd5e9832137389a3355/gears/system/oagw/oagw/src/infra/proxy/service.rs#L239-L241): every response the bridge returns, including upstream 4xx/5xx and the connect/timeout failures that Pingora writes itself, goes through the `on_response` pipeline.
  - [`service.rs:881-884`](https://github.com/constructorfabric/gears-rust/blob/66da80e5715e10c08c6fafd5e9832137389a3355/gears/system/oagw/oagw/src/infra/proxy/service.rs#L881-L884): any `Ok` response is recorded as a successful request with its status; only `Err` counts as an error.

    ```rust
    match &result {
        Ok(resp) => guard.set_success(resp.status().as_u16()),
        Err(err) => guard.set_error(domain_error_type_name(err)),
    ```

    The metrics guard that records them is at [`service.rs:506-513`](https://github.com/constructorfabric/gears-rust/blob/66da80e5715e10c08c6fafd5e9832137389a3355/gears/system/oagw/oagw/src/infra/proxy/service.rs#L506-L513).
- **Evidence of intent:** none.
- **Why this decision:** the feature doc and the flow scenario are the only sources for `on_error` on upstream errors; no ADR or PRD defines it. Upstream error responses are real responses, so they stay on the `on_response` path, and the docs change.
- **Impact:** plugin authors and operators; no client-visible change. Not breaking. Follow-ups: FIX-DOC [`F0004:150`](https://github.com/constructorfabric/gears-rust/blob/66da80e5715e10c08c6fafd5e9832137389a3355/gears/system/oagw/docs/features/0004-cpt-cf-oagw-feature-proxy-engine.md#L150) (inst-proxy-31a) and [`flows/plugin-execution.md:336-378`](https://github.com/constructorfabric/gears-rust/blob/66da80e5715e10c08c6fafd5e9832137389a3355/gears/system/oagw/scenarios/flows/plugin-execution.md#L336-L378). Pingora-written connect/timeout failures are gateway errors; [U-5](#u-5-sdk-callers-get-gateway-failures-in-two-different-ways-as-err-or-as-an-ok-503504-response) routes them to `Err`, which then triggers `on_error`. Metric semantics change under [O-03](#o-03-gateway-rejected-requests-are-missing-from-requests_total-and-the-in-flight-and-rate-limit-gauges-are-skewed).
- **Decided together with:** [O-03](#o-03-gateway-rejected-requests-are-missing-from-requests_total-and-the-in-flight-and-rate-limit-gauges-are-skewed), [U-5](#u-5-sdk-callers-get-gateway-failures-in-two-different-ways-as-err-or-as-an-ok-503504-response).

### U-5: SDK callers get gateway failures in two different ways: as `Err`, or as an `Ok` 503/504 response

- **Decision:** `FIX-CODE`, P2. SDK callers get gateway failures only as `Err(CanonicalError)`; `Ok` means an upstream response.
- **What the docs say:**
  - [`sdk/api.rs:42-44`](https://github.com/constructorfabric/gears-rust/blob/66da80e5715e10c08c6fafd5e9832137389a3355/gears/system/oagw/oagw-sdk/src/api.rs#L42-L44)
    > All fallible methods return `Result<_, CanonicalError>` — the platform's … canonical error type
  - [`sdk/api.rs:141`](https://github.com/constructorfabric/gears-rust/blob/66da80e5715e10c08c6fafd5e9832137389a3355/gears/system/oagw/oagw-sdk/src/api.rs#L141)
    > `ErrorSource` is available on the response via `resp.extensions().get::<ErrorSource>()`.
- **What the code does:**
  - [`pingora_proxy.rs:867-870`](https://github.com/constructorfabric/gears-rust/blob/66da80e5715e10c08c6fafd5e9832137389a3355/gears/system/oagw/oagw/src/infra/proxy/pingora_proxy.rs#L867-L870): `fail_to_proxy` writes connect, TLS, timeout and HTTP/2 failures as a problem+json response. It comes back through the bridge like an upstream response, so SDK callers get `Ok` with 503/504 and `ErrorSource::Gateway`.

    ```rust
    if let Ok(mut resp) = ResponseHeader::build(status, Some(body_bytes.len())) {
        let _ = resp.insert_header("content-type", "application/problem+json");
        let _ = resp.insert_header("x-oagw-error-source", "gateway");
    ```

    The whole function is [`pingora_proxy.rs:777-880`](https://github.com/constructorfabric/gears-rust/blob/66da80e5715e10c08c6fafd5e9832137389a3355/gears/system/oagw/oagw/src/infra/proxy/pingora_proxy.rs#L777-L880).
  - [`proxy_integration.rs:333-340`](https://github.com/constructorfabric/gears-rust/blob/66da80e5715e10c08c6fafd5e9832137389a3355/gears/system/oagw/oagw/tests/proxy_integration.rs#L333-L340): pipeline errors (auth, rate limit, route) are `Err(CanonicalError)`, e.g. an unknown alias.

    ```rust
    Err(err) => assert!(matches!(err, CanonicalError::NotFound { .. })),
    ```
  - [`handlers/proxy.rs:260-264`](https://github.com/constructorfabric/gears-rust/blob/66da80e5715e10c08c6fafd5e9832137389a3355/gears/system/oagw/oagw/src/api/rest/handlers/proxy.rs#L260-L264): the REST handler treats a missing `ErrorSource` as `Gateway` …

    ```rust
    .get::<ErrorSource>()
    .copied()
    .unwrap_or(ErrorSource::Gateway);
    ```
  - [`headers.rs:101-108`](https://github.com/constructorfabric/gears-rust/blob/66da80e5715e10c08c6fafd5e9832137389a3355/gears/system/oagw/oagw/src/infra/proxy/headers.rs#L101-L108): … while the data plane treats a missing header as `Upstream`.

    ```rust
    Some("gateway") => ErrorSource::Gateway,
    _ => ErrorSource::Upstream,
    ```
- **Evidence of intent:** tests accept both channels. [`proxy_all_backends_unreachable`](https://github.com/constructorfabric/gears-rust/blob/66da80e5715e10c08c6fafd5e9832137389a3355/gears/system/oagw/oagw/tests/proxy_integration.rs#L1507-L1520) passes on either `Err(ServiceUnavailable)` or `Ok` with 503/504; [`proxy_unreachable_backend_returns_rfc9457_problem_body`](https://github.com/constructorfabric/gears-rust/blob/66da80e5715e10c08c6fafd5e9832137389a3355/gears/system/oagw/oagw/tests/proxy_integration.rs#L2143) does the same.

  [`proxy_integration.rs:1507-1520`](https://github.com/constructorfabric/gears-rust/blob/66da80e5715e10c08c6fafd5e9832137389a3355/gears/system/oagw/oagw/tests/proxy_integration.rs#L1507-L1520)

  ```rust
  Ok(resp) => {
      // Pingora may return a 503 response directly via fail_to_proxy.
  ```
- **Why this decision:** the SDK trait doc is the SDK contract: fallible calls report failures as `Err(CanonicalError)`. One channel per failure class keeps `ErrorSource` meaningful.
- **Impact:** Breaking for SDK callers that read `Ok` 503/504 responses with `ErrorSource::Gateway`: they get `Err` instead. REST clients keep getting a problem+json response with `X-OAGW-Error-Source: gateway`. Follow-ups: the SDK facade converts `fail_to_proxy` problem responses (`x-oagw-error-source: gateway`, [`pingora_proxy.rs:777-880`](https://github.com/constructorfabric/gears-rust/blob/66da80e5715e10c08c6fafd5e9832137389a3355/gears/system/oagw/oagw/src/infra/proxy/pingora_proxy.rs#L777-L880)) into `Err`; align the default for a missing `ErrorSource` (REST → Gateway [`handlers/proxy.rs:264`](https://github.com/constructorfabric/gears-rust/blob/66da80e5715e10c08c6fafd5e9832137389a3355/gears/system/oagw/oagw/src/api/rest/handlers/proxy.rs#L264) vs data plane → Upstream [`headers.rs:101-108`](https://github.com/constructorfabric/gears-rust/blob/66da80e5715e10c08c6fafd5e9832137389a3355/gears/system/oagw/oagw/src/infra/proxy/headers.rs#L101-L108)); update the [`sdk/api.rs`](https://github.com/constructorfabric/gears-rust/blob/66da80e5715e10c08c6fafd5e9832137389a3355/gears/system/oagw/oagw-sdk/src/api.rs#L42-L44) docs. The two tests above can then drop their `Ok` branch.
- **Decided together with:** [U-4](#u-4-on_error-transforms-never-run-for-upstream-error-responses-and-metrics-count-those-responses-as-successes).

### U-6: Tests cover only a GET 500 passthrough

- **Decision:** `FIX-DOC` (tests), no priority. Add upstream-passthrough tests.
- **What the docs say:**
  - [`ADR-0013:74`](https://github.com/constructorfabric/gears-rust/blob/66da80e5715e10c08c6fafd5e9832137389a3355/gears/system/oagw/docs/ADR/0013-error-source-distinction.md#L74)
    > Integration tests verify: gateway errors include `X-OAGW-Error-Source: gateway`, upstream errors include `X-OAGW-Error-Source: upstream`
- **What the code does:**
  - [`test_error_handling.py:71-76`](https://github.com/constructorfabric/gears-rust/blob/66da80e5715e10c08c6fafd5e9832137389a3355/testing/e2e/suites/oagw/test_error_handling.py#L71-L76): the only e2e passthrough test, `test_upstream_500_passthrough`, sends a GET and checks a 500.

    ```python
    resp = await client.get(
        f"{oagw_base_url}/oagw/v1/proxy/{alias}/error/500",
        headers=oagw_headers,
    )
    assert resp.status_code == 500
    assert resp.headers.get("x-oagw-error-source") == "upstream"
    ```
  - [`proxy_integration.rs:302-320`](https://github.com/constructorfabric/gears-rust/blob/66da80e5715e10c08c6fafd5e9832137389a3355/gears/system/oagw/oagw/tests/proxy_integration.rs#L302-L320): the Rust test `proxy_upstream_500_passthrough` also covers GET 500 only (status and `ErrorSource::Upstream`).
  - Nothing covers 4xx passthrough, POST bodies, header passthrough (`Retry-After`, `WWW-Authenticate`), or telling an upstream 429 apart from a gateway 429.
  - [`mock_upstream.py:308-310`](https://github.com/constructorfabric/gears-rust/blob/66da80e5715e10c08c6fafd5e9832137389a3355/testing/e2e/suites/oagw/mock_upstream.py#L308-L310): an endpoint that no test uses, for a feature that does not exist (refresh on upstream 401 is planned, see [PLG-11](#other-doc-only-fixes-plugins)).

    ```python
    # POST /echo-401-once — returns 401 on first call, 200 thereafter.
    # Used to exercise the OAGW 401-retry logic.
    elif method == "POST" and path == "/echo-401-once":
    ```
- **Evidence of intent:** none.
- **Why this decision:** ADR-0013 (accepted) asks for integration tests of both error sources; only one upstream case is tested.
- **Impact:** Not breaking. Follow-ups: add tests for 4xx passthrough, POST bodies, `Retry-After`/`WWW-Authenticate` passthrough, and upstream-429 vs gateway-429. Remove the unused `/echo-401-once` ([`mock_upstream.py:308-311`](https://github.com/constructorfabric/gears-rust/blob/66da80e5715e10c08c6fafd5e9832137389a3355/testing/e2e/suites/oagw/mock_upstream.py#L308-L311)). Failures in the middle of a stream, after the headers are sent, are a separate case: see [STR-01](#str-01-a-mid-stream-upstream-failure-appends-problem-json-to-a-200-sse-stream-likely).

## 2. Persistence, architecture and configuration

The docs describe a database-backed, cached gateway with a different file layout; the code keeps all config in memory, and several config and path references are stale.

Mechanical doc fixes in this area: [A-03](#architecture-names), [A-04](#adr-statuses), [A-05](#config-reference) (see [Mechanical doc fixes](#mechanical-doc-fixes)).

### A-01: The docs describe database persistence, but all config lives in memory and REST/SDK-created config is lost on restart

- **Decision:** `FIX-DOC`, no priority. In-memory storage is the intended current state; fix the docs. DESIGN, PRD and ADR-0008 stay the target design.
- **What the docs say:**
  - [`F0001:92-94`](https://github.com/constructorfabric/gears-rust/blob/66da80e5715e10c08c6fafd5e9832137389a3355/gears/system/oagw/docs/features/0001-cpt-cf-oagw-feature-domain-foundation.md#L92-L94)
    > 4. [x] - `p1` - DB: RUN all `oagw_*` migrations (SeaORM migrator) - `inst-boot-4`
  - [`F0002:107-113`](https://github.com/constructorfabric/gears-rust/blob/66da80e5715e10c08c6fafd5e9832137389a3355/gears/system/oagw/docs/features/0002-cpt-cf-oagw-feature-management-api.md#L107-L113)
    > 8. [x] - `p1` - DB: BEGIN transaction - `inst-create-us-8` … 12. [x] - `p1` - DB: COMMIT - `inst-create-us-12`

    Same pattern at [`F0002:147-153`](https://github.com/constructorfabric/gears-rust/blob/66da80e5715e10c08c6fafd5e9832137389a3355/gears/system/oagw/docs/features/0002-cpt-cf-oagw-feature-management-api.md#L147-L153) and [`F0002:229-234`](https://github.com/constructorfabric/gears-rust/blob/66da80e5715e10c08c6fafd5e9832137389a3355/gears/system/oagw/docs/features/0002-cpt-cf-oagw-feature-management-api.md#L229-L234).
  - [`F0004:166`](https://github.com/constructorfabric/gears-rust/blob/66da80e5715e10c08c6fafd5e9832137389a3355/gears/system/oagw/docs/features/0004-cpt-cf-oagw-feature-proxy-engine.md#L166)
    > 1. [x] - `p1` - DB: SELECT upstream FROM oagw_upstream WHERE alias = :alias AND tenant_id = :tenant_id AND enabled = true - `inst-alias-1`

    SQL steps continue through [`F0004:166-216`](https://github.com/constructorfabric/gears-rust/blob/66da80e5715e10c08c6fafd5e9832137389a3355/gears/system/oagw/docs/features/0004-cpt-cf-oagw-feature-proxy-engine.md#L166-L216).
  - [`F0005:97`](https://github.com/constructorfabric/gears-rust/blob/66da80e5715e10c08c6fafd5e9832137389a3355/gears/system/oagw/docs/features/0005-cpt-cf-oagw-feature-tenant-hierarchy.md#L97)
    > 6. [x] - `p2` - DB: INSERT oagw_upstream with sharing mode columns
  - [`F0001:310`](https://github.com/constructorfabric/gears-rust/blob/66da80e5715e10c08c6fafd5e9832137389a3355/gears/system/oagw/docs/features/0001-cpt-cf-oagw-feature-domain-foundation.md#L310): no migration tests exist.
    > - [x] `cargo test` passes for all domain model unit tests and migration tests
  - [`F0001:226`](https://github.com/constructorfabric/gears-rust/blob/66da80e5715e10c08c6fafd5e9832137389a3355/gears/system/oagw/docs/features/0001-cpt-cf-oagw-feature-domain-foundation.md#L226): the same doc leaves the DB-schema DoD unticked, so it contradicts itself.
    > - [ ] `p1` - **ID**: `cpt-cf-oagw-dod-domain-db-schema`

    The ORM-entities DoD is unticked too: [`F0001:239`](https://github.com/constructorfabric/gears-rust/blob/66da80e5715e10c08c6fafd5e9832137389a3355/gears/system/oagw/docs/features/0001-cpt-cf-oagw-feature-domain-foundation.md#L239) “- [ ] `p1` - **ID**: `cpt-cf-oagw-dod-domain-orm-entities`”.
  - [`PRD.md:645`](https://github.com/constructorfabric/gears-rust/blob/66da80e5715e10c08c6fafd5e9832137389a3355/gears/system/oagw/docs/PRD.md#L645)
    > 3. System persists upstream configuration

    Also [`PRD.md:667`](https://github.com/constructorfabric/gears-rust/blob/66da80e5715e10c08c6fafd5e9832137389a3355/gears/system/oagw/docs/PRD.md#L667) “3. System persists route configuration” and [`PRD.md:739`](https://github.com/constructorfabric/gears-rust/blob/66da80e5715e10c08c6fafd5e9832137389a3355/gears/system/oagw/docs/PRD.md#L739) “| `toolkit-db` | Database persistence for upstream, route, and plugin configurations | p1 |”.
  - [`DESIGN.md:98`](https://github.com/constructorfabric/gears-rust/blob/66da80e5715e10c08c6fafd5e9832137389a3355/gears/system/oagw/docs/DESIGN.md#L98)
    > | SeaORM + `toolkit-db` | Database persistence (PostgreSQL, MySQL, SQLite) |

    Also [`DESIGN.md:88`](https://github.com/constructorfabric/gears-rust/blob/66da80e5715e10c08c6fafd5e9832137389a3355/gears/system/oagw/docs/DESIGN.md#L88) “SeaORM repositories”, [`DESIGN.md:116`](https://github.com/constructorfabric/gears-rust/blob/66da80e5715e10c08c6fafd5e9832137389a3355/gears/system/oagw/docs/DESIGN.md#L116) “CP -->|read/write| DB[(Database)]”, [`DESIGN.md:777`](https://github.com/constructorfabric/gears-rust/blob/66da80e5715e10c08c6fafd5e9832137389a3355/gears/system/oagw/docs/DESIGN.md#L777) “Database persistence (SeaORM, multi-backend)” and the table list at [`DESIGN.md:824-870`](https://github.com/constructorfabric/gears-rust/blob/66da80e5715e10c08c6fafd5e9832137389a3355/gears/system/oagw/docs/DESIGN.md#L824-L870) “### 3.6 Database Schemas & Tables”.
  - [`ADR-0008:91-92`](https://github.com/constructorfabric/gears-rust/blob/66da80e5715e10c08c6fafd5e9832137389a3355/gears/system/oagw/docs/ADR/0008-state-management.md#L91-L92) (status: accepted); ADR-0009 describes the full schema.
    > // Database connection pool … db_pool: Arc<DbConnectionPool>,
- **What the code does:**
  - [`gear.rs:80-81`](https://github.com/constructorfabric/gears-rust/blob/66da80e5715e10c08c6fafd5e9832137389a3355/gears/system/oagw/oagw/src/gear.rs#L80-L81): the gear wires in-memory repositories.

    ```rust
    let upstream_repo = Arc::new(InMemoryUpstreamRepo::new());
    let route_repo = Arc::new(InMemoryRouteRepo::new());
    ```
  - [`upstream_repo.rs:8-15`](https://github.com/constructorfabric/gears-rust/blob/66da80e5715e10c08c6fafd5e9832137389a3355/gears/system/oagw/oagw/src/infra/storage/upstream_repo.rs#L8-L15)

    ```rust
    /// In-memory upstream repository backed by `DashMap`.
    // …
        store: DashMap<Uuid, Upstream>,
    ```
  - [`gear.rs:43-44`](https://github.com/constructorfabric/gears-rust/blob/66da80e5715e10c08c6fafd5e9832137389a3355/gears/system/oagw/oagw/src/gear.rs#L43-L44): the gear declares no database capability.

    ```rust
    deps = [types_registry, authz_resolver, credstore, tenant_resolver],
    capabilities = [system, rest]
    ```
  - [`Cargo.toml:36-96`](https://github.com/constructorfabric/gears-rust/blob/66da80e5715e10c08c6fafd5e9832137389a3355/gears/system/oagw/oagw/Cargo.toml#L36-L96): no sea-orm or `toolkit-db` dependency.
  - [`gear.rs:253-299`](https://github.com/constructorfabric/gears-rust/blob/66da80e5715e10c08c6fafd5e9832137389a3355/gears/system/oagw/oagw/src/gear.rs#L253-L299): at startup `post_init` re-creates only the upstreams and routes provisioned through the types registry (GTS). Anything created over REST or the SDK is lost on restart, and each replica holds its own copy.
  - [`rate_limit.rs:43`](https://github.com/constructorfabric/gears-rust/blob/66da80e5715e10c08c6fafd5e9832137389a3355/gears/system/oagw/oagw/src/domain/rate_limit.rs#L43): rate-limit counters are in memory too; the PRD lists persisting them as a risk mitigation ([`PRD.md:755`](https://github.com/constructorfabric/gears-rust/blob/66da80e5715e10c08c6fafd5e9832137389a3355/gears/system/oagw/docs/PRD.md#L755): “Persist rate limit counters; accept brief burst on cold start”).
- **Evidence of intent:** in-memory since the first implementation, [`a989f9ba7`](https://github.com/constructorfabric/gears-rust/commit/a989f9ba7d464064a7aeb73e490ee61669f5682e) (DashMap-backed `InMemoryUpstreamRepo`). The `[x]` marks on the DB steps, including `inst-boot-4` and the migration-tests AC, were set by the [bulk checkbox sync](#why-the-docs-drifted). All tests run single-instance and in memory.
- **Why this decision:** the feature-doc checkboxes are delivery status and must match the code. The PRD, DESIGN and ADR-0008 describe where the gateway is going, so they stay as the target design.
- **Impact:** docs only; readers of F0001–F0006 stop seeing persistence as done. Not breaking. Follow-ups: untick the persistence steps and acceptance criteria in [`F0001`–`F0006`](https://github.com/constructorfabric/gears-rust/tree/66da80e5715e10c08c6fafd5e9832137389a3355/gears/system/oagw/docs/features) (about 55: F0001 ×4, F0002 ×31, F0003 ×2, F0004 ×10, F0005 ×7, F0006 ×1) as part of the status-marker sync ([O-06](#status-markers-checkbox-sync)). DESIGN, PRD and ADR-0008 stay the target design; ADR-0009 becomes `proposed` ([A-04](#adr-statuses)).
- **Decided together with:** [O-06](#status-markers-checkbox-sync).

### A-02: The documented multi-layer config cache (L1/L2/Redis) does not exist

- **Decision:** `FUTURE` + `ADR` (status notes), no priority. The multi-layer config cache is deferred until persistence exists; [A-01](#a-01-the-docs-describe-database-persistence-but-all-config-lives-in-memory-and-restsdk-created-config-is-lost-on-restart) (in-memory) is the newer decision.
- **What the docs say:**
  - [`ADR-0007:56`](https://github.com/constructorfabric/gears-rust/blob/66da80e5715e10c08c6fafd5e9832137389a3355/gears/system/oagw/docs/ADR/0007-data-plane-caching.md#L56) (status: accepted)
    > Chosen option: "Multi-layer caching: L1 (in-memory) + optional L2 (Redis) + Database"

    Cache layers: [`ADR-0007:58-106`](https://github.com/constructorfabric/gears-rust/blob/66da80e5715e10c08c6fafd5e9832137389a3355/gears/system/oagw/docs/ADR/0007-data-plane-caching.md#L58-L106) “| L2 (Redis, optional) | Shared across instances |”.
  - [`ADR-0008:57`](https://github.com/constructorfabric/gears-rust/blob/66da80e5715e10c08c6fafd5e9832137389a3355/gears/system/oagw/docs/ADR/0008-state-management.md#L57) (status: accepted)
    > **L1 Cache**: Small in-memory LRU (1000 entries, no TTL, explicit invalidation). Caches upstream and route configs resolved from CP.

    DP state: [`ADR-0008:55-116`](https://github.com/constructorfabric/gears-rust/blob/66da80e5715e10c08c6fafd5e9832137389a3355/gears/system/oagw/docs/ADR/0008-state-management.md#L55-L116) “hot_cache: Arc<Mutex<LruCache<CacheKey, ConfigValue>>>,”.
  - [`F0008:412`](https://github.com/constructorfabric/gears-rust/blob/66da80e5715e10c08c6fafd5e9832137389a3355/gears/system/oagw/docs/features/0008-cpt-cf-oagw-feature-observability.md#L412)
    > The system **MUST** implement multi-layer configuration caching to minimize DB reads on the proxy hot path

    DoD [`F0008:408-426`](https://github.com/constructorfabric/gears-rust/blob/66da80e5715e10c08c6fafd5e9832137389a3355/gears/system/oagw/docs/features/0008-cpt-cf-oagw-feature-observability.md#L408-L426); algorithm [`F0008:281-309`](https://github.com/constructorfabric/gears-rust/blob/66da80e5715e10c08c6fafd5e9832137389a3355/gears/system/oagw/docs/features/0008-cpt-cf-oagw-feature-observability.md#L281-L309) “### Multi-Layer Config Cache”; config params [`F0008:73-83`](https://github.com/constructorfabric/gears-rust/blob/66da80e5715e10c08c6fafd5e9832137389a3355/gears/system/oagw/docs/features/0008-cpt-cf-oagw-feature-observability.md#L73-L83) “DP L1 cache capacity … CP L1 cache capacity … CP L2 cache TTL … Redis operation timeout”.
  - [`DECOMPOSITION.md:481`](https://github.com/constructorfabric/gears-rust/blob/66da80e5715e10c08c6fafd5e9832137389a3355/gears/system/oagw/docs/DECOMPOSITION.md#L481)
    > Multi-layer caching: DP L1 (1,000 entries, <1μs), CP L1 (10,000 entries, <1μs), CP L2 Redis (optional, ~1-2ms), DB (source of truth)
  - [`flows/cache-invalidation.md:5`](https://github.com/constructorfabric/gears-rust/blob/66da80e5715e10c08c6fafd5e9832137389a3355/gears/system/oagw/scenarios/flows/cache-invalidation.md#L5)
    > Cache invalidation ensures consistency across Control Plane and Data Plane caches when configuration changes.

    Also [`flows/proxy-operations.md:29-42`](https://github.com/constructorfabric/gears-rust/blob/66da80e5715e10c08c6fafd5e9832137389a3355/gears/system/oagw/scenarios/flows/proxy-operations.md#L29-L42) “Checks L1 cache for hot configs”, [`:78-85`](https://github.com/constructorfabric/gears-rust/blob/66da80e5715e10c08c6fafd5e9832137389a3355/gears/system/oagw/scenarios/flows/proxy-operations.md#L78-L85) “→ Check L1 cache: upstream:{tenant_id}:openai” and [`:212-232`](https://github.com/constructorfabric/gears-rust/blob/66da80e5715e10c08c6fafd5e9832137389a3355/gears/system/oagw/scenarios/flows/proxy-operations.md#L212-L232) “Request → CP L1 miss → DP L1 miss → DP L2 miss → DB query”.
  - [`DESIGN.md:878`](https://github.com/constructorfabric/gears-rust/blob/66da80e5715e10c08c6fafd5e9832137389a3355/gears/system/oagw/docs/DESIGN.md#L878): DESIGN itself calls it future work.
    > Config caching (in-memory caching of effective upstream/route configuration to avoid DB reads on every proxy request) is a future consideration.
- **What the code does:**
  - [`service.rs:561-565`](https://github.com/constructorfabric/gears-rust/blob/66da80e5715e10c08c6fafd5e9832137389a3355/gears/system/oagw/oagw/src/infra/proxy/service.rs#L561-L565): every proxy call resolves directly against the in-memory repositories; there is no config cache in between.

    ```rust
    // 1. Resolve upstream + route in one pass (single hierarchy walk).
    let (upstream, route) = self
        .cp
        .resolve_proxy_target(&ctx, &alias, method.as_ref(), &path_suffix)
    ```
  - [`config.rs:23-53`](https://github.com/constructorfabric/gears-rust/blob/66da80e5715e10c08c6fafd5e9832137389a3355/gears/system/oagw/oagw/src/config.rs#L23-L53): `OagwConfig` has no config-cache settings. Its only cache settings are for the OAuth2 token cache and the ALPN protocol cache.
  - [`pingora_proxy.rs:60-62`](https://github.com/constructorfabric/gears-rust/blob/66da80e5715e10c08c6fafd5e9832137389a3355/gears/system/oagw/oagw/src/infra/proxy/pingora_proxy.rs#L60-L62) and [`pingora_proxy.rs:359-360`](https://github.com/constructorfabric/gears-rust/blob/66da80e5715e10c08c6fafd5e9832137389a3355/gears/system/oagw/oagw/src/infra/proxy/pingora_proxy.rs#L359-L360): the caches that do exist are the ALPN protocol cache and the per-upstream load-balancer cache, plus the OAuth2 token cache. None of them appears in the ADRs.
- **Evidence of intent:** DESIGN's "future consideration" line and ADR-0007 as `accepted` were written in the same commit, [`3010e054f`](https://github.com/constructorfabric/gears-rust/commit/3010e054fead23d9e60efa63fe7a2d4be11fd72c) ([design-era docs](#why-the-docs-drifted)).
- **Why this decision:** ADR-0007 and ADR-0008 are accepted, but the cache sits in front of a database that the newer A-01 decision defers. An accepted ADR gets a status note, not a rewrite.
- **Impact:** docs only. Not breaking. Follow-ups:
  - Add a "deferred until persistence" status note to [ADR-0007](https://github.com/constructorfabric/gears-rust/blob/66da80e5715e10c08c6fafd5e9832137389a3355/gears/system/oagw/docs/ADR/0007-data-plane-caching.md#L1-L5) and [ADR-0008](https://github.com/constructorfabric/gears-rust/blob/66da80e5715e10c08c6fafd5e9832137389a3355/gears/system/oagw/docs/ADR/0008-state-management.md#L1-L5) (no history rewrite).
  - Untick [`F0008:281-309`](https://github.com/constructorfabric/gears-rust/blob/66da80e5715e10c08c6fafd5e9832137389a3355/gears/system/oagw/docs/features/0008-cpt-cf-oagw-feature-observability.md#L281-L309), DoD [`F0008:408-426`](https://github.com/constructorfabric/gears-rust/blob/66da80e5715e10c08c6fafd5e9832137389a3355/gears/system/oagw/docs/features/0008-cpt-cf-oagw-feature-observability.md#L408-L426), config params [`F0008:73-83`](https://github.com/constructorfabric/gears-rust/blob/66da80e5715e10c08c6fafd5e9832137389a3355/gears/system/oagw/docs/features/0008-cpt-cf-oagw-feature-observability.md#L73-L83). At baseline these checkboxes are already `[ ]` and the config table has none, so what they need is a future marker.
  - Mark [`DECOMPOSITION:481`](https://github.com/constructorfabric/gears-rust/blob/66da80e5715e10c08c6fafd5e9832137389a3355/gears/system/oagw/docs/DECOMPOSITION.md#L481), [`flows/cache-invalidation.md`](https://github.com/constructorfabric/gears-rust/blob/66da80e5715e10c08c6fafd5e9832137389a3355/gears/system/oagw/scenarios/flows/cache-invalidation.md) and [`flows/proxy-operations.md:29-42`](https://github.com/constructorfabric/gears-rust/blob/66da80e5715e10c08c6fafd5e9832137389a3355/gears/system/oagw/scenarios/flows/proxy-operations.md#L29-L42), [`:78-85`](https://github.com/constructorfabric/gears-rust/blob/66da80e5715e10c08c6fafd5e9832137389a3355/gears/system/oagw/scenarios/flows/proxy-operations.md#L78-L85), [`:212-232`](https://github.com/constructorfabric/gears-rust/blob/66da80e5715e10c08c6fafd5e9832137389a3355/gears/system/oagw/scenarios/flows/proxy-operations.md#L212-L232) as future.
  - Document the caches that exist (OAuth2 token cache, ALPN protocol cache [`pingora_proxy.rs:60`](https://github.com/constructorfabric/gears-rust/blob/66da80e5715e10c08c6fafd5e9832137389a3355/gears/system/oagw/oagw/src/infra/proxy/pingora_proxy.rs#L60), per-upstream LB [`pingora_proxy.rs:360`](https://github.com/constructorfabric/gears-rust/blob/66da80e5715e10c08c6fafd5e9832137389a3355/gears/system/oagw/oagw/src/infra/proxy/pingora_proxy.rs#L360)) in DESIGN.
- **Decided together with:** [A-01](#a-01-the-docs-describe-database-persistence-but-all-config-lives-in-memory-and-restsdk-created-config-is-lost-on-restart).

### A-06: Docs put every OAGW path under `/api/oagw/v1/...`, but the gear serves `/oagw/v1/...`, and error `instance` values are built inconsistently

- **Decision:** `FIX-DOC` + `FIX-CODE`, P2 (code). Document paths as `/oagw/v1/...`, with one note that deployments may add an api-gateway `prefix_path` (e.g. `/api`). The problem `instance` is the request path without the query string, built the same way everywhere.
- **What the docs say:**
  - [`DESIGN.md:374-377`](https://github.com/constructorfabric/gears-rust/blob/66da80e5715e10c08c6fafd5e9832137389a3355/gears/system/oagw/docs/DESIGN.md#L374-L377)
    > | `/api/oagw/v1/upstreams/*` | Control Plane | Upstream CRUD | … | `/api/oagw/v1/proxy/*` | Data Plane | Proxy requests |
  - [`PRD.md:643`](https://github.com/constructorfabric/gears-rust/blob/66da80e5715e10c08c6fafd5e9832137389a3355/gears/system/oagw/docs/PRD.md#L643)
    > 1. Operator sends POST to `/api/oagw/v1/upstreams` with server endpoints, protocol, auth config
  - [scenario 12.2](https://github.com/constructorfabric/gears-rust/blob/66da80e5715e10c08c6fafd5e9832137389a3355/gears/system/oagw/scenarios/protocols/http/negative-12.2-upstream-error-passthrough-esrc-upstream.md#L10)
    > GET /api/oagw/v1/proxy/<alias>/fail HTTP/1.1
  - In total 289 lines under [`docs/`](https://github.com/constructorfabric/gears-rust/tree/66da80e5715e10c08c6fafd5e9832137389a3355/gears/system/oagw/docs) and [`scenarios/`](https://github.com/constructorfabric/gears-rust/tree/66da80e5715e10c08c6fafd5e9832137389a3355/gears/system/oagw/scenarios) use `/api/oagw/v1`.
- **What the code does:**
  - [`routes/upstream.rs:18`](https://github.com/constructorfabric/gears-rust/blob/66da80e5715e10c08c6fafd5e9832137389a3355/gears/system/oagw/oagw/src/api/rest/routes/upstream.rs#L18): routes are registered without `/api`.

    ```rust
    router = OperationBuilder::post("/oagw/v1/upstreams")
    ```

    The proxy route likewise: [`routes/proxy.rs:13`](https://github.com/constructorfabric/gears-rust/blob/66da80e5715e10c08c6fafd5e9832137389a3355/gears/system/oagw/oagw/src/api/rest/routes/proxy.rs#L13).
  - [`api-gateway/src/config.rs:68-72`](https://github.com/constructorfabric/gears-rust/blob/66da80e5715e10c08c6fafd5e9832137389a3355/gears/system/api-gateway/src/config.rs#L68-L72): a prefix appears only if the api-gateway `prefix_path` is set, and it defaults to empty. The e2e config sets none, and e2e tests call `/oagw/v1/proxy/...` directly.

    ```rust
    /// Empty string (the default) means no prefix.
    #[serde(default)]
    pub prefix_path: String,
    ```
  - [`handlers/upstream.rs:41`](https://github.com/constructorfabric/gears-rust/blob/66da80e5715e10c08c6fafd5e9832137389a3355/gears/system/oagw/oagw/src/api/rest/handlers/upstream.rs#L41): management handlers hard-code `instance`, without any deployment prefix.

    ```rust
    .map_err(|e| domain_error_to_problem(e, "/oagw/v1/upstreams"))?;
    ```

    Same at [`handlers/upstream.rs:52`](https://github.com/constructorfabric/gears-rust/blob/66da80e5715e10c08c6fafd5e9832137389a3355/gears/system/oagw/oagw/src/api/rest/handlers/upstream.rs#L52).
  - [`service.rs:365`](https://github.com/constructorfabric/gears-rust/blob/66da80e5715e10c08c6fafd5e9832137389a3355/gears/system/oagw/oagw/src/infra/proxy/service.rs#L365): the proxy path uses the full rewritten URI, `/{alias}/{path}?query`, as `instance`, query string included.

    ```rust
    let instance_uri = req.uri().to_string();
    ```
  - [`pingora_proxy.rs:860`](https://github.com/constructorfabric/gears-rust/blob/66da80e5715e10c08c6fafd5e9832137389a3355/gears/system/oagw/oagw/src/infra/proxy/pingora_proxy.rs#L860): problems written by Pingora use the same value.
  - [`canonical_error_layer.rs:212-213`](https://github.com/constructorfabric/gears-rust/blob/66da80e5715e10c08c6fafd5e9832137389a3355/libs/toolkit/src/api/canonical_error_layer.rs#L212-L213): when `instance` is missing, the toolkit middleware fills in the request path only, with no query string.
- **Evidence of intent:** none.
- **Why this decision:** reference docs must match the wire, and `/oagw/v1` is what the gear serves; `/api` is deployment config. Query strings may carry keys, which then get echoed in error bodies and logged.
- **Impact:** docs: not breaking. Code: clients that read `instance` in problem bodies see it change (no query string, one consistent form). Follow-ups: rewrite the 289 `/api/oagw/v1` lines in [`docs/`](https://github.com/constructorfabric/gears-rust/tree/66da80e5715e10c08c6fafd5e9832137389a3355/gears/system/oagw/docs) and [`scenarios/`](https://github.com/constructorfabric/gears-rust/tree/66da80e5715e10c08c6fafd5e9832137389a3355/gears/system/oagw/scenarios); add the prefix note to DESIGN and [README](https://github.com/constructorfabric/gears-rust/blob/66da80e5715e10c08c6fafd5e9832137389a3355/gears/system/oagw/oagw/README.md); build `instance` in one place instead of [`service.rs:365`](https://github.com/constructorfabric/gears-rust/blob/66da80e5715e10c08c6fafd5e9832137389a3355/gears/system/oagw/oagw/src/infra/proxy/service.rs#L365), [`pingora_proxy.rs:860`](https://github.com/constructorfabric/gears-rust/blob/66da80e5715e10c08c6fafd5e9832137389a3355/gears/system/oagw/oagw/src/infra/proxy/pingora_proxy.rs#L860) and the hard-coded strings in [`handlers/upstream.rs:41`](https://github.com/constructorfabric/gears-rust/blob/66da80e5715e10c08c6fafd5e9832137389a3355/gears/system/oagw/oagw/src/api/rest/handlers/upstream.rs#L41) and [`:52`](https://github.com/constructorfabric/gears-rust/blob/66da80e5715e10c08c6fafd5e9832137389a3355/gears/system/oagw/oagw/src/api/rest/handlers/upstream.rs#L52).

## 3. Management API

This section covers the upstream and route management API (REST and SDK): authorization, request validation, list queries, schemas and the side effects of writes.

Mechanical doc fixes in this area: [M-05](#other-doc-only-fixes-management-api), [M-06](#other-doc-only-fixes-management-api), [M-14](#other-doc-only-fixes-management-api), [M-15](#other-doc-only-fixes-management-api), [M-16](#other-doc-only-fixes-management-api), [M-17](#other-doc-only-fixes-management-api), [M-18](#other-doc-only-fixes-management-api) (see [Mechanical doc fixes](#mechanical-doc-fixes)).

### M-01: Any authenticated caller can create, change and delete its tenant's upstreams and routes

- **Decision:** `FIX-CODE`, P0. Add a per-operation permission check to upstream and route create, get, list, update and delete.
- **What the docs say:**
  - [`F0002:101`](https://github.com/constructorfabric/gears-rust/blob/66da80e5715e10c08c6fafd5e9832137389a3355/gears/system/oagw/docs/features/0002-cpt-cf-oagw-feature-management-api.md#L101)
    > 3. [x] - `p1` - API: Validate actor has `gts.cf.core.oagw.upstream.v1~:create` permission - `inst-create-us-3`
  - The same ticked step exists for update ([l.136](https://github.com/constructorfabric/gears-rust/blob/66da80e5715e10c08c6fafd5e9832137389a3355/gears/system/oagw/docs/features/0002-cpt-cf-oagw-feature-management-api.md#L136)), delete ([l.170](https://github.com/constructorfabric/gears-rust/blob/66da80e5715e10c08c6fafd5e9832137389a3355/gears/system/oagw/docs/features/0002-cpt-cf-oagw-feature-management-api.md#L170)), list ([l.194](https://github.com/constructorfabric/gears-rust/blob/66da80e5715e10c08c6fafd5e9832137389a3355/gears/system/oagw/docs/features/0002-cpt-cf-oagw-feature-management-api.md#L194)), route create ([l.224](https://github.com/constructorfabric/gears-rust/blob/66da80e5715e10c08c6fafd5e9832137389a3355/gears/system/oagw/docs/features/0002-cpt-cf-oagw-feature-management-api.md#L224)) and route update/delete/read ([l.256](https://github.com/constructorfabric/gears-rust/blob/66da80e5715e10c08c6fafd5e9832137389a3355/gears/system/oagw/docs/features/0002-cpt-cf-oagw-feature-management-api.md#L256), [l.263](https://github.com/constructorfabric/gears-rust/blob/66da80e5715e10c08c6fafd5e9832137389a3355/gears/system/oagw/docs/features/0002-cpt-cf-oagw-feature-management-api.md#L263), [l.269](https://github.com/constructorfabric/gears-rust/blob/66da80e5715e10c08c6fafd5e9832137389a3355/gears/system/oagw/docs/features/0002-cpt-cf-oagw-feature-management-api.md#L269)).
  - [`F0002:505`](https://github.com/constructorfabric/gears-rust/blob/66da80e5715e10c08c6fafd5e9832137389a3355/gears/system/oagw/docs/features/0002-cpt-cf-oagw-feature-management-api.md#L505)
    > - [x] All operations require appropriate GTS permissions and return 403 on unauthorized access
  - [`DESIGN.md:760-764`](https://github.com/constructorfabric/gears-rust/blob/66da80e5715e10c08c6fafd5e9832137389a3355/gears/system/oagw/docs/DESIGN.md#L760-L764)
    > | `gts.cf.core.oagw.upstream.v1~:{create;override;read;delete}` | Upstream CRUD |
    > | `gts.cf.core.oagw.route.v1~:{create;override;read;delete}` | Route CRUD |
  - The same permissions appear in the table at [`DESIGN.md:586-591`](https://github.com/constructorfabric/gears-rust/blob/66da80e5715e10c08c6fafd5e9832137389a3355/gears/system/oagw/docs/DESIGN.md#L586-L591).
  - [`PRD.md:640`](https://github.com/constructorfabric/gears-rust/blob/66da80e5715e10c08c6fafd5e9832137389a3355/gears/system/oagw/docs/PRD.md#L640) (use case "Configure Upstream")
    > - Actor is authenticated with `gts.cf.core.oagw.upstream.v1~:create` permission
  - [scenario auth/negative-1.2](https://github.com/constructorfabric/gears-rust/blob/66da80e5715e10c08c6fafd5e9832137389a3355/gears/system/oagw/scenarios/management-api/auth/negative-1.2-permission-gates-upstream-route-plugin-crud.md#L3-L28)
    > Scenario A: missing permission … POST /api/oagw/v1/routes HTTP/1.1 … - `403 Forbidden`
  - [`flows/management-operations.md:58`](https://github.com/constructorfabric/gears-rust/blob/66da80e5715e10c08c6fafd5e9832137389a3355/gears/system/oagw/scenarios/flows/management-operations.md#L58)
    > - Checks `oagw:upstream:create` permission → 403 if denied
- **What the code does:**
  - [`routes/upstream.rs:18-33`](https://github.com/constructorfabric/gears-rust/blob/66da80e5715e10c08c6fafd5e9832137389a3355/gears/system/oagw/oagw/src/api/rest/routes/upstream.rs#L18-L33)

    ```rust
            .authenticated()
            .require_license_features::<License>([])
    ```

    Every upstream and route operation is registered with only these two guards ([`routes/route.rs`](https://github.com/constructorfabric/gears-rust/blob/66da80e5715e10c08c6fafd5e9832137389a3355/gears/system/oagw/oagw/src/api/rest/routes/route.rs#L18-L33) is the same).
  - [`bind.rs:55-135`](https://github.com/constructorfabric/gears-rust/blob/66da80e5715e10c08c6fafd5e9832137389a3355/gears/system/oagw/oagw/src/domain/services/management/bind.rs#L55-L135): the only `access_scope_with` calls in management are the bind and per-field override checks.
  - [`bind.rs:214-218`](https://github.com/constructorfabric/gears-rust/blob/66da80e5715e10c08c6fafd5e9832137389a3355/gears/system/oagw/oagw/src/domain/services/management/bind.rs#L214-L218)

    ```rust
        for &ancestor_tid in &tenant_chain[1..] {
            match upstreams.get_by_alias(ancestor_tid, alias).await {
                Ok(ancestor_upstream) => {
                    validate_bind_constraints(ctx, enforcer, &ancestor_upstream, overrides).await?;
    ```

    These checks run only when an ancestor tenant has an upstream with the same alias, and only on upstream create and update ([`mod.rs:112-125`](https://github.com/constructorfabric/gears-rust/blob/66da80e5715e10c08c6fafd5e9832137389a3355/gears/system/oagw/oagw/src/domain/services/management/mod.rs#L112-L125), [`mod.rs:247-260`](https://github.com/constructorfabric/gears-rust/blob/66da80e5715e10c08c6fafd5e9832137389a3355/gears/system/oagw/oagw/src/domain/services/management/mod.rs#L247-L260)). Route operations and upstream get/list/delete have no check at all.
  - Result: any authenticated subject can create, update or delete its tenant's upstreams and routes.
  - Other gears check each operation. Example from file-storage, [`infra/authz.rs:60-67`](https://github.com/constructorfabric/gears-rust/blob/66da80e5715e10c08c6fafd5e9832137389a3355/gears/file-storage/file-storage/src/infra/authz.rs#L60-L67):

    ```rust
            self.enforcer
                .access_scope_with(ctx, &FILE_RESOURCE, action, file_id, &request)
    ```

    This authorizer is wired in at [`file-storage gear.rs:152`](https://github.com/constructorfabric/gears-rust/blob/66da80e5715e10c08c6fafd5e9832137389a3355/gears/file-storage/file-storage/src/gear.rs#L152) and called per action (for example [`policy_service.rs:136`](https://github.com/constructorfabric/gears-rust/blob/66da80e5715e10c08c6fafd5e9832137389a3355/gears/file-storage/file-storage/src/domain/policy_service.rs#L136)).
- **Evidence of intent:** none. The `[x]` marks come from the [bulk checkbox sync](#why-the-docs-drifted) ([`2aed1705e`](https://github.com/constructorfabric/gears-rust/commit/2aed1705ef781a87695c9e29f9b09838ccf419f5)), and no commit ever added CRUD action checks. No test covers it: [`e2e/test_authz.py`](https://github.com/constructorfabric/gears-rust/blob/66da80e5715e10c08c6fafd5e9832137389a3355/testing/e2e/suites/oagw/test_authz.py) only tests proxy authorization.
- **Why this decision:** Security override: missing authorization is fixed in code whatever the docs say. The DESIGN permission table and the PRD use-case preconditions both require these permissions.
- **Impact:** Operators and tenant admins. Breaking for operators: a subject whose policy does not grant `create`/`override`/`read`/`delete` on `upstream.v1~`/`route.v1~` starts getting 403 on management calls, so policies must be in place before rollout.
