  - [scenario 3.5](https://github.com/constructorfabric/gears-rust/blob/66da80e5715e10c08c6fafd5e9832137389a3355/gears/system/oagw/scenarios/management-api/routes/positive-3.5-path-suffix-mode-append.md#L19-L29)
    > Outbound path equals `/api` + `/v1/users/123`. … No `//` introduced in path.
- **What the code does:**
  - [`route_repo.rs:142-145`](https://github.com/constructorfabric/gears-rust/blob/66da80e5715e10c08c6fafd5e9832137389a3355/gears/system/oagw/oagw/src/infra/storage/route_repo.rs#L142-L145)

    ```rust
    // Path must be a prefix match.
    if !path.starts_with(&http_match.path) {
        continue;
    }
    ```
  - [`service.rs:783`](https://github.com/constructorfabric/gears-rust/blob/66da80e5715e10c08c6fafd5e9832137389a3355/gears/system/oagw/oagw/src/infra/proxy/service.rs#L783): strips the route path from the request path as plain text, so `/v1/chatty` under route `/v1/chat` leaves `ty`.
  - [`request_builder.rs:37-46`](https://github.com/constructorfabric/gears-rust/blob/66da80e5715e10c08c6fafd5e9832137389a3355/gears/system/oagw/oagw/src/infra/proxy/request_builder.rs#L37-L46): inserts a `/` between route path and remainder, so the upstream gets `/v1/chat/ty` (and `/administrator` under route `/admin` becomes `/admin/istrator`).
  - [`service.rs:370-378`](https://github.com/constructorfabric/gears-rust/blob/66da80e5715e10c08c6fafd5e9832137389a3355/gears/system/oagw/oagw/src/infra/proxy/service.rs#L370-L378) and [`service.rs:1764-1781`](https://github.com/constructorfabric/gears-rust/blob/66da80e5715e10c08c6fafd5e9832137389a3355/gears/system/oagw/oagw/src/infra/proxy/service.rs#L1764-L1781): `normalize_path` drops empty segments and trailing slashes; a bare `/proxy/{alias}` yields an empty path, which never matches route `/`.
- **Evidence of intent:** none. The plain `starts_with` dates from the first implementation ([`a989f9ba7`](https://github.com/constructorfabric/gears-rust/commit/a989f9ba7d464064a7aeb73e490ee61669f5682e)). No test uses a prefix that ends mid-segment.
- **Why this decision:** The DESIGN transformation rule (append the suffix to `match.http.path`) and ADR-0009's segment-wise candidate prefixes both assume segment boundaries, and scenario 3.5 expects a clean joined path. No document asks for mid-segment matching.
- **Impact:** Breaking for clients: a request that matched only through a mid-segment prefix (e.g. `/v1/chatty` under route `/v1/chat`) gets 404 instead of reaching a mangled upstream path; a bare `/proxy/{alias}` starts matching route `/`. Follow-ups: tests for `/v1/chat` vs `/v1/chatty`, `/admin` vs `/administrator`, and scenario 3.5 (no `//`). Ships in the same change as [P-02](#p-02-percent-encoded--in-the-proxy-path-reaches-the-upstream-unchanged).
- **Decided together with:** [P-02](#p-02-percent-encoded--in-the-proxy-path-reaches-the-upstream-unchanged)

### P-02: Percent-encoded `..` in the proxy path reaches the upstream unchanged

- **Decision:** `FIX-CODE`, P0. The proxy rejects percent-encoded dot-segments and encoded `/` in the path with 400.
- **What the docs say:**
  - [`F0008:252`](https://github.com/constructorfabric/gears-rust/blob/66da80e5715e10c08c6fafd5e9832137389a3355/gears/system/oagw/docs/features/0008-cpt-cf-oagw-feature-observability.md#L252)
    > 6. [ ] - `p1` - Validate request path against route configuration: reject path traversal sequences (`../`, `..\\`, encoded variants) - `inst-ssrf-6`
  - [`PRD.md:492`](https://github.com/constructorfabric/gears-rust/blob/66da80e5715e10c08c6fafd5e9832137389a3355/gears/system/oagw/docs/PRD.md#L492)
    > The system **MUST** validate DNS resolution results, enforce IP pinning rules, strip well-known internal headers, and validate request paths and query parameters against route configuration.
- **What the code does:**
  - [`service.rs:1764-1774`](https://github.com/constructorfabric/gears-rust/blob/66da80e5715e10c08c6fafd5e9832137389a3355/gears/system/oagw/oagw/src/infra/proxy/service.rs#L1764-L1774)

    ```rust
    fn normalize_path(path: &str) -> String {
        let mut segments: Vec<&str> = Vec::new();
        for seg in path.split('/') {
            match seg {
                "" | "." => {}
                ".." => {
    ```
    Only literal `.` and `..` are resolved (silently). `%2e%2e` and `..%2f` are ordinary segments.
  - [`service.rs:370-378`](https://github.com/constructorfabric/gears-rust/blob/66da80e5715e10c08c6fafd5e9832137389a3355/gears/system/oagw/oagw/src/infra/proxy/service.rs#L370-L378): the suffix comes from the raw, still percent-encoded URI path. Route matching ([`route_repo.rs:143`](https://github.com/constructorfabric/gears-rust/blob/66da80e5715e10c08c6fafd5e9832137389a3355/gears/system/oagw/oagw/src/infra/storage/route_repo.rs#L143)) and the upstream URL ([`request_builder.rs:48`](https://github.com/constructorfabric/gears-rust/blob/66da80e5715e10c08c6fafd5e9832137389a3355/gears/system/oagw/oagw/src/infra/proxy/request_builder.rs#L48)) use that string as-is.
  - Result: `/v1/public/%2e%2e/admin` matches route `/v1/public` and is forwarded verbatim. *Likely* impact: most upstreams (nginx, most frameworks) decode it and serve `/v1/admin`.
- **Evidence of intent:** Tests cover literal `..` only: [`normalize_resolves_dot_dot`](https://github.com/constructorfabric/gears-rust/blob/66da80e5715e10c08c6fafd5e9832137389a3355/gears/system/oagw/oagw/src/infra/proxy/service.rs#L1796-L1799) asserts `/alias/../admin/secret` → `/admin/secret`, and [`normalize_clamps_above_root`](https://github.com/constructorfabric/gears-rust/blob/66da80e5715e10c08c6fafd5e9832137389a3355/gears/system/oagw/oagw/src/infra/proxy/service.rs#L1801-L1804) asserts `/alias/../../etc/passwd` → `/etc/passwd`.
- **Why this decision:** Security first: the PRD SSRF requirement asks for path validation, and F0008 names encoded variants explicitly.
- **Impact:** Breaking for clients: a path containing `%2e` or `%2f` variants gets 400 instead of being forwarded. Follow-ups: tests for `/v1/public/%2e%2e/admin` and `..%2f`. Same change as [P-01](#p-01-a-route-prefix-matches-mid-segment-so-v1chatty-reaches-the-upstream-as-v1chatty).
- **Decided together with:** [P-01](#p-01-a-route-prefix-matches-mid-segment-so-v1chatty-reaches-the-upstream-as-v1chatty)

### P-03: A common-suffix pool called without `X-OAGW-Target-Host` round-robins instead of returning 400

- **Decision:** `FIX-CODE`, P1. A derived common-suffix alias with 2+ endpoints and no `X-OAGW-Target-Host` returns 400 `MissingTargetHost`, per the ADR-0002 matrix. Explicit aliases keep round-robin.
- **What the docs say:**
  - [`ADR-0002:183`](https://github.com/constructorfabric/gears-rust/blob/66da80e5715e10c08c6fafd5e9832137389a3355/gears/system/oagw/docs/ADR/0002-request-routing.md#L183)
    > | Multi-endpoint | 2+ | Common suffix | No | 400 Bad Request (missing required header) |
  - [`ADR-0002:173`](https://github.com/constructorfabric/gears-rust/blob/66da80e5715e10c08c6fafd5e9832137389a3355/gears/system/oagw/docs/ADR/0002-request-routing.md#L173)
    > Upstream configuration: Common suffix alias `vendor.com` with endpoints `us.vendor.com` and `eu.vendor.com`. The `X-OAGW-Target-Host` header is **required** to disambiguate the target endpoint.
  - [`F0004:131-132`](https://github.com/constructorfabric/gears-rust/blob/66da80e5715e10c08c6fafd5e9832137389a3355/gears/system/oagw/docs/features/0004-cpt-cf-oagw-feature-proxy-engine.md#L131-L132)
    > 21. [x] - `p1` - **IF** multi-endpoint upstream with common-suffix alias AND `X-OAGW-Target-Host` header missing - `inst-proxy-21` … **RETURN** 400 MissingTargetHost with `X-OAGW-Error-Source: gateway` - `inst-proxy-21a`
  - [custom-header-routing scenario negative-1.1](https://github.com/constructorfabric/gears-rust/blob/66da80e5715e10c08c6fafd5e9832137389a3355/gears/system/oagw/scenarios/proxy-api/custom-header-routing/negative-1.1-missing-header-common-suffix.md#L20-L22)
    > Request is rejected with `400 Bad Request`
- **What the code does:**
  - [`service.rs:320-335`](https://github.com/constructorfabric/gears-rust/blob/66da80e5715e10c08c6fafd5e9832137389a3355/gears/system/oagw/oagw/src/infra/proxy/service.rs#L320-L335)

    ```rust
    // Tier 2: Automatic selection.
    if endpoints.len() == 1 {
    // …
    // Multi-endpoint: round-robin via BackendSelector.
    self.backend_selector
        .select(upstream.id, endpoints)
    ```
  - [`domain/error.rs:41-42`](https://github.com/constructorfabric/gears-rust/blob/66da80e5715e10c08c6fafd5e9832137389a3355/gears/system/oagw/oagw/src/domain/error.rs#L41-L42): `DomainError::MissingTargetHost` exists, but nothing constructs it.
- **Evidence of intent:** The F0004 steps are ticked by the [bulk checkbox sync](#why-the-docs-drifted); no test covers the missing-header case. Scenarios disagree with each other: [upstreams scenario positive-2.10](https://github.com/constructorfabric/gears-rust/blob/66da80e5715e10c08c6fafd5e9832137389a3355/gears/system/oagw/scenarios/management-api/upstreams/positive-2.10-multi-endpoint-load-balancing-distributes-requests.md#L36) expects “Requests are distributed across endpoints.” for the same `vendor.com` pool with no header, which matches the code. [Alias-resolution scenario negative-6.3](https://github.com/constructorfabric/gears-rust/blob/66da80e5715e10c08c6fafd5e9832137389a3355/gears/system/oagw/scenarios/proxy-api/alias-resolution/negative-6.3-multi-endpoint-common-suffix-alias-requires-host-header.md#L22) asks for the inbound Host instead: “`detail` indicates `Host` header required.”, but OAGW overwrites `Host` ([`service.rs:760`](https://github.com/constructorfabric/gears-rust/blob/66da80e5715e10c08c6fafd5e9832137389a3355/gears/system/oagw/oagw/src/infra/proxy/service.rs#L760)).
- **Why this decision:** ADR-0002 (accepted) sets the behaviour. It matters for region-split pools (e.g. `us.`/`eu.vendor.com`) used for data residency, where silent round-robin can send a request to the wrong region.
- **Impact:** **Breaking for clients**: callers of a derived common-suffix alias with 2+ endpoints and no `X-OAGW-Target-Host` get 400 instead of a round-robin response. Needs a release note. Follow-ups: in `select_endpoint` ([`service.rs:261`](https://github.com/constructorfabric/gears-rust/blob/66da80e5715e10c08c6fafd5e9832137389a3355/gears/system/oagw/oagw/src/infra/proxy/service.rs#L261)) construct `DomainError::MissingTargetHost`; rewrite scenario `upstreams/positive-2.10` to use an explicit alias; scenario `alias-resolution/negative-6.3` uses `X-OAGW-Target-Host`, not `Host`.

### P-04: The REST handler buffers the whole request body before checking `proxy:invoke`

- **Decision:** `FIX-CODE`, P1 (authz order) / P2 (streaming). Authorize before buffering the body, and run Content-Type/smuggling validation after authz. Streaming REST bodies is a separate P2 part.
- **What the docs say:**
  - [`F0004:107-108`](https://github.com/constructorfabric/gears-rust/blob/66da80e5715e10c08c6fafd5e9832137389a3355/gears/system/oagw/docs/features/0004-cpt-cf-oagw-feature-proxy-engine.md#L107-L108)
    > 4. [x] - `p1` - **IF** token lacks `gts.cf.core.oagw.proxy.v1~:invoke` permission - `inst-proxy-4` … **RETURN** 403 Forbidden with `X-OAGW-Error-Source: gateway` - `inst-proxy-4a`
  - [`DESIGN.md:172`](https://github.com/constructorfabric/gears-rust/blob/66da80e5715e10c08c6fafd5e9832137389a3355/gears/system/oagw/docs/DESIGN.md#L172)
    > Body size hard limit: 100MB. Prevent resource exhaustion; reject before buffering.
  - [`F0004:285-286`](https://github.com/constructorfabric/gears-rust/blob/66da80e5715e10c08c6fafd5e9832137389a3355/gears/system/oagw/docs/features/0004-cpt-cf-oagw-feature-proxy-engine.md#L285-L286)
    > 5. [x] - `p1` - **ELSE IF** request has streaming body - `inst-bridge-5` … Write headers only, then stream body chunks
  - [`INDEX:419-421`](https://github.com/constructorfabric/gears-rust/blob/66da80e5715e10c08c6fafd5e9832137389a3355/gears/system/oagw/scenarios/INDEX.md#L419-L421)
    > Streaming request bodies are not buffered … For endpoints supporting streaming body, memory does not grow with body size.
- **What the code does:**
  - [`handlers/proxy.rs:164-175`](https://github.com/constructorfabric/gears-rust/blob/66da80e5715e10c08c6fafd5e9832137389a3355/gears/system/oagw/oagw/src/api/rest/handlers/proxy.rs#L164-L175)

    ```rust
    let body_bytes = if is_upgrade {
        Bytes::new()
    } else {
        axum::body::to_bytes(body, max_body_size)
            .await
    ```
  - [`service.rs:545-557`](https://github.com/constructorfabric/gears-rust/blob/66da80e5715e10c08c6fafd5e9832137389a3355/gears/system/oagw/oagw/src/infra/proxy/service.rs#L545-L557): `parse_proxy_request` runs first (Content-Type and CL/TE checks, [`service.rs:398-403`](https://github.com/constructorfabric/gears-rust/blob/66da80e5715e10c08c6fafd5e9832137389a3355/gears/system/oagw/oagw/src/infra/proxy/service.rs#L398-L403)), then `policy_enforcer.access_scope_with(… actions::INVOKE …)`. So a caller without `proxy:invoke` can make OAGW buffer a body up to `max_body_size` (100 MiB default; over REST the api-gateway body limit, 16 MB by default, caps it first). The same caller gets 400, not 403, for a malformed Content-Type or CL/TE.
  - [`handlers/proxy.rs:192-194`](https://github.com/constructorfabric/gears-rust/blob/66da80e5715e10c08c6fafd5e9832137389a3355/gears/system/oagw/oagw/src/api/rest/handlers/proxy.rs#L192-L194): the handler always hands the DP buffered bytes. Streaming (`Body::Stream`) is reachable only through the in-process SDK.
- **Evidence of intent:** Streaming bodies were added to the DP only, in [`9ca475ed8`](https://github.com/constructorfabric/gears-rust/commit/9ca475ed85297e79cf23b236b5089926b4010c1e) ("chunked transfer encoding for streaming request bodies"). The handler's `to_bytes` dates from the first implementation ([`a989f9ba7`](https://github.com/constructorfabric/gears-rust/commit/a989f9ba7d464064a7aeb73e490ee61669f5682e)). All streaming-body tests use the SDK facade ([`proxy_integration.rs:2249-2690`](https://github.com/constructorfabric/gears-rust/blob/66da80e5715e10c08c6fafd5e9832137389a3355/gears/system/oagw/oagw/tests/proxy_integration.rs#L2249-L2690)).
- **Why this decision:** Authorizing before doing work is a security matter. F0004 puts the invoke check first, and DESIGN's body-limit constraint says to reject before buffering.
- **Impact:** Not breaking for authorized clients. Callers without `proxy:invoke` get 403 instead of 400 when their Content-Type or CL/TE is malformed. The P2 part streams REST bodies through the existing `Body::Stream` DP path ([scenario 8.3](https://github.com/constructorfabric/gears-rust/blob/66da80e5715e10c08c6fafd5e9832137389a3355/gears/system/oagw/scenarios/proxy-api/body-validation/positive-8.3-streaming-request-bodies-not-buffered.md#L21) “Gateway forwards chunks without buffering the full body.”, [`INDEX:419`](https://github.com/constructorfabric/gears-rust/blob/66da80e5715e10c08c6fafd5e9832137389a3355/gears/system/oagw/scenarios/INDEX.md#L419), F0004 inst-bridge-5 [`F0004:285-286`](https://github.com/constructorfabric/gears-rust/blob/66da80e5715e10c08c6fafd5e9832137389a3355/gears/system/oagw/docs/features/0004-cpt-cf-oagw-feature-proxy-engine.md#L285-L286)).

### P-05: Over REST, hyper answers a bad Content-Length or Transfer-Encoding with a bare 400 before OAGW runs

- **Decision:** `FIX-CODE` + `FIX-DOC`, P2. Label body-read failures correctly, and document that hyper handles CL/TE over REST.
- **What the docs say:**
  - [`DESIGN.md:514`](https://github.com/constructorfabric/gears-rust/blob/66da80e5715e10c08c6fafd5e9832137389a3355/gears/system/oagw/docs/DESIGN.md#L514)
    > | Content-Length | Must be valid integer if present; must match actual size | `400 ValidationError` |
  - [`F0004:440`](https://github.com/constructorfabric/gears-rust/blob/66da80e5715e10c08c6fafd5e9832137389a3355/gears/system/oagw/docs/features/0004-cpt-cf-oagw-feature-proxy-engine.md#L440)
    > - [x] Content-Length validation rejects non-integer or mismatched values with 400
  - [scenario 7.4](https://github.com/constructorfabric/gears-rust/blob/66da80e5715e10c08c6fafd5e9832137389a3355/gears/system/oagw/scenarios/proxy-api/request-transforms/negative-7.4-well-known-header-validation-errors-400.md#L15-L35)
    > - `400 Bad Request` - `Content-Type: application/problem+json` - `X-OAGW-Error-Source: gateway` … `detail` mentions mismatch between declared and actual size.
  - [scenario 8.2](https://github.com/constructorfabric/gears-rust/blob/66da80e5715e10c08c6fafd5e9832137389a3355/gears/system/oagw/scenarios/proxy-api/body-validation/negative-8.2-transfer-encoding-support-limited-chunked.md#L10-L20)
    > Transfer-Encoding: gzip … `X-OAGW-Error-Source: gateway` - `detail` mentions unsupported transfer encoding.
- **What the code does:**
  - hyper 1.10.1 treats a non-numeric Content-Length ([`role.rs:280-281`](https://github.com/hyperium/hyper/blob/e3bcd379118e707b3e49185b047d49ebbaa943fc/src/proto/h1/role.rs#L280-L281)) and a non-`chunked` Transfer-Encoding as header parse errors:
  - [`role.rs:332-335`](https://github.com/hyperium/hyper/blob/e3bcd379118e707b3e49185b047d49ebbaa943fc/src/proto/h1/role.rs#L332-L335)

    ```rust
    if is_te && !is_te_chunked {
        debug!("request with transfer-encoding header, but not chunked, bad request");
        return Err(Parse::transfer_encoding_invalid());
    }
    ```
  - [`role.rs:466-484`](https://github.com/hyperium/hyper/blob/e3bcd379118e707b3e49185b047d49ebbaa943fc/src/proto/h1/role.rs#L466-L484): hyper answers header parse errors with an automatic 400 status line: no problem+json body, no `X-OAGW-Error-Source`.
  - OAGW's own checks ([`handlers/proxy.rs:134-160`](https://github.com/constructorfabric/gears-rust/blob/66da80e5715e10c08c6fafd5e9832137389a3355/gears/system/oagw/oagw/src/api/rest/handlers/proxy.rs#L134-L160), [`headers.rs:273-338`](https://github.com/constructorfabric/gears-rust/blob/66da80e5715e10c08c6fafd5e9832137389a3355/gears/system/oagw/oagw/src/infra/proxy/headers.rs#L273-L338)) therefore matter only for in-process SDK callers (the Content-Length size check in the handler still runs over REST).
  - [`handlers/proxy.rs:167-174`](https://github.com/constructorfabric/gears-rust/blob/66da80e5715e10c08c6fafd5e9832137389a3355/gears/system/oagw/oagw/src/api/rest/handlers/proxy.rs#L167-L174)

    ```rust
    axum::body::to_bytes(body, max_body_size)
        .await
        .map_err(|_| {
            error_response(DomainError::PayloadTooLarge {
    ```
    Every body-read error, including a client abort, is reported as `PayloadTooLarge`.
  - [`session_bridge.rs:79-97`](https://github.com/constructorfabric/gears-rust/blob/66da80e5715e10c08c6fafd5e9832137389a3355/gears/system/oagw/oagw/src/infra/proxy/session_bridge.rs#L79-L97): the bridge drops the inbound Content-Length and writes the real body length, so on the SDK path a mismatched Content-Length is silently corrected.
- **Evidence of intent:** e2e [`test_invalid_content_length_returns_400`](https://github.com/constructorfabric/gears-rust/blob/66da80e5715e10c08c6fafd5e9832137389a3355/testing/e2e/suites/oagw/test_body_validation.py#L30-L68) checks only `status == 400`, so it passes because of hyper. No test covers the body-read labelling.
- **Why this decision:** Reference docs must describe what the client gets on the wire, and over REST that is hyper's response. Reporting a client abort as "payload too large" is a plain code bug.
- **Impact:** Not breaking. Code: `to_bytes` errors other than the size limit (e.g. a client abort) must not become `PayloadTooLarge`. Docs: scenarios [7.4 A/B](https://github.com/constructorfabric/gears-rust/blob/66da80e5715e10c08c6fafd5e9832137389a3355/gears/system/oagw/scenarios/proxy-api/request-transforms/negative-7.4-well-known-header-validation-errors-400.md#L15-L35) and [8.2](https://github.com/constructorfabric/gears-rust/blob/66da80e5715e10c08c6fafd5e9832137389a3355/gears/system/oagw/scenarios/proxy-api/body-validation/negative-8.2-transfer-encoding-support-limited-chunked.md#L10-L20), DESIGN Body Validation Rules ([`DESIGN.md:514`](https://github.com/constructorfabric/gears-rust/blob/66da80e5715e10c08c6fafd5e9832137389a3355/gears/system/oagw/docs/DESIGN.md#L514)) and [`F0004:440`](https://github.com/constructorfabric/gears-rust/blob/66da80e5715e10c08c6fafd5e9832137389a3355/gears/system/oagw/docs/features/0004-cpt-cf-oagw-feature-proxy-engine.md#L440) say that over REST hyper rejects a bad CL or non-chunked TE with a bare 400 (no problem+json, no `X-OAGW-Error-Source`) before OAGW; OAGW's checks apply in-process, and the SDK path corrects a mismatched CL.

### P-06: An oversized body gets 400 `out_of_range` from OAGW, or a plain-text 413 from the api-gateway

- **Decision:** `FIX-CODE` (#4911) + `FIX-DOC`, P1. OAGW returns 413 through [PR #4911](https://github.com/constructorfabric/gears-rust/pull/4911); the docs state the effective limit.
- **What the docs say:**
  - [`PRD.md:450`](https://github.com/constructorfabric/gears-rust/blob/66da80e5715e10c08c6fafd5e9832137389a3355/gears/system/oagw/docs/PRD.md#L450)
    > | 413  | PayloadTooLarge      | No        |
  - [`F0004:441`](https://github.com/constructorfabric/gears-rust/blob/66da80e5715e10c08c6fafd5e9832137389a3355/gears/system/oagw/docs/features/0004-cpt-cf-oagw-feature-proxy-engine.md#L441)
    > - [x] 100MB body limit is enforced before buffering (413 PayloadTooLarge)
  - [scenario 8.1](https://github.com/constructorfabric/gears-rust/blob/66da80e5715e10c08c6fafd5e9832137389a3355/gears/system/oagw/scenarios/proxy-api/body-validation/negative-8.1-maximum-body-size-limit-enforced.md#L19-L21)
    > - `413 Payload Too Large` - `Content-Type: application/problem+json` - `X-OAGW-Error-Source: gateway`
  - [`INDEX:559-561`](https://github.com/constructorfabric/gears-rust/blob/66da80e5715e10c08c6fafd5e9832137389a3355/gears/system/oagw/scenarios/INDEX.md#L559-L561)
    > Maximum body size limit enforced (100MB) → 413 … Body > 100MB rejected early with `413 PD`, `ESrc=gateway`.
- **What the code does:**
  - [`error.rs:212-216`](https://github.com/constructorfabric/gears-rust/blob/66da80e5715e10c08c6fafd5e9832137389a3355/gears/system/oagw/oagw/src/api/rest/error.rs#L212-L216)

    ```rust
    DomainError::PayloadTooLarge { detail, .. } => {
        OagwProxyError::out_of_range(detail.clone())
    ```
    The canonical `out_of_range` category is sent as HTTP 400.
  - [`api-gateway gear.rs:532-534`](https://github.com/constructorfabric/gears-rust/blob/66da80e5715e10c08c6fafd5e9832137389a3355/gears/system/api-gateway/src/gear.rs#L532-L534): the api-gateway body limit (16 MiB by default, [`api-gateway config.rs:15-17`](https://github.com/constructorfabric/gears-rust/blob/66da80e5715e10c08c6fafd5e9832137389a3355/gears/system/api-gateway/src/config.rs#L15-L17); 64 MB in e2e, [`e2e-local.yaml:107-108`](https://github.com/constructorfabric/gears-rust/blob/66da80e5715e10c08c6fafd5e9832137389a3355/config/e2e-local.yaml#L107-L108)) answers first over REST, with a plain-text 413: no problem+json, no `X-OAGW-Error-Source`.
  - [`handlers/proxy.rs:152-159`](https://github.com/constructorfabric/gears-rust/blob/66da80e5715e10c08c6fafd5e9832137389a3355/gears/system/oagw/oagw/src/api/rest/handlers/proxy.rs#L152-L159): OAGW rejects on the declared Content-Length before reading the body. A client that keeps sending a large body can then see the connection reset instead of the error response; that case is handled under [U-2](#u-2-a-large-request-whose-upstream-answers-early-eg-413-before-reading-the-body-gets-a-gateway-503-instead-of-the-upstreams-response).
- **Evidence of intent:** e2e [`test_body_exceeding_limit_returns_413`](https://github.com/constructorfabric/gears-rust/blob/66da80e5715e10c08c6fafd5e9832137389a3355/testing/e2e/suites/oagw/test_body_validation.py#L73-L120) hits the api-gateway limit, not OAGW's. Its docstring is stale: [`test_body_validation.py:82-84`](https://github.com/constructorfabric/gears-rust/blob/66da80e5715e10c08c6fafd5e9832137389a3355/testing/e2e/suites/oagw/test_body_validation.py#L82-L84) “A request that hits oagw's own 100 MB body cap surfaces as a canonical-error 400 (`failed_precondition`)”, while the code uses `out_of_range`. The Rust test [`proxy_streaming_body_exceeding_limit_returns_400`](https://github.com/constructorfabric/gears-rust/blob/66da80e5715e10c08c6fafd5e9832137389a3355/gears/system/oagw/oagw/tests/proxy_integration.rs#L2249-L2334) asserts `CanonicalError::OutOfRange`.
- **Why this decision:** The PRD error-code table lists 413 PayloadTooLarge, so OAGW's 400 is the bug.
- **Impact:** Breaking for clients: an oversized body that reaches OAGW gets 413 instead of 400 `out_of_range` (via #4911). Docs: [`F0004:441`](https://github.com/constructorfabric/gears-rust/blob/66da80e5715e10c08c6fafd5e9832137389a3355/gears/system/oagw/docs/features/0004-cpt-cf-oagw-feature-proxy-engine.md#L441), [scenario 8.1](https://github.com/constructorfabric/gears-rust/blob/66da80e5715e10c08c6fafd5e9832137389a3355/gears/system/oagw/scenarios/proxy-api/body-validation/negative-8.1-maximum-body-size-limit-enforced.md#L19-L21) and [`INDEX:559`](https://github.com/constructorfabric/gears-rust/blob/66da80e5715e10c08c6fafd5e9832137389a3355/gears/system/oagw/scenarios/INDEX.md#L559) state the effective limit, `min(api-gateway body limit, OAGW max_body_size)`, and that the api-gateway 413 is plain text with no `X-OAGW-Error-Source`. Fix the [`test_body_validation.py:82-84`](https://github.com/constructorfabric/gears-rust/blob/66da80e5715e10c08c6fafd5e9832137389a3355/testing/e2e/suites/oagw/test_body_validation.py#L82-L84) docstring and rename the Rust test `..._returns_400` ([`proxy_integration.rs:2251`](https://github.com/constructorfabric/gears-rust/blob/66da80e5715e10c08c6fafd5e9832137389a3355/gears/system/oagw/oagw/tests/proxy_integration.rs#L2251)).
- **Decided together with:** [P-08](#p-08-unreachable-tls-failed-and-http-only-violating-upstreams-get-different-statuses-than-documented) (both through PR #4911), [U-2](#u-2-a-large-request-whose-upstream-answers-early-eg-413-before-reading-the-body-gets-a-gateway-503-instead-of-the-upstreams-response)

### P-07: Error `type` IDs, field names and resource identifiers don't match what OAGW sends

- **Decision:** `FIX-DOC` + `FIX-CODE`, P2 (code). Doc part: the error tables use canonical `cf.core.err.*` types plus reason codes. Code part: fix the field name and resource identifiers.
- **What the docs say** (every location, with its quote and edit, is in the `error-type-ids` batch under [Mechanical doc fixes](#mechanical-doc-fixes)):
  - [`DESIGN.md:723`](https://github.com/constructorfabric/gears-rust/blob/66da80e5715e10c08c6fafd5e9832137389a3355/gears/system/oagw/docs/DESIGN.md#L723)
    > | RateLimitExceeded | 429 | `gts.cf.core.errors.err.v1~cf.oagw.rate_limit.exceeded.v1` | Yes | Rate limit exceeded |
  - [`F0004:307`](https://github.com/constructorfabric/gears-rust/blob/66da80e5715e10c08c6fafd5e9832137389a3355/gears/system/oagw/docs/features/0004-cpt-cf-oagw-feature-proxy-engine.md#L307)
    > Include extension fields where applicable: `upstream_id`, `host`, `path`, `retry_after_seconds`, `trace_id`
  - [custom-header-routing scenario negative-2.1](https://github.com/constructorfabric/gears-rust/blob/66da80e5715e10c08c6fafd5e9832137389a3355/gears/system/oagw/scenarios/proxy-api/custom-header-routing/negative-2.1-unknown-host.md#L32-L39)
    > "type": "gts.cf.core.errors.err.v1~cf.oagw.routing.unknown_target_host.v1", … "valid_hosts": ["us.vendor.com", "eu.vendor.com"]
- **What the code does:**
  - [`toolkit-canonical-errors error.rs:570-604`](https://github.com/constructorfabric/gears-rust/blob/66da80e5715e10c08c6fafd5e9832137389a3355/libs/toolkit-canonical-errors/src/error.rs#L570-L604): wire types are canonical, e.g. `cf.core.errors.err.v1~cf.core.err.invalid_argument.v1~`; OAGW maps every `DomainError` to one of them ([`error.rs:127-345`](https://github.com/constructorfabric/gears-rust/blob/66da80e5715e10c08c6fafd5e9832137389a3355/gears/system/oagw/oagw/src/api/rest/error.rs#L127-L345)).
  - [`error.rs:170-176`](https://github.com/constructorfabric/gears-rust/blob/66da80e5715e10c08c6fafd5e9832137389a3355/gears/system/oagw/oagw/src/api/rest/error.rs#L170-L176)

    ```rust
    DomainError::MissingTargetHost { .. } => OagwProxyError::invalid_argument()
        .with_field_violation(
            "x-target-host",
    ```
    The real header is `X-OAGW-Target-Host`. The same wrong name is in [`error.rs:178-188`](https://github.com/constructorfabric/gears-rust/blob/66da80e5715e10c08c6fafd5e9832137389a3355/gears/system/oagw/oagw/src/api/rest/error.rs#L178-L188) and in the SDK constants' docs ([`field.rs:33-44`](https://github.com/constructorfabric/gears-rust/blob/66da80e5715e10c08c6fafd5e9832137389a3355/gears/system/oagw/oagw-sdk/src/field.rs#L33-L44)).
  - [`resolution.rs:92`](https://github.com/constructorfabric/gears-rust/blob/66da80e5715e10c08c6fafd5e9832137389a3355/gears/system/oagw/oagw/src/domain/services/management/resolution.rs#L92) and [`resolution.rs:142`](https://github.com/constructorfabric/gears-rust/blob/66da80e5715e10c08c6fafd5e9832137389a3355/gears/system/oagw/oagw/src/domain/services/management/resolution.rs#L142): not-found uses a nil UUID, which becomes the wire `resource_name` ([`error.rs:200-210`](https://github.com/constructorfabric/gears-rust/blob/66da80e5715e10c08c6fafd5e9832137389a3355/gears/system/oagw/oagw/src/api/rest/error.rs#L200-L210)) instead of the alias the client sent.
  - [`error.rs:130-149`](https://github.com/constructorfabric/gears-rust/blob/66da80e5715e10c08c6fafd5e9832137389a3355/gears/system/oagw/oagw/src/api/rest/error.rs#L130-L149): validation errors always use the proxy scope, so the wire `resource_type` is the proxy even for upstream or route payloads.
  - [`service.rs:298-305`](https://github.com/constructorfabric/gears-rust/blob/66da80e5715e10c08c6fafd5e9832137389a3355/gears/system/oagw/oagw/src/infra/proxy/service.rs#L298-L305): the list of valid hosts appears only in a warn log.
- **Evidence of intent:** The [canonical-errors migration](#why-the-docs-drifted) ([`10d7e329c`](https://github.com/constructorfabric/gears-rust/commit/10d7e329c40e69c5421c1bf0dbdfdc250f49626e)) changed no docs. Tests assert canonical types: e2e [`test_authz.py:88-91`](https://github.com/constructorfabric/gears-rust/blob/66da80e5715e10c08c6fafd5e9832137389a3355/testing/e2e/suites/oagw/test_authz.py#L88-L91) (`cf.core.err.permission_denied`) and Rust [`get_upstream_invalid_gts_returns_400`](https://github.com/constructorfabric/gears-rust/blob/66da80e5715e10c08c6fafd5e9832137389a3355/gears/system/oagw/oagw/tests/management_api_test.rs#L88-L104) (`cf.core.err.invalid_argument`).
- **Why this decision:** Error tables are a reference contract and must match the wire. The code part makes the wire name the header and resource the client actually used.
- **Impact:** Breaking for clients (code part): a client that reads the field violation sees `x-oagw-target-host` instead of `x-target-host`; a not-found `resource_name` becomes the alias instead of the nil UUID; a validation `resource_type` becomes upstream or route. Valid hosts stay in the log only. Doc part: ADR-0006's error section gets only a deferred note ([PLG-13](#plg-13-route-level-cors-is-ignored-unless-sharing-inherit-and-a-descendants-own-private-or-enforce-cors-is-dropped)). The `x-target-host` name used by the streaming tests is fixed here ([STR-14](#str-14-streaming-tests-pass-without-checking-the-behaviour-they-are-named-after)).
- **Decided together with:** [PLG-13](#plg-13-route-level-cors-is-ignored-unless-sharing-inherit-and-a-descendants-own-private-or-enforce-cors-is-dropped), [STR-14](#str-14-streaming-tests-pass-without-checking-the-behaviour-they-are-named-after)

### P-08: Unreachable, TLS-failed and HTTP-only-violating upstreams get different statuses than documented

- **Decision:** `FIX-CODE` (#4911) + `FIX-CODE`, P1 (#4911) / P2 (HTTPS). Network, TLS and protocol failures return 502 through [PR #4911](https://github.com/constructorfabric/gears-rust/pull/4911). HTTPS-only is enforced at write time. The other rows below are handled in other items.
- **What the docs say:**
  - [`PRD.md:453`](https://github.com/constructorfabric/gears-rust/blob/66da80e5715e10c08c6fafd5e9832137389a3355/gears/system/oagw/docs/PRD.md#L453)
    > | 502  | DownstreamError      | Depends   |
  - [`F0004:141-143`](https://github.com/constructorfabric/gears-rust/blob/66da80e5715e10c08c6fafd5e9832137389a3355/gears/system/oagw/docs/features/0004-cpt-cf-oagw-feature-proxy-engine.md#L141-L143)
    > 27. [x] - `p1` - **IF** Pingora reports upstream connection failure (refused, DNS, TLS) via `fail_to_proxy` - `inst-proxy-27` … **RETURN** 502 DownstreamError with `X-OAGW-Error-Source: gateway` - `inst-proxy-27b`
  - [`F0007:212-213`](https://github.com/constructorfabric/gears-rust/blob/66da80e5715e10c08c6fafd5e9832137389a3355/gears/system/oagw/docs/features/0007-cpt-cf-oagw-feature-streaming.md#L212-L213)
    > 6. [x] - `p1` - **IF** TLS handshake fails entirely - `inst-proto-6` … **RETURN** error: connection failed (502 DownstreamError) - `inst-proto-6a`
  - [`DESIGN.md:176`](https://github.com/constructorfabric/gears-rust/blob/66da80e5715e10c08c6fafd5e9832137389a3355/gears/system/oagw/docs/DESIGN.md#L176)
    > HTTPS-only for upstream connections in MVP. SSRF prevention; plaintext upstreams explicitly blocked.
- **What the code does:**
  - [`error.rs:243-248`](https://github.com/constructorfabric/gears-rust/blob/66da80e5715e10c08c6fafd5e9832137389a3355/gears/system/oagw/oagw/src/api/rest/error.rs#L243-L248)

    ```rust
    DomainError::DownstreamError { detail, .. } => {
        tracing::warn!(reason = %detail, "OAGW downstream error");
        CanonicalError::service_unavailable()
    ```
    Connection refused, TLS handshake failure and other network errors ([`pingora_proxy.rs:826-854`](https://github.com/constructorfabric/gears-rust/blob/66da80e5715e10c08c6fafd5e9832137389a3355/gears/system/oagw/oagw/src/infra/proxy/pingora_proxy.rs#L826-L854)) become `LinkUnavailable` or `DownstreamError`, both sent as 503.
  - [`service.rs:750-758`](https://github.com/constructorfabric/gears-rust/blob/66da80e5715e10c08c6fafd5e9832137389a3355/gears/system/oagw/oagw/src/infra/proxy/service.rs#L750-L758): an `http` endpoint is rejected with 400 `invalid_argument` only at proxy time, after the auth plugin has run; creating the upstream succeeds.
  - Other rows, decided elsewhere:

    | Case | Documented | Actual | Decided in |
    |---|---|---|---|
    | Disabled upstream | [`F0004:114`](https://github.com/constructorfabric/gears-rust/blob/66da80e5715e10c08c6fafd5e9832137389a3355/gears/system/oagw/docs/features/0004-cpt-cf-oagw-feature-proxy-engine.md#L114) “**RETURN** 503 LinkUnavailable” | generic 503 `service_unavailable` ([`error.rs:257-262`](https://github.com/constructorfabric/gears-rust/blob/66da80e5715e10c08c6fafd5e9832137389a3355/gears/system/oagw/oagw/src/api/rest/error.rs#L257-L262)) | type name: canonical-errors doc batch ([P-07](#p-07-error-type-ids-field-names-and-resource-identifiers-dont-match-what-oagw-sends)) |
    | Unknown plugin id | [`F0004:220`](https://github.com/constructorfabric/gears-rust/blob/66da80e5715e10c08c6fafd5e9832137389a3355/gears/system/oagw/docs/features/0004-cpt-cf-oagw-feature-proxy-engine.md#L220) “**RETURN** 503 PluginNotFound” | auth → 401, guard → 500, transform silently skipped (200) ([`service.rs:1209-1215`](https://github.com/constructorfabric/gears-rust/blob/66da80e5715e10c08c6fafd5e9832137389a3355/gears/system/oagw/oagw/src/infra/proxy/service.rs#L1209-L1215), [`1272-1280`](https://github.com/constructorfabric/gears-rust/blob/66da80e5715e10c08c6fafd5e9832137389a3355/gears/system/oagw/oagw/src/infra/proxy/service.rs#L1272-L1280), [`1361-1368`](https://github.com/constructorfabric/gears-rust/blob/66da80e5715e10c08c6fafd5e9832137389a3355/gears/system/oagw/oagw/src/infra/proxy/service.rs#L1361-L1368)) | [PLG-03](#plg-03-an-unknown-plugin-id-gives-401-auth-500-guard-or-is-silently-skipped-transform-not-the-documented-503) |
    | Upstream error response | [`F0004:93`](https://github.com/constructorfabric/gears-rust/blob/66da80e5715e10c08c6fafd5e9832137389a3355/gears/system/oagw/docs/features/0004-cpt-cf-oagw-feature-proxy-engine.md#L93) “502 DownstreamError passthrough” | passed through as-is | [U-1](#other-doc-only-fixes-proxy) |
    | SSRF DNS block | [`F0008:249`](https://github.com/constructorfabric/gears-rust/blob/66da80e5715e10c08c6fafd5e9832137389a3355/gears/system/oagw/docs/features/0008-cpt-cf-oagw-feature-observability.md#L249) “**RETURN** reject with 400 ValidationError” | 503 "DNS returned no usable addresses" ([`pingora_proxy.rs:606-635`](https://github.com/constructorfabric/gears-rust/blob/66da80e5715e10c08c6fafd5e9832137389a3355/gears/system/oagw/oagw/src/infra/proxy/pingora_proxy.rs#L606-L635)) | [P-19](#p-19-x-forwarded--and-x-real-ip-reach-the-upstream-under-passthrough-all) |
- **Evidence of intent:** Rust [`proxy_unreachable_backend_returns_rfc9457_problem_body`](https://github.com/constructorfabric/gears-rust/blob/66da80e5715e10c08c6fafd5e9832137389a3355/gears/system/oagw/oagw/tests/proxy_integration.rs#L2141-L2247) accepts either code today:
  - [`proxy_integration.rs:2214-2215`](https://github.com/constructorfabric/gears-rust/blob/66da80e5715e10c08c6fafd5e9832137389a3355/gears/system/oagw/oagw/tests/proxy_integration.rs#L2214-L2215)

    ```rust
    status == StatusCode::SERVICE_UNAVAILABLE || status == StatusCode::GATEWAY_TIMEOUT,
    "expected 503 or 504, got: {status}"
    ```
- **Why this decision:** The PRD error-code table lists 502 DownstreamError, and HTTPS-only is a DESIGN security constraint; the code is wrong in both cases.
- **Impact:** Breaking for clients: network, TLS and protocol failures change from 503 to 502 (via #4911). With `allow_http_upstream=false`, creating or updating an upstream with an `http` endpoint fails with 400 (today it is accepted and fails at proxy time). The proxy-time check stays but moves before the auth plugin. The `LinkUnavailable` type name is fixed in the canonical-errors doc batch; PluginNotFound is [PLG-03](#plg-03-an-unknown-plugin-id-gives-401-auth-500-guard-or-is-silently-skipped-transform-not-the-documented-503); the SSRF DNS block is [P-19](#p-19-x-forwarded--and-x-real-ip-reach-the-upstream-under-passthrough-all).
- **Decided together with:** [P-06](#p-06-an-oversized-body-gets-400-out_of_range-from-oagw-or-a-plain-text-413-from-the-api-gateway) (both through PR #4911)

### P-09: `Retry-After` is sent only on 429

- **Decision:** `FIX-CODE`, P2. Send the `Retry-After` header wherever the body carries `retry_after_seconds`.
- **What the docs say:**
  - [`INDEX:753-755`](https://github.com/constructorfabric/gears-rust/blob/66da80e5715e10c08c6fafd5e9832137389a3355/gears/system/oagw/scenarios/INDEX.md#L753-L755)
    > Retry-After present for retriable gateway errors … `429`, `503 link unavailable`, `503 circuit breaker open`, `504` timeouts include `Retry-After`.
- **What the code does:**
  - [`error.rs:522-526`](https://github.com/constructorfabric/gears-rust/blob/66da80e5715e10c08c6fafd5e9832137389a3355/gears/system/oagw/oagw/src/api/rest/error.rs#L522-L526)

    ```rust
    if let Some((retry_after_secs, limit, remaining, reset_epoch)) = rate_limit_meta {
        if let Some(secs) = retry_after_secs
            && let Ok(v) = secs.to_string().parse()
        {
            response.headers_mut().insert("retry-after", v);
    ```
    `rate_limit_meta` is set only for `RateLimitExceeded` ([`error.rs:495-504`](https://github.com/constructorfabric/gears-rust/blob/66da80e5715e10c08c6fafd5e9832137389a3355/gears/system/oagw/oagw/src/api/rest/error.rs#L495-L504)).
  - 503s put `retry_after_seconds` only in the body ([`error.rs:243-262`](https://github.com/constructorfabric/gears-rust/blob/66da80e5715e10c08c6fafd5e9832137389a3355/gears/system/oagw/oagw/src/api/rest/error.rs#L243-L262), [`error.rs:308-320`](https://github.com/constructorfabric/gears-rust/blob/66da80e5715e10c08c6fafd5e9832137389a3355/gears/system/oagw/oagw/src/api/rest/error.rs#L308-L320)).
  - 504s (`deadline_exceeded`, [`error.rs:264-270`](https://github.com/constructorfabric/gears-rust/blob/66da80e5715e10c08c6fafd5e9832137389a3355/gears/system/oagw/oagw/src/api/rest/error.rs#L264-L270)) carry neither. `fail_to_proxy` responses set only `content-type` and `x-oagw-error-source` ([`pingora_proxy.rs:867-871`](https://github.com/constructorfabric/gears-rust/blob/66da80e5715e10c08c6fafd5e9832137389a3355/gears/system/oagw/oagw/src/infra/proxy/pingora_proxy.rs#L867-L871)).
- **Evidence of intent:** none. Tests check `Retry-After` on 429 only (e.g. [`e2e_smoke_test.rs:891-894`](https://github.com/constructorfabric/gears-rust/blob/66da80e5715e10c08c6fafd5e9832137389a3355/gears/system/oagw/oagw/tests/e2e_smoke_test.rs#L891-L894)).
- **Why this decision:** INDEX is the only document on this, and the code already computes the retry hint; the standard header is where clients look for it.
- **Impact:** Not breaking (adds a header). Clients that honour `Retry-After` start backing off on 503. Cover 503 link-unavailable, transient and admin cases and `fail_to_proxy` responses; 504 only if a retry hint applies. Source: [`INDEX:753-755`](https://github.com/constructorfabric/gears-rust/blob/66da80e5715e10c08c6fafd5e9832137389a3355/gears/system/oagw/scenarios/INDEX.md#L753-L755).

### P-10: Rate limiting runs last, after credential lookups, token fetches and guards

- **Decision:** `FIX-CODE` + `FIX-DOC`, P2. Move rate limiting before the auth plugin (right after resolve/CORS), and document the rest of the actual order.
- **What the docs say:**
  - [`ADR-0003:100-110`](https://github.com/constructorfabric/gears-rust/blob/66da80e5715e10c08c6fafd5e9832137389a3355/gears/system/oagw/docs/ADR/0003-plugin-system.md#L100-L110)
    > Incoming Request → Auth Plugin (credential injection) → Guard Plugins (validation, can reject) → Transform Plugins (modify request) → HTTP call to external service
  - [`ADR-0011:278`](https://github.com/constructorfabric/gears-rust/blob/66da80e5715e10c08c6fafd5e9832137389a3355/gears/system/oagw/docs/ADR/0011-concurrency-control.md#L278)
    > **Order**: Check rate limit first (cheaper, rejects quota violations early).
  - [`flows/proxy-operations.md:66-71`](https://github.com/constructorfabric/gears-rust/blob/66da80e5715e10c08c6fafd5e9832137389a3355/gears/system/oagw/scenarios/flows/proxy-operations.md#L66-L71)
    > → Validate Bearer token → 401 if invalid → Check rate limit → 429 if exceeded → Route to Data Plane
  - [`F0004:128-129`](https://github.com/constructorfabric/gears-rust/blob/66da80e5715e10c08c6fafd5e9832137389a3355/gears/system/oagw/docs/features/0004-cpt-cf-oagw-feature-proxy-engine.md#L128-L129)
    > 18. [x] - `p1` - Execute transform plugins: `on_request` phase — mutate outbound request - `inst-proxy-18` … Apply header transformation via `cpt-cf-oagw-algo-header-transformation` - `inst-proxy-19`
  - [scenario 11.6](https://github.com/constructorfabric/gears-rust/blob/66da80e5715e10c08c6fafd5e9832137389a3355/gears/system/oagw/scenarios/plugins/transforms/positive-11.6-plugin-ordering-layering.md#L23-L28)
    > Execution order: - Auth plugin - Upstream guards - Upstream transforms - Route guards - Route transforms
- **What the code does** ([`service.rs`](https://github.com/constructorfabric/gears-rust/blob/66da80e5715e10c08c6fafd5e9832137389a3355/gears/system/oagw/oagw/src/infra/proxy/service.rs), in this order):
  1. parse and validate ([`545`](https://github.com/constructorfabric/gears-rust/blob/66da80e5715e10c08c6fafd5e9832137389a3355/gears/system/oagw/oagw/src/infra/proxy/service.rs#L545)), authz ([`547`](https://github.com/constructorfabric/gears-rust/blob/66da80e5715e10c08c6fafd5e9832137389a3355/gears/system/oagw/oagw/src/infra/proxy/service.rs#L547-L557)), resolve upstream and route ([`562`](https://github.com/constructorfabric/gears-rust/blob/66da80e5715e10c08c6fafd5e9832137389a3355/gears/system/oagw/oagw/src/infra/proxy/service.rs#L562-L565))
  2. CORS ([`579-592`](https://github.com/constructorfabric/gears-rust/blob/66da80e5715e10c08c6fafd5e9832137389a3355/gears/system/oagw/oagw/src/infra/proxy/service.rs#L579-L592)), query allowlist ([`594-611`](https://github.com/constructorfabric/gears-rust/blob/66da80e5715e10c08c6fafd5e9832137389a3355/gears/system/oagw/oagw/src/infra/proxy/service.rs#L594-L611)), suffix mode ([`613-630`](https://github.com/constructorfabric/gears-rust/blob/66da80e5715e10c08c6fafd5e9832137389a3355/gears/system/oagw/oagw/src/infra/proxy/service.rs#L613-L630)), passthrough and header stripping ([`632-649`](https://github.com/constructorfabric/gears-rust/blob/66da80e5715e10c08c6fafd5e9832137389a3355/gears/system/oagw/oagw/src/infra/proxy/service.rs#L632-L649))
  3. auth plugin ([`677`](https://github.com/constructorfabric/gears-rust/blob/66da80e5715e10c08c6fafd5e9832137389a3355/gears/system/oagw/oagw/src/infra/proxy/service.rs#L677-L694)), all guards ([`705`](https://github.com/constructorfabric/gears-rust/blob/66da80e5715e10c08c6fafd5e9832137389a3355/gears/system/oagw/oagw/src/infra/proxy/service.rs#L702-L714)), header rules ([`721-725`](https://github.com/constructorfabric/gears-rust/blob/66da80e5715e10c08c6fafd5e9832137389a3355/gears/system/oagw/oagw/src/infra/proxy/service.rs#L721-L725)), all transforms ([`733`](https://github.com/constructorfabric/gears-rust/blob/66da80e5715e10c08c6fafd5e9832137389a3355/gears/system/oagw/oagw/src/infra/proxy/service.rs#L733-L742))
  4. endpoint selection ([`745`](https://github.com/constructorfabric/gears-rust/blob/66da80e5715e10c08c6fafd5e9832137389a3355/gears/system/oagw/oagw/src/infra/proxy/service.rs#L744-L748)), HTTPS-only ([`751`](https://github.com/constructorfabric/gears-rust/blob/66da80e5715e10c08c6fafd5e9832137389a3355/gears/system/oagw/oagw/src/infra/proxy/service.rs#L750-L758)), Host ([`760`](https://github.com/constructorfabric/gears-rust/blob/66da80e5715e10c08c6fafd5e9832137389a3355/gears/system/oagw/oagw/src/infra/proxy/service.rs#L760)), and last, **rate limit** ([`762-773`](https://github.com/constructorfabric/gears-rust/blob/66da80e5715e10c08c6fafd5e9832137389a3355/gears/system/oagw/oagw/src/infra/proxy/service.rs#L762-L773))

  So credstore lookups, OAuth2 token fetches and guards all run for requests that are then rate-limited. Header rules before transforms is deliberate:
  - [`service.rs:729-730`](https://github.com/constructorfabric/gears-rust/blob/66da80e5715e10c08c6fafd5e9832137389a3355/gears/system/oagw/oagw/src/infra/proxy/service.rs#L729-L730)

    ```rust
    // Placed after header rules so transforms have the final word on
    // outbound headers.
    ```
  - [`service.rs:219-237`](https://github.com/constructorfabric/gears-rust/blob/66da80e5715e10c08c6fafd5e9832137389a3355/gears/system/oagw/oagw/src/infra/proxy/service.rs#L219-L237): `finalize_response` also runs guards on the upstream response. No doc mentions response-phase guards.
- **Evidence of intent:** The order comes from deliberate code changes ([`ed93ce7bc`](https://github.com/constructorfabric/gears-rust/commit/ed93ce7bcfded1a94d29e4edd35eb3c6c6790752) pingora-based proxy, [`bdcf33e55`](https://github.com/constructorfabric/gears-rust/commit/bdcf33e55b2d4d09ace35b613c7f4c1cb953b7cd) guard and transform plugins, [`b017db121`](https://github.com/constructorfabric/gears-rust/commit/b017db121b13068c54f0fb37ebc7d0202b4613a7) split `proxy_request`); the docs were never updated.
- **Why this decision:** ADR-0003 (accepted) fixes only the plugin order (auth → guards → transforms), which the code keeps. Checking the rate limit first, as ADR-0011 and the flow doc say, stops over-limit calls from spending credstore, OAuth2 and guard work. The rest of the code order is deliberate, so the docs follow it.
- **Impact:** Breaking for clients (minor): an over-limit request gets 429 even when the auth plugin or a guard would have rejected it, and requests later rejected by the auth plugin or a guard now use up rate-limit budget. Code: move `check_rate_limits` ([`service.rs:762-773`](https://github.com/constructorfabric/gears-rust/blob/66da80e5715e10c08c6fafd5e9832137389a3355/gears/system/oagw/oagw/src/infra/proxy/service.rs#L762-L773)). Docs: [`ADR-0003:100-110`](https://github.com/constructorfabric/gears-rust/blob/66da80e5715e10c08c6fafd5e9832137389a3355/gears/system/oagw/docs/ADR/0003-plugin-system.md#L100-L110), [`flows/plugin-execution.md:5`](https://github.com/constructorfabric/gears-rust/blob/66da80e5715e10c08c6fafd5e9832137389a3355/gears/system/oagw/scenarios/flows/plugin-execution.md#L5) “Three plugin types execute in strict order: Auth → Guards → Transform → Proxy Call → Transform.”, [`flows/proxy-operations.md:11`](https://github.com/constructorfabric/gears-rust/blob/66da80e5715e10c08c6fafd5e9832137389a3355/gears/system/oagw/scenarios/flows/proxy-operations.md#L11) “→ API Handler (auth, rate limit)” and [`:70`](https://github.com/constructorfabric/gears-rust/blob/66da80e5715e10c08c6fafd5e9832137389a3355/gears/system/oagw/scenarios/flows/proxy-operations.md#L70), F0004 inst-proxy-11 [`F0004:118`](https://github.com/constructorfabric/gears-rust/blob/66da80e5715e10c08c6fafd5e9832137389a3355/gears/system/oagw/docs/features/0004-cpt-cf-oagw-feature-proxy-engine.md#L118) “Validate request body via `cpt-cf-oagw-algo-body-validation`”, inst-proxy-16 [`F0004:125`](https://github.com/constructorfabric/gears-rust/blob/66da80e5715e10c08c6fafd5e9832137389a3355/gears/system/oagw/docs/features/0004-cpt-cf-oagw-feature-proxy-engine.md#L125) “Execute guard plugins: validate method, query params, path suffix, CORS origin”, inst-proxy-18/19 [`F0004:128-129`](https://github.com/constructorfabric/gears-rust/blob/66da80e5715e10c08c6fafd5e9832137389a3355/gears/system/oagw/docs/features/0004-cpt-cf-oagw-feature-proxy-engine.md#L128-L129), [scenario 11.6](https://github.com/constructorfabric/gears-rust/blob/66da80e5715e10c08c6fafd5e9832137389a3355/gears/system/oagw/scenarios/plugins/transforms/positive-11.6-plugin-ordering-layering.md#L23-L28) (all guards, then header rules, then all transforms); document response-phase guards.

### P-11: With the default passthrough, the upstream never sees the client's `X-Request-ID`, `Accept` or trace headers

- **Decision:** `FIX-CODE`, P2. Always forward a safe header allowlist (`Accept`, `X-Request-ID`, `traceparent`, `tracestate`), even under `passthrough: none`.
- **What the docs say:**
  - [scenario 7.6](https://github.com/constructorfabric/gears-rust/blob/66da80e5715e10c08c6fafd5e9832137389a3355/gears/system/oagw/scenarios/proxy-api/request-transforms/positive-7.6-request-correlation-headers-propagate-end-end.md#L3-L14)
    > Scenario A: client provides X-Request-ID … Upstream receives `X-Request-ID: req-abc-123`.
  - [scenario 12.1](https://github.com/constructorfabric/gears-rust/blob/66da80e5715e10c08c6fafd5e9832137389a3355/gears/system/oagw/scenarios/protocols/http/positive-12.1-plain-http-request-response-passthrough.md#L17-L23)
    > Expected outbound request … X-Request-ID: req-http-001
  - [SSE example 02](https://github.com/constructorfabric/gears-rust/blob/66da80e5715e10c08c6fafd5e9832137389a3355/gears/system/oagw/scenarios/protocols/sse/examples/positive-example-02-sse-streaming.md#L61-L68)
    > Outbound Request (to upstream) … Accept: text/event-stream X-Request-ID: req-sse-001
  - [`flows/proxy-operations.md:147-161`](https://github.com/constructorfabric/gears-rust/blob/66da80e5715e10c08c6fafd5e9832137389a3355/gears/system/oagw/scenarios/flows/proxy-operations.md#L147-L161)
    > Execute transform plugins (pre-request): → Add X-Request-ID header
- **What the code does:**
  - [`service.rs:633-637`](https://github.com/constructorfabric/gears-rust/blob/66da80e5715e10c08c6fafd5e9832137389a3355/gears/system/oagw/oagw/src/infra/proxy/service.rs#L633-L637): with no `headers.request` config, the mode is `PassthroughMode::None`.
  - [`headers.rs:27-28`](https://github.com/constructorfabric/gears-rust/blob/66da80e5715e10c08c6fafd5e9832137389a3355/gears/system/oagw/oagw/src/infra/proxy/headers.rs#L27-L28)

    ```rust
    let mut out = match mode {
        PassthroughMode::None => HeaderMap::new(),
    ```
    Only Content-Type is added back ([`headers.rs:43-48`](https://github.com/constructorfabric/gears-rust/blob/66da80e5715e10c08c6fafd5e9832137389a3355/gears/system/oagw/oagw/src/infra/proxy/headers.rs#L43-L48)). SSE upstreams that need `Accept: text/event-stream` don't get it.
  - [`request_id_transform.rs:18-27`](https://github.com/constructorfabric/gears-rust/blob/66da80e5715e10c08c6fafd5e9832137389a3355/gears/system/oagw/oagw/src/infra/plugin/request_id_transform.rs#L18-L27): the `request_id` plugin sees the filtered outbound headers ([`service.rs:733-742`](https://github.com/constructorfabric/gears-rust/blob/66da80e5715e10c08c6fafd5e9832137389a3355/gears/system/oagw/oagw/src/infra/proxy/service.rs#L733-L742)), so under `none` it generates a *new* UUID even when the client sent one.
  - The response-side id comes from the api-gateway's `PropagateRequestIdLayer` ([`api-gateway gear.rs:632`](https://github.com/constructorfabric/gears-rust/blob/66da80e5715e10c08c6fafd5e9832137389a3355/gears/system/api-gateway/src/gear.rs#L632)), not from OAGW.
- **Evidence of intent:** Rust [`proxy_transform_preserves_request_id`](https://github.com/constructorfabric/gears-rust/blob/66da80e5715e10c08c6fafd5e9832137389a3355/gears/system/oagw/oagw/tests/proxy_integration.rs#L3291-L3391) and e2e [`test_request_id_transform_preserves_existing`](https://github.com/constructorfabric/gears-rust/blob/66da80e5715e10c08c6fafd5e9832137389a3355/testing/e2e/suites/oagw/test_transform_plugins.py#L81-L120) both switch to `passthrough: all`, which hides the problem:
  - [`proxy_integration.rs:3324-3326`](https://github.com/constructorfabric/gears-rust/blob/66da80e5715e10c08c6fafd5e9832137389a3355/gears/system/oagw/oagw/tests/proxy_integration.rs#L3324-L3326)

    ```rust
    .headers(HeadersConfig {
        request: Some(RequestHeaderRules {
            passthrough: PassthroughMode::All,
    ```
- **Why this decision:** The scenarios describe end-to-end correlation as client-visible behaviour, and trace propagation ([O-04](#o-04-no-audit-log-config-change-log-or-log-sampling-and-the-trace-breaks-at-oagw)) needs the same headers. None of the four carries credentials.
- **Impact:** Not breaking for clients. Under the default passthrough, upstreams start receiving `Accept`, `X-Request-ID`, `traceparent` and `tracestate`. The request_id plugin reuses the inbound id (same root cause as [PLG-05](#plg-05-guards-check-the-gateways-outbound-headers-after-passthrough-filtering-and-auth-not-the-headers-the-client-sent)). Fix `proxy_transform_preserves_request_id` and `test_request_id_transform_preserves_existing` to use the default passthrough.
- **Decided together with:** [O-04](#o-04-no-audit-log-config-change-log-or-log-sampling-and-the-trace-breaks-at-oagw), [PLG-05](#plg-05-guards-check-the-gateways-outbound-headers-after-passthrough-filtering-and-auth-not-the-headers-the-client-sent)

### P-12: Several proxy tests pass whether or not the behaviour works

- **Decision:** `FIX-DOC` (tests), no priority. Strengthen or replace the vacuous proxy tests.
- **What the docs say:**
  - [scenario 7.2](https://github.com/constructorfabric/gears-rust/blob/66da80e5715e10c08c6fafd5e9832137389a3355/gears/system/oagw/scenarios/proxy-api/request-transforms/positive-7.2-hop-hop-headers-stripped.md#L9-L22)
    > Connection: keep-alive, Upgrade Upgrade: websocket TE: trailers Transfer-Encoding: chunked … Gateway strips hop-by-hop headers when building outbound request
- **What the code does:**
  - [`test_proxy_round_trip.py:82-84`](https://github.com/constructorfabric/gears-rust/blob/66da80e5715e10c08c6fafd5e9832137389a3355/testing/e2e/suites/oagw/test_proxy_round_trip.py#L82-L84): `test_hop_by_hop_headers_stripped` creates the upstream without `headers`, so the default passthrough `none` drops every client header anyway. The test would pass with stripping removed.

    ```python
    upstream = await create_upstream(
        client, oagw_base_url, oagw_headers, mock_upstream_url, alias=alias,
    )
    ```
  - [`test_proxy_round_trip.py:151-158`](https://github.com/constructorfabric/gears-rust/blob/66da80e5715e10c08c6fafd5e9832137389a3355/testing/e2e/suites/oagw/test_proxy_round_trip.py#L151-L158): `test_host_header_replaced` only asserts that Host is not OAGW's own host.
  - Scenario 7.2's request is now a WebSocket upgrade (`Upgrade: websocket` plus `Connection: … Upgrade`, [`headers.rs:123-140`](https://github.com/constructorfabric/gears-rust/blob/66da80e5715e10c08c6fafd5e9832137389a3355/gears/system/oagw/oagw/src/infra/proxy/headers.rs#L123-L140)) that carries `Transfer-Encoding`, so OAGW rejects it with 400 `WS_UPGRADE_BODY_FORBIDDEN` ([`handlers/proxy.rs:93-102`](https://github.com/constructorfabric/gears-rust/blob/66da80e5715e10c08c6fafd5e9832137389a3355/gears/system/oagw/oagw/src/api/rest/handlers/proxy.rs#L93-L102)).
  - [`test_error_handling.py:110-125`](https://github.com/constructorfabric/gears-rust/blob/66da80e5715e10c08c6fafd5e9832137389a3355/testing/e2e/suites/oagw/test_error_handling.py#L110-L125): `test_upstream_timeout_returns_504_gateway` skips on `ReadTimeout` and accepts any other status.

    ```python
    except httpx.ReadTimeout:
    # …
        else:
            # Accept any response but log it.
            pass
    ```
  - [`proxy_tests.rs:2-13`](https://github.com/constructorfabric/gears-rust/blob/66da80e5715e10c08c6fafd5e9832137389a3355/gears/system/oagw/oagw/src/api/rest/handlers/proxy_tests.rs#L2-L13): re-implements the handler's alias parsing instead of calling `proxy_handler`.
- **Evidence of intent:** none.
- **Why this decision:** Tests are evidence, not authority; these give false confidence for scenarios 7.2 and 7.3 and for the timeout path.
- **Impact:** Not breaking. Follow-ups: run `test_hop_by_hop_headers_stripped` with `passthrough: all`; make `test_host_header_replaced` check the upstream host; rewrite scenario 7.2's request (a WS upgrade → 400 today); make the timeout test fail unless it gets 504; have [`proxy_tests.rs`](https://github.com/constructorfabric/gears-rust/blob/66da80e5715e10c08c6fafd5e9832137389a3355/gears/system/oagw/oagw/src/api/rest/handlers/proxy_tests.rs) call `proxy_handler`. The timeout test is also referenced by [P-16](#p-16-there-is-no-per-route-timeout-and-oagw-sets-no-timeout-on-plugin-calls).

### P-16: There is no per-route timeout, and OAGW sets no timeout on plugin calls

- **Decision:** `FUTURE` + `FIX-CODE`, P2. The per-route timeout is planned; plugin calls get a timeout now ([`PRD.md:472`](https://github.com/constructorfabric/gears-rust/blob/66da80e5715e10c08c6fafd5e9832137389a3355/gears/system/oagw/docs/PRD.md#L472) MUST).
- **What the docs say:**
  - [`PRD.md:472`](https://github.com/constructorfabric/gears-rust/blob/66da80e5715e10c08c6fafd5e9832137389a3355/gears/system/oagw/docs/PRD.md#L472)
    > Plugin execution timeouts **MUST** be enforced.
  - [scenario 10.1](https://github.com/constructorfabric/gears-rust/blob/66da80e5715e10c08c6fafd5e9832137389a3355/gears/system/oagw/scenarios/plugins/guards/negative-10.1-timeout-guard-plugin-enforces-request-timeout.md#L5-L22)
    > Attach builtin timeout guard plugin to the route: … `504 Gateway Timeout`
  - [`INDEX:689-691`](https://github.com/constructorfabric/gears-rust/blob/66da80e5715e10c08c6fafd5e9832137389a3355/gears/system/oagw/scenarios/INDEX.md#L689-L691)
    > Timeout guard plugin enforces request timeout → 504 … Request exceeding timeout returns `504` gateway timeout
  - [`flows/proxy-operations.md:292-298`](https://github.com/constructorfabric/gears-rust/blob/66da80e5715e10c08c6fafd5e9832137389a3355/gears/system/oagw/scenarios/flows/proxy-operations.md#L292-L298)
    > HTTP/1.1 408 Request Timeout … "type": "gts.cf.core.errors.err.v1~cf.oagw.guard.timeout.v1",
  - [`DESIGN.md:282`](https://github.com/constructorfabric/gears-rust/blob/66da80e5715e10c08c6fafd5e9832137389a3355/gears/system/oagw/docs/DESIGN.md#L282)
    > Timeout and CORS are core Data Plane functionality, not `GuardPlugin` trait implementations: request timeout is gear-level configuration
- **What the code does:**
  - [`config.rs:11-12`](https://github.com/constructorfabric/gears-rust/blob/66da80e5715e10c08c6fafd5e9832137389a3355/gears/system/oagw/oagw/src/config.rs#L11-L12) and [`config.rs:117-119`](https://github.com/constructorfabric/gears-rust/blob/66da80e5715e10c08c6fafd5e9832137389a3355/gears/system/oagw/oagw/src/config.rs#L117-L119): one gear-level `proxy_timeout_secs`, default 30.
  - [`service.rs:1173-1179`](https://github.com/constructorfabric/gears-rust/blob/66da80e5715e10c08c6fafd5e9832137389a3355/gears/system/oagw/oagw/src/infra/proxy/service.rs#L1173-L1179): the DP applies it to the wait for the response head only.

    ```rust
    tokio::time::timeout(timeout, session_bridge::parse_response_stream(client_io))
        .await
        .map_err(|_| DomainError::RequestTimeout {
    ```
  - [`api-gateway gear.rs:540-544`](https://github.com/constructorfabric/gears-rust/blob/66da80e5715e10c08c6fafd5e9832137389a3355/gears/system/api-gateway/src/gear.rs#L540-L544): a hard-coded 30 s timeout layer races it. Values above 30 s are silently capped, and that 504 carries no `X-OAGW-Error-Source`.
  - [`gear.rs:112`](https://github.com/constructorfabric/gears-rust/blob/66da80e5715e10c08c6fafd5e9832137389a3355/gears/system/oagw/oagw/src/gear.rs#L112): the connect timeout is hard-coded at 10 s.
  - Plugin calls: OAGW sets no timeout of its own on credstore or OAuth2 calls. OAuth2 token requests inherit toolkit-http's defaults, 30 s per attempt with up to 3 retries and no total timeout ([`toolkit-http config.rs:754-761`](https://github.com/constructorfabric/gears-rust/blob/66da80e5715e10c08c6fafd5e9832137389a3355/libs/toolkit-http/src/config.rs#L754-L761), used by [`toolkit-auth source.rs:52`](https://github.com/constructorfabric/gears-rust/blob/66da80e5715e10c08c6fafd5e9832137389a3355/libs/toolkit-auth/src/oauth2/source.rs#L52)).
  - Binding `timeout.v1` fails with 500 ([PLG-03](#plg-03-an-unknown-plugin-id-gives-401-auth-500-guard-or-is-silently-skipped-transform-not-the-documented-503)). A guard that rejects with 408 falls into the default arm and becomes 400 ([`error.rs:460-462`](https://github.com/constructorfabric/gears-rust/blob/66da80e5715e10c08c6fafd5e9832137389a3355/gears/system/oagw/oagw/src/api/rest/error.rs#L460-L462)).
- **Evidence of intent:** none. The e2e timeout test is vacuous ([P-12](#p-12-several-proxy-tests-pass-whether-or-not-the-behaviour-works)).
- **Why this decision:** [`PRD.md:472`](https://github.com/constructorfabric/gears-rust/blob/66da80e5715e10c08c6fafd5e9832137389a3355/gears/system/oagw/docs/PRD.md#L472) is a MUST for plugin timeouts, so the code part is needed now. The per-route timeout exists only in scenarios and as a catalog-only id ([`DESIGN.md:282`](https://github.com/constructorfabric/gears-rust/blob/66da80e5715e10c08c6fafd5e9832137389a3355/gears/system/oagw/docs/DESIGN.md#L282)), so it is marked future.
- **Impact:** Not breaking for normal traffic. When credstore or the token endpoint hangs, clients get a timeout error instead of waiting; a guard's 408 gets a matching status instead of 400. FUTURE: [scenario 10.1](https://github.com/constructorfabric/gears-rust/blob/66da80e5715e10c08c6fafd5e9832137389a3355/gears/system/oagw/scenarios/plugins/guards/negative-10.1-timeout-guard-plugin-enforces-request-timeout.md#L5-L22) and [`INDEX:689-691`](https://github.com/constructorfabric/gears-rust/blob/66da80e5715e10c08c6fafd5e9832137389a3355/gears/system/oagw/scenarios/INDEX.md#L689-L691) get a future-facing marker, and the flow doc's 408 `guard.timeout` example ([`flows/proxy-operations.md:292-298`](https://github.com/constructorfabric/gears-rust/blob/66da80e5715e10c08c6fafd5e9832137389a3355/gears/system/oagw/scenarios/flows/proxy-operations.md#L292-L298)) goes. FIX-CODE: bound credstore and OAuth2 token-endpoint calls with a timeout; map a guard 408 to a matching status. The api-gateway 30 s cap and the 10 s connect timeout are documented under [A-05](#config-reference). The vacuous e2e timeout test is under [P-12](#p-12-several-proxy-tests-pass-whether-or-not-the-behaviour-works).

### P-18: The query string is re-encoded, HEAD/OPTIONS can't be routed, and the Host port rule ignores the scheme

- **Decision:** `FIX-CODE` + `FIX-DOC`, P2. Forward the raw query string, make HEAD/OPTIONS routable, and drop the Host port only for the scheme's default. Fix the retry and target-host wording.
- **What the docs say:**
  - [`DESIGN.md:528`](https://github.com/constructorfabric/gears-rust/blob/66da80e5715e10c08c6fafd5e9832137389a3355/gears/system/oagw/docs/DESIGN.md#L528)
    > | Query params | Query | Passthrough allowed params; plugin mutable |
  - [`DESIGN.md:130`](https://github.com/constructorfabric/gears-rust/blob/66da80e5715e10c08c6fafd5e9832137389a3355/gears/system/oagw/docs/DESIGN.md#L130)
    > **No automatic retries**: OAGW never retries failed upstream requests. Retry logic is the client's responsibility.
  - [`PRD.md:228`](https://github.com/constructorfabric/gears-rust/blob/66da80e5715e10c08c6fafd5e9832137389a3355/gears/system/oagw/docs/PRD.md#L228)
    > The gateway performs no automatic full client-request retries—i.e., it will not re-issue the original client request as a whole—but connector-level endpoint/connection failover or connection-retry attempts (endpoint-level retries) performed by the upstream connector are permitted.
  - [`ADR-0002:148`](https://github.com/constructorfabric/gears-rust/blob/66da80e5715e10c08c6fafd5e9832137389a3355/gears/system/oagw/docs/ADR/0002-request-routing.md#L148)
    > The `X-OAGW-Target-Host` header is optional (ignored if provided).
  - [`ADR-0002:180`](https://github.com/constructorfabric/gears-rust/blob/66da80e5715e10c08c6fafd5e9832137389a3355/gears/system/oagw/docs/ADR/0002-request-routing.md#L180)
    > | Single endpoint | 1 | Any | Yes | Validate and route (header optional but validated if present) |
- **What the code does:**
  - Query: [`service.rs:380-389`](https://github.com/constructorfabric/gears-rust/blob/66da80e5715e10c08c6fafd5e9832137389a3355/gears/system/oagw/oagw/src/infra/proxy/service.rs#L380-L389) decodes the query with `form_urlencoded::parse`, and [`request_builder.rs:50-56`](https://github.com/constructorfabric/gears-rust/blob/66da80e5715e10c08c6fafd5e9832137389a3355/gears/system/oagw/oagw/src/infra/proxy/request_builder.rs#L50-L56) re-encodes it. The upstream sees `%20`→`+`, `?flag`→`flag=`, `~`→`%7E`.
  - Host: [`headers.rs:342-346`](https://github.com/constructorfabric/gears-rust/blob/66da80e5715e10c08c6fafd5e9832137389a3355/gears/system/oagw/oagw/src/infra/proxy/headers.rs#L342-L346)

    ```rust
    let host_value = if port == 443 || port == 80 {
        host.to_string()
    ```
    The URL, however, keeps the port unless it is the scheme's default ([`request_builder.rs:61-63`](https://github.com/constructorfabric/gears-rust/blob/66da80e5715e10c08c6fafd5e9832137389a3355/gears/system/oagw/oagw/src/infra/proxy/request_builder.rs#L61-L63)). So an `http` endpoint on 443 gets `Host` without the port.
  - Methods: `HttpMethod` has no `Head` or `Options` ([`model.rs:286-293`](https://github.com/constructorfabric/gears-rust/blob/66da80e5715e10c08c6fafd5e9832137389a3355/gears/system/oagw/oagw/src/domain/model.rs#L286-L293)), so HEAD and OPTIONS never match a route; the HEAD/OPTIONS arms in `error_while_proxy` ([`pingora_proxy.rs:762-769`](https://github.com/constructorfabric/gears-rust/blob/66da80e5715e10c08c6fafd5e9832137389a3355/gears/system/oagw/oagw/src/infra/proxy/pingora_proxy.rs#L762-L769)) are dead code.
  - Retries: stale pooled connections are reconnected for idempotent methods ([`pingora_proxy.rs:753-773`](https://github.com/constructorfabric/gears-rust/blob/66da80e5715e10c08c6fafd5e9832137389a3355/gears/system/oagw/oagw/src/infra/proxy/pingora_proxy.rs#L753-L773)), and DNS lookups retry 3 times ([`pingora_proxy.rs:203-241`](https://github.com/constructorfabric/gears-rust/blob/66da80e5715e10c08c6fafd5e9832137389a3355/gears/system/oagw/oagw/src/infra/proxy/pingora_proxy.rs#L203-L241)). The comment at [`pingora_proxy.rs:742`](https://github.com/constructorfabric/gears-rust/blob/66da80e5715e10c08c6fafd5e9832137389a3355/gears/system/oagw/oagw/src/infra/proxy/pingora_proxy.rs#L742) “Per DESIGN.md §311 and scenario 12.6” cites a stale section.
  - Target host: the header is validated for single-endpoint upstreams too ([`service.rs:276-318`](https://github.com/constructorfabric/gears-rust/blob/66da80e5715e10c08c6fafd5e9832137389a3355/gears/system/oagw/oagw/src/infra/proxy/service.rs#L276-L318) runs before the single-endpoint branch; test [`select_endpoint_single_endpoint_validates_header`](https://github.com/constructorfabric/gears-rust/blob/66da80e5715e10c08c6fafd5e9832137389a3355/gears/system/oagw/oagw/src/infra/proxy/service.rs#L2198-L2215)).
- **Evidence of intent:** none for the query re-encoding or the Host rule; both date from the first implementation.
- **Why this decision:** DESIGN says query parameters pass through, and re-encoding changes signed or order-sensitive queries. [`PRD.md:228`](https://github.com/constructorfabric/gears-rust/blob/66da80e5715e10c08c6fafd5e9832137389a3355/gears/system/oagw/docs/PRD.md#L228) permits connection-level retries, so DESIGN §2.1 ([`DESIGN.md:130`](https://github.com/constructorfabric/gears-rust/blob/66da80e5715e10c08c6fafd5e9832137389a3355/gears/system/oagw/docs/DESIGN.md#L130)) is the stale side. ADR-0002's own matrix ([`ADR-0002:180`](https://github.com/constructorfabric/gears-rust/blob/66da80e5715e10c08c6fafd5e9832137389a3355/gears/system/oagw/docs/ADR/0002-request-routing.md#L180)) already says "validated".
- **Impact:** Breaking for upstreams: they receive the client's query bytes unchanged (parsing stays only for the allowlist check), and an `http` endpoint on 443 or an `https` endpoint on 80 gets the port in `Host`. HEAD and OPTIONS become routable (needed once the CORS preflight is removed, [PLG-13](#plg-13-route-level-cors-is-ignored-unless-sharing-inherit-and-a-descendants-own-private-or-enforce-cors-is-dropped)). Docs: DESIGN §2.1 ([`DESIGN.md:130`](https://github.com/constructorfabric/gears-rust/blob/66da80e5715e10c08c6fafd5e9832137389a3355/gears/system/oagw/docs/DESIGN.md#L130)) describes the connection-level retries; fix the stale code comment ([`pingora_proxy.rs:742`](https://github.com/constructorfabric/gears-rust/blob/66da80e5715e10c08c6fafd5e9832137389a3355/gears/system/oagw/oagw/src/infra/proxy/pingora_proxy.rs#L742)); [`ADR-0002:148`](https://github.com/constructorfabric/gears-rust/blob/66da80e5715e10c08c6fafd5e9832137389a3355/gears/system/oagw/docs/ADR/0002-request-routing.md#L148) → "validated".
- **Decided together with:** [PLG-13](#plg-13-route-level-cors-is-ignored-unless-sharing-inherit-and-a-descendants-own-private-or-enforce-cors-is-dropped)

### P-19: `X-Forwarded-*` and `X-Real-IP` reach the upstream under `passthrough: all`

- **Decision:** `FIX-CODE`, P2. Strip `X-Forwarded-*` and `X-Real-IP` from outbound requests, per F0008. The stale SSRF status markers are a mechanical doc fix.
- **What the docs say:**
  - [`F0008:250-251`](https://github.com/constructorfabric/gears-rust/blob/66da80e5715e10c08c6fafd5e9832137389a3355/gears/system/oagw/docs/features/0008-cpt-cf-oagw-feature-observability.md#L250-L251)
    > 5. [ ] - `p1` - Strip well-known internal headers from outbound request - `inst-ssrf-5` … Remove: `X-Forwarded-For`, `X-Forwarded-Host`, `X-Forwarded-Proto`, `X-Real-IP` (unless explicitly allowed) - `inst-ssrf-5a`
  - [`PRD.md:492`](https://github.com/constructorfabric/gears-rust/blob/66da80e5715e10c08c6fafd5e9832137389a3355/gears/system/oagw/docs/PRD.md#L492)
    > The system **MUST** validate DNS resolution results, enforce IP pinning rules, strip well-known internal headers
  - [`F0008:236`](https://github.com/constructorfabric/gears-rust/blob/66da80e5715e10c08c6fafd5e9832137389a3355/gears/system/oagw/docs/features/0008-cpt-cf-oagw-feature-observability.md#L236)
    > - [ ] `p1` - **ID**: `cpt-cf-oagw-algo-obs-ssrf-protection`
- **What the code does:**
  - [`headers.rs:83-88`](https://github.com/constructorfabric/gears-rust/blob/66da80e5715e10c08c6fafd5e9832137389a3355/gears/system/oagw/oagw/src/infra/proxy/headers.rs#L83-L88)

    ```rust
    /// Remove X-OAGW-* internal headers.
    pub fn strip_internal_headers(headers: &mut HeaderMap) {
        let to_remove: Vec<HeaderName> = headers
            .keys()
            .filter(|k| k.as_str().starts_with("x-oagw-"))
    ```
  - Under `passthrough: all` every inbound header is copied ([`headers.rs:29`](https://github.com/constructorfabric/gears-rust/blob/66da80e5715e10c08c6fafd5e9832137389a3355/gears/system/oagw/oagw/src/infra/proxy/headers.rs#L29)) and only credential headers are removed ([`headers.rs:13-18`](https://github.com/constructorfabric/gears-rust/blob/66da80e5715e10c08c6fafd5e9832137389a3355/gears/system/oagw/oagw/src/infra/proxy/headers.rs#L13-L18)), so `X-Forwarded-*` and `X-Real-IP` reach the upstream.
  - SSRF protection itself is implemented ([`ssrf.rs`](https://github.com/constructorfabric/gears-rust/blob/66da80e5715e10c08c6fafd5e9832137389a3355/gears/system/oagw/oagw/src/domain/ssrf.rs), wired in [`gear.rs:72-76`](https://github.com/constructorfabric/gears-rust/blob/66da80e5715e10c08c6fafd5e9832137389a3355/gears/system/oagw/oagw/src/gear.rs#L72-L76)), although every F0008 SSRF checkbox is `[ ]`.
- **Evidence of intent:** SSRF landed in [`edb361205`](https://github.com/constructorfabric/gears-rust/commit/edb361205f5800210226727f15609d76eccb734f) ("ssrf protection") without header stripping. SSRF is off in e2e ([e2e config sidesteps constraints](#why-the-docs-drifted); [`e2e-local.yaml:531-532`](https://github.com/constructorfabric/gears-rust/blob/66da80e5715e10c08c6fafd5e9832137389a3355/config/e2e-local.yaml#L531-L532)).
- **Why this decision:** The PRD SSRF requirement asks OAGW to strip well-known internal headers; this is a security fix.
- **Impact:** Not breaking for clients. Upstreams configured with `passthrough: all` stop receiving `X-Forwarded-*` and `X-Real-IP`. Code: `strip_internal_headers` ([`headers.rs:84-93`](https://github.com/constructorfabric/gears-rust/blob/66da80e5715e10c08c6fafd5e9832137389a3355/gears/system/oagw/oagw/src/infra/proxy/headers.rs#L84-L93)). The SSRF checkbox status (implemented in `edb361205`) is ticked by the status-marker sync ([O-06](#status-markers-checkbox-sync)). [P-08](#p-08-unreachable-tls-failed-and-http-only-violating-upstreams-get-different-statuses-than-documented) hands the SSRF DNS-block status (503 today, 400 in F0008) to this item; this decision does not change that status.
- **Decided together with:** [O-06](#status-markers-checkbox-sync), [P-08](#p-08-unreachable-tls-failed-and-http-only-violating-upstreams-get-different-statuses-than-documented)

## 5. Plugins, auth, guards, transforms, CORS

This area covers which plugins exist and how they run: plugin merging, unknown plugin ids, what guards see, credential failures, hop-by-hop stripping, and the CORS deferral.

Mechanical doc fixes in this area: [PLG-04](#other-doc-only-fixes-plugins), [PLG-06](#other-doc-only-fixes-plugins), [PLG-07](#other-doc-only-fixes-plugins), [PLG-08](#other-doc-only-fixes-plugins), [PLG-09](#other-doc-only-fixes-plugins), [PLG-11](#other-doc-only-fixes-plugins), [PLG-14](#other-doc-only-fixes-plugins), [PLG-15](#other-doc-only-fixes-plugins), [PLG-16](#other-doc-only-fixes-plugins) (see [Mechanical doc fixes](#mechanical-doc-fixes)).

### PLG-01: Six cataloged plugins (basic, bearer, timeout, cors, logging, metrics) have no implementation, but some docs still promise them

- **Decision:** `FUTURE` + `FIX-DOC`, no priority. The six plugins without a runtime are planned, not built now; all 12 plugin GTS ids stay registered as catalog-only, per ADR-0003.
- **What the docs say:**
  - [`F0003:348-350`](https://github.com/constructorfabric/gears-rust/blob/66da80e5715e10c08c6fafd5e9832137389a3355/gears/system/oagw/docs/features/0003-cpt-cf-oagw-feature-plugin-system.md#L348-L350)
    > - [ ] `p1` - **ID**: `cpt-cf-oagw-dod-builtin-plugins` … The system **MUST** implement 6 auth plugins (`noop`, `apikey`, `basic`, `bearer`, `oauth2_client_cred`, `oauth2_client_cred_basic`), 2 guard plugins (`timeout`, `cors`), and 3 transform plugins (`logging`, `metrics`, `request_id`).
  - [`F0003:425`](https://github.com/constructorfabric/gears-rust/blob/66da80e5715e10c08c6fafd5e9832137389a3355/gears/system/oagw/docs/features/0003-cpt-cf-oagw-feature-plugin-system.md#L425) “- [ ] All 11 built-in plugins (6 auth, 2 guard, 3 transform) register in the in-process registry”
  - [`DECOMPOSITION.md:183`](https://github.com/constructorfabric/gears-rust/blob/66da80e5715e10c08c6fafd5e9832137389a3355/gears/system/oagw/docs/DECOMPOSITION.md#L183)
    > All 11 built-in plugin implementations (6 auth, 2 guard, 3 transform)
  - [`PRD.md:171`](https://github.com/constructorfabric/gears-rust/blob/66da80e5715e10c08c6fafd5e9832137389a3355/gears/system/oagw/docs/PRD.md#L171) “Credential injection via auth plugins (API Key, Basic Auth, OAuth2 Client Credentials, Bearer Token)” and [`PRD.md:237`](https://github.com/constructorfabric/gears-rust/blob/66da80e5715e10c08c6fafd5e9832137389a3355/gears/system/oagw/docs/PRD.md#L237)
    > Supported authentication methods: API Key (header/query), HTTP Basic Auth, OAuth2 Client Credentials, and Bearer Token.
  - [`plugin-execution.md:419-429`](https://github.com/constructorfabric/gears-rust/blob/66da80e5715e10c08c6fafd5e9832137389a3355/gears/system/oagw/scenarios/flows/plugin-execution.md#L419-L429)
    > Included in `oagw` crate (`infra/plugin/`): - `ApiKeyAuthPlugin` - `BearerTokenAuthPlugin` - `TimeoutGuardPlugin` - `CorsGuardPlugin` - `RateLimitGuardPlugin` - `LoggingTransformPlugin` - `MetricsTransformPlugin`
  - The example at [`plugin-execution.md:54-81`](https://github.com/constructorfabric/gears-rust/blob/66da80e5715e10c08c6fafd5e9832137389a3355/gears/system/oagw/scenarios/flows/plugin-execution.md#L54-L81) “"plugin": "gts.cf.core.oagw.auth_plugin.v1~cf.core.oagw.bearer.v1",” binds bearer, timeout, logging and cors.
