This also covers the shared-pool cross-resets ([`merge.rs:284-290`](https://github.com/constructorfabric/gears-rust/blob/66da80e5715e10c08c6fafd5e9832137389a3355/gears/system/oagw/oagw/src/domain/services/management/merge.rs#L284-L290), [`merge.rs:369-371`](https://github.com/constructorfabric/gears-rust/blob/66da80e5715e10c08c6fafd5e9832137389a3355/gears/system/oagw/oagw/src/domain/services/management/merge.rs#L369-L371)).
- **Decided together with:** [R-14](#r-14-a-route-cannot-set-only-a-cost-that-draws-on-the-upstream-budget).

### R-07: `scope=ip` trusts client-supplied `X-Forwarded-For`, and buckets are never evicted

- **Decision:** `FIX-CODE`, P0. `scope=ip` must not trust client-supplied headers, and buckets must be evicted.
- **What the docs say:**
  - [`F0006:163`](https://github.com/constructorfabric/gears-rust/blob/66da80e5715e10c08c6fafd5e9832137389a3355/gears/system/oagw/docs/features/0006-cpt-cf-oagw-feature-rate-limiting.md#L163) (`inst-tb-1`)
    > `ip`: `oagw:ratelimit:{resource_type}:{resource_id}:ip:{client_ip}:{window}` (falls back to `unknown` when no client IP)
  - The code comment at [`headers.rs:357-358`](https://github.com/constructorfabric/gears-rust/blob/66da80e5715e10c08c6fafd5e9832137389a3355/gears/system/oagw/oagw/src/infra/proxy/headers.rs#L357-L358) promises a trust check that does not exist:
    > // Only trusted when headers originate from a trusted proxy.
- **What the code does:**
  - The leftmost `X-Forwarded-For` value is used, then `X-Real-IP` ([`headers.rs:356-377`](https://github.com/constructorfabric/gears-rust/blob/66da80e5715e10c08c6fafd5e9832137389a3355/gears/system/oagw/oagw/src/infra/proxy/headers.rs#L356-L377)). There is no trusted-proxy config and no fallback to the socket peer, so a client that rotates `X-Forwarded-For` gets a fresh bucket on every request.

    ```rust
    if let Some(xff) = headers.get("x-forwarded-for")
        && let Ok(val) = xff.to_str()
        && let Some(candidate) = val
            .split(',')
            .next()
    ```
  - Each new key adds a map entry that is never evicted; `purge_keys` is `#[allow(dead_code)]` ([`rate_limit.rs:362-366`](https://github.com/constructorfabric/gears-rust/blob/66da80e5715e10c08c6fafd5e9832137389a3355/gears/system/oagw/oagw/src/domain/rate_limit.rs#L362-L366)). The same applies to `user` scope.
- **Evidence of intent:** none for trusting the header. Unit tests cover the extraction and key format only; for example [`extract_client_ip_from_xff`](https://github.com/constructorfabric/gears-rust/blob/66da80e5715e10c08c6fafd5e9832137389a3355/gears/system/oagw/oagw/src/infra/proxy/headers.rs#L974-L978) asserts that the leftmost `X-Forwarded-For` value wins.
- **Why this decision:** security first. A client-controlled header must not choose the bucket, and unbounded per-key growth is a memory-exhaustion risk.
- **Impact:** IP-scoped limits apply per real client; deployments behind a proxy must configure its CIDR. Operators see changed keying. Follow-ups: take the client IP from a trusted-proxy CIDR config, falling back to the socket peer ([`headers.rs:356-377`](https://github.com/constructorfabric/gears-rust/blob/66da80e5715e10c08c6fafd5e9832137389a3355/gears/system/oagw/oagw/src/infra/proxy/headers.rs#L356-L377)); add TTL/LRU eviction by wiring `purge_keys` ([`rate_limit.rs:363`](https://github.com/constructorfabric/gears-rust/blob/66da80e5715e10c08c6fafd5e9832137389a3355/gears/system/oagw/oagw/src/domain/rate_limit.rs#L363)), for `user` scope too; fix the misleading comment at [`headers.rs:358`](https://github.com/constructorfabric/gears-rust/blob/66da80e5715e10c08c6fafd5e9832137389a3355/gears/system/oagw/oagw/src/infra/proxy/headers.rs#L358).

### R-08: `scope=route` on an upstream-level limit acts as one global bucket

- **Decision:** `FIX-CODE`, P2. `scope=route` on an upstream-level limit includes the route id in the key.
- **What the docs say:**
  - [scenario 18.3](https://github.com/constructorfabric/gears-rust/blob/66da80e5715e10c08c6fafd5e9832137389a3355/gears/system/oagw/scenarios/rate-limiting/positive-18.3-rate-limit-scope-variants.md#L28)
    > - `route`: limit isolated per route.
  - [`F0006:165`](https://github.com/constructorfabric/gears-rust/blob/66da80e5715e10c08c6fafd5e9832137389a3355/gears/system/oagw/docs/features/0006-cpt-cf-oagw-feature-rate-limiting.md#L165) (key table)
    > `route`: `oagw:ratelimit:{resource_type}:{resource_id}:route:{window}` (no scope_id segment)
- **What the code does:**
  - [`rate_limit.rs:330-332`](https://github.com/constructorfabric/gears-rust/blob/66da80e5715e10c08c6fafd5e9832137389a3355/gears/system/oagw/oagw/src/domain/rate_limit.rs#L330-L332): the key has no route segment. For an upstream-level limit, `resource_id` is the upstream id, so all routes and all tenants share one bucket.

    ```rust
    RateLimitScope::Route => {
        format!("oagw:ratelimit:{res}:{}:route:{w}", ctx.resource_id)
    ```
- **Evidence of intent:** none. The e2e test [`test_scope_route_isolation`](https://github.com/constructorfabric/gears-rust/blob/66da80e5715e10c08c6fafd5e9832137389a3355/testing/e2e/suites/oagw/test_rate_limiting.py#L315-L372) and the Rust test [`proxy_rate_limit_scope_route_isolates_by_route`](https://github.com/constructorfabric/gears-rust/blob/66da80e5715e10c08c6fafd5e9832137389a3355/gears/system/oagw/oagw/tests/proxy_integration.rs#L587) put the limit on the routes, so they cannot catch this.
- **Why this decision:** scenario 18.3 says `route` isolates per route; an upstream-level limit with that scope should do the same.
- **Impact:** upstream-level `scope=route` limits split per route, so a caller hitting several routes gets more total capacity than today. Follow-ups: [`rate_limit.rs:330-332`](https://github.com/constructorfabric/gears-rust/blob/66da80e5715e10c08c6fafd5e9832137389a3355/gears/system/oagw/oagw/src/domain/rate_limit.rs#L330-L332); add a test with the limit on the upstream.

### R-09: A shared budget with `scope=tenant` is not shared, and `budget.total` is ignored at runtime

- **Decision:** `FIX-CODE`, P1. A shared pool is shared: no tenant segment in the pool key, and the capacity is `budget.total`, as in ADR-0004 Example 2.
- **What the docs say:**
  - [`ADR-0004:571-585`](https://github.com/constructorfabric/gears-rust/blob/66da80e5715e10c08c6fafd5e9832137389a3355/gears/system/oagw/docs/ADR/0004-rate-limiting.md#L571-L585) (Example 2)
    > "budget": { "mode": "shared", "total": 5000 } … Tenants A, B, C share 5000/min pool. First-come-first-served. No individual guarantees.
  - [scenario 18.7-B](https://github.com/constructorfabric/gears-rust/blob/66da80e5715e10c08c6fafd5e9832137389a3355/gears/system/oagw/scenarios/rate-limiting/positive-18.7-budget-modes-behave-specified.md#L22-L29) (default scope is `tenant`)
    > Multiple tenants share the same pool; first-come-first-served.
- **What the code does:**
  - The pool owner id replaces only the resource id segment of the key ([`service.rs:1449`](https://github.com/constructorfabric/gears-rust/blob/66da80e5715e10c08c6fafd5e9832137389a3355/gears/system/oagw/oagw/src/infra/proxy/service.rs#L1449)).

    ```rust
    let effective_resource_id = rl.pool_owner_id.as_ref().unwrap_or(&upstream.id);
    ```
  - `scope=tenant` still appends the caller's tenant id ([`rate_limit.rs:309-314`](https://github.com/constructorfabric/gears-rust/blob/66da80e5715e10c08c6fafd5e9832137389a3355/gears/system/oagw/oagw/src/domain/rate_limit.rs#L309-L314)), so each tenant gets its own bucket.
  - The pool size is the effective `sustained`/`burst` ([`rate_limit.rs:55-61`](https://github.com/constructorfabric/gears-rust/blob/66da80e5715e10c08c6fafd5e9832137389a3355/gears/system/oagw/oagw/src/domain/rate_limit.rs#L55-L61)). `budget.total` is only checked for presence and a minimum of 1 ([`budget.rs:14-24`](https://github.com/constructorfabric/gears-rust/blob/66da80e5715e10c08c6fafd5e9832137389a3355/gears/system/oagw/oagw/src/domain/services/management/budget.rs#L14-L24)).
  - The [R-06](#r-06-requests-with-a-different-merged-limit-reset-the-shared-bucket-so-callers-bypass-limits) resets also apply.
- **Evidence of intent:** none. The e2e tests cannot tell: [`test_scope_global`](https://github.com/constructorfabric/gears-rust/blob/66da80e5715e10c08c6fafd5e9832137389a3355/testing/e2e/suites/oagw/test_rate_limiting.py#L238-L306) combines a shared budget with `scope: global`, so it cannot show which one causes the sharing; [`test_shared_budget_config_accepted`](https://github.com/constructorfabric/gears-rust/blob/66da80e5715e10c08c6fafd5e9832137389a3355/testing/e2e/suites/oagw/test_budget_allocation.py#L443-L468) only checks that the config is accepted; [`test_scope_tenant_isolation`](https://github.com/constructorfabric/gears-rust/blob/66da80e5715e10c08c6fafd5e9832137389a3355/testing/e2e/suites/oagw/test_rate_limiting.py#L375-L441) would pass even under global scope, because the isolation comes from the separate binding ids.
- **Why this decision:** the accepted ADR-0004 Example 2 and scenario 18.7-B both say tenants share one pool of `budget.total`.
- **Impact:** tenants under a shared pool now draw from one bucket sized by `budget.total`, so a busy tenant can exhaust it for the others. Follow-ups: [`rate_limit.rs:309-314`](https://github.com/constructorfabric/gears-rust/blob/66da80e5715e10c08c6fafd5e9832137389a3355/gears/system/oagw/oagw/src/domain/rate_limit.rs#L309-L314), [`service.rs:1449`](https://github.com/constructorfabric/gears-rust/blob/66da80e5715e10c08c6fafd5e9832137389a3355/gears/system/oagw/oagw/src/infra/proxy/service.rs#L1449), [`budget.rs:16-23`](https://github.com/constructorfabric/gears-rust/blob/66da80e5715e10c08c6fafd5e9832137389a3355/gears/system/oagw/oagw/src/domain/services/management/budget.rs#L16-L23). Rewrite `test_scope_global`, `test_shared_budget_config_accepted` and `test_scope_tenant_isolation` so each isolates one variable. Add an e2e test for scenario 18.7-B.

### R-10: `strategy: queue` and `degrade` are accepted but behave as `reject`; the `queue` block is dropped

- **Decision:** `FUTURE` + `FIX-CODE`, P2. The `queue` and `degrade` strategies are planned. Until then, reject them at write time.
- **What the docs say:**
  - [`ADR-0004:282`](https://github.com/constructorfabric/gears-rust/blob/66da80e5715e10c08c6fafd5e9832137389a3355/gears/system/oagw/docs/ADR/0004-rate-limiting.md#L282)
    > | `strategy`         | enum | `reject`         | `reject` (429), `queue`, `degrade`                       |
  - [`upstream.v1.schema.json:253-258`](https://github.com/constructorfabric/gears-rust/blob/66da80e5715e10c08c6fafd5e9832137389a3355/gears/system/oagw/docs/schemas/upstream.v1.schema.json#L253-L258)
    > "enum": [ "reject", "queue", "degrade" ],
  - [scenario 18.5-B](https://github.com/constructorfabric/gears-rust/blob/66da80e5715e10c08c6fafd5e9832137389a3355/gears/system/oagw/scenarios/rate-limiting/negative-18.5-strategy-variants-limit-exceeded.md#L8-L25)
    > "strategy": "queue", … Second request waits and then succeeds when tokens refill, or times out with:
  - [`F0006:460`](https://github.com/constructorfabric/gears-rust/blob/66da80e5715e10c08c6fafd5e9832137389a3355/gears/system/oagw/docs/features/0006-cpt-cf-oagw-feature-rate-limiting.md#L460) (Deliberate Omissions)
    > Only `reject` and `queue` strategies implemented.
  - [`PRD.md:246`](https://github.com/constructorfabric/gears-rust/blob/66da80e5715e10c08c6fafd5e9832137389a3355/gears/system/oagw/docs/PRD.md#L246)
    > strategy (reject with 429 + Retry-After, queue, or degrade).
  - Consistent: [`F0006:99`](https://github.com/constructorfabric/gears-rust/blob/66da80e5715e10c08c6fafd5e9832137389a3355/gears/system/oagw/docs/features/0006-cpt-cf-oagw-feature-rate-limiting.md#L99)
    > 2. [ ] - `p2` - **IF** strategy = `queue` - `inst-rl-6b`
- **What the code does:**
  - `strategy` is only mapped between layers ([`dto.rs:136`](https://github.com/constructorfabric/gears-rust/blob/66da80e5715e10c08c6fafd5e9832137389a3355/gears/system/oagw/oagw/src/api/rest/dto.rs#L136), [`client.rs:370-374`](https://github.com/constructorfabric/gears-rust/blob/66da80e5715e10c08c6fafd5e9832137389a3355/gears/system/oagw/oagw/src/domain/services/client.rs#L370-L374), [`type_provisioning.rs:511`](https://github.com/constructorfabric/gears-rust/blob/66da80e5715e10c08c6fafd5e9832137389a3355/gears/system/oagw/oagw/src/infra/type_provisioning.rs#L511)) and never read at runtime. An exceeded limit always returns 429.
  - The DTOs do not use `deny_unknown_fields`, so a `queue{…}` block is silently dropped.
- **Evidence of intent:** none; `inst-rl-6b` is unticked and ADR-0012 (backpressure queueing) is `proposed`.
- **Why this decision:** the feature is unbuilt, so it is marked planned. Rejecting it at write time stops a config from silently meaning something other than what it says.
- **Impact:** upstream and route writes with `strategy` other than `reject`, or with `rate_limit.queue`, now get 400. Breaking for clients that send them today. Follow-ups: 400 "not supported" for `strategy ≠ reject` and for `rate_limit.queue`; untick or fix [`F0006:460`](https://github.com/constructorfabric/gears-rust/blob/66da80e5715e10c08c6fafd5e9832137389a3355/gears/system/oagw/docs/features/0006-cpt-cf-oagw-feature-rate-limiting.md#L460) ("queue implemented"); scenarios 18.5-B/C get a future-facing marker. No blanket `deny_unknown_fields` (it breaks GET-then-PUT clients). [`PRD.md:246`](https://github.com/constructorfabric/gears-rust/blob/66da80e5715e10c08c6fafd5e9832137389a3355/gears/system/oagw/docs/PRD.md#L246) lists `queue` and `degrade` under a MUST; a planned marker there needs product sign-off.
- **Decided together with:** [R-18](#status-markers-checkbox-sync) (its `F0006` omission note is fixed here).

### R-11: Circuit breaker, concurrency limit and backpressure fields are silently accepted and ignored

- **Decision:** `FUTURE` + `FIX-CODE`, P2. Circuit breaker, concurrency limits and backpressure are planned. Until then, reject their fields at write time.
- **What the docs say:**
  - [`F0006:134`](https://github.com/constructorfabric/gears-rust/blob/66da80e5715e10c08c6fafd5e9832137389a3355/gears/system/oagw/docs/features/0006-cpt-cf-oagw-feature-rate-limiting.md#L134)
    > 1. [x] - `p2` - Actor sends PUT /api/oagw/v1/upstreams/{id} or PUT /api/oagw/v1/routes/{id} with `rate_limit`, `circuit_breaker`, or `concurrency_limit` in request body - `inst-cfg-1`
  - [`F0006:393`](https://github.com/constructorfabric/gears-rust/blob/66da80e5715e10c08c6fafd5e9832137389a3355/gears/system/oagw/docs/features/0006-cpt-cf-oagw-feature-rate-limiting.md#L393)
    > - DB: `oagw_upstream` (concurrency_limit column), `oagw_route` (concurrency_limit column)
  - [`PRD.md:482`](https://github.com/constructorfabric/gears-rust/blob/66da80e5715e10c08c6fafd5e9832137389a3355/gears/system/oagw/docs/PRD.md#L482)
    > Circuit breakers **MUST** prevent cascade failures from unhealthy upstreams.
  - [`DESIGN.md:729`](https://github.com/constructorfabric/gears-rust/blob/66da80e5715e10c08c6fafd5e9832137389a3355/gears/system/oagw/docs/DESIGN.md#L729) lists it as a live error:
    > | CircuitBreakerOpen | 503 | `gts.cf.core.errors.err.v1~cf.oagw.circuit_breaker.open.v1` | Yes | Circuit breaker open |
  - [`INDEX:763`](https://github.com/constructorfabric/gears-rust/blob/66da80e5715e10c08c6fafd5e9832137389a3355/gears/system/oagw/scenarios/INDEX.md#L763)
    > `CircuitBreakerOpen`, `ConnectionTimeout`, `RequestTimeout`, `IdleTimeout`, `PluginNotFound`, `PluginInUse` — each has at least one reproducer scenario.
- **What the code does:**
  - No `circuit_breaker` or `concurrency_limit` field exists in [`model.rs`](https://github.com/constructorfabric/gears-rust/blob/66da80e5715e10c08c6fafd5e9832137389a3355/gears/system/oagw/oagw/src/domain/model.rs), [`dto.rs`](https://github.com/constructorfabric/gears-rust/blob/66da80e5715e10c08c6fafd5e9832137389a3355/gears/system/oagw/oagw/src/api/rest/dto.rs) or [`sdk/models.rs`](https://github.com/constructorfabric/gears-rust/blob/66da80e5715e10c08c6fafd5e9832137389a3355/gears/system/oagw/oagw-sdk/src/models.rs), and there is no semaphore or queue. The fields are silently dropped.
  - `CircuitBreakerOpen` is mapped to 503 with a fixed 30 s `Retry-After` ([`error.rs:67`](https://github.com/constructorfabric/gears-rust/blob/66da80e5715e10c08c6fafd5e9832137389a3355/gears/system/oagw/oagw/src/api/rest/error.rs#L67), [`error.rs:315-319`](https://github.com/constructorfabric/gears-rust/blob/66da80e5715e10c08c6fafd5e9832137389a3355/gears/system/oagw/oagw/src/api/rest/error.rs#L315-L319)) but never constructed outside tests. There is no `X-Circuit-State` header.
- **Evidence of intent:** the consistent docs treat these as future: ADR-0005, ADR-0011 and ADR-0012 are `proposed`, the algorithm boxes in `F0006` are `[ ]`, and INDEX marks its concurrency and circuit-breaker section "(future-facing)". Also, ADR-0005's error body is `{error:{code,message}}`, not RFC 9457 ([`ADR-0005:295-301`](https://github.com/constructorfabric/gears-rust/blob/66da80e5715e10c08c6fafd5e9832137389a3355/gears/system/oagw/docs/ADR/0005-circuit-breaker.md#L295-L301)), and ADR-0012 answers `reject` with 503 ([`ADR-0012:75`](https://github.com/constructorfabric/gears-rust/blob/66da80e5715e10c08c6fafd5e9832137389a3355/gears/system/oagw/docs/ADR/0012-backpressure-queueing.md#L75)) while ADR-0004 answers it with 429.
- **Why this decision:** the only ADRs for these features are `proposed`, so the features are unbuilt; they get a planned marker, and write-time rejection stops configs being silently ignored.
- **Impact:** requests carrying `circuit_breaker` or `concurrency_limit` now get 400. Breaking for clients that send them today. Follow-ups: 400 for `circuit_breaker`/`concurrency_limit` in request bodies. Docs: untick `F0006` `inst-cfg-1` ([l.134](https://github.com/constructorfabric/gears-rust/blob/66da80e5715e10c08c6fafd5e9832137389a3355/gears/system/oagw/docs/features/0006-cpt-cf-oagw-feature-rate-limiting.md#L134)) and the DB-column claim ([l.393](https://github.com/constructorfabric/gears-rust/blob/66da80e5715e10c08c6fafd5e9832137389a3355/gears/system/oagw/docs/features/0006-cpt-cf-oagw-feature-rate-limiting.md#L393)); mark [`PRD.md:482`](https://github.com/constructorfabric/gears-rust/blob/66da80e5715e10c08c6fafd5e9832137389a3355/gears/system/oagw/docs/PRD.md#L482) as planned (needs product sign-off); `DESIGN` [l.729](https://github.com/constructorfabric/gears-rust/blob/66da80e5715e10c08c6fafd5e9832137389a3355/gears/system/oagw/docs/DESIGN.md#L729) `CircuitBreakerOpen` → future; [`INDEX:763`](https://github.com/constructorfabric/gears-rust/blob/66da80e5715e10c08c6fafd5e9832137389a3355/gears/system/oagw/scenarios/INDEX.md#L763). ADR-0005/0011/0012 stay `proposed`.

### R-12: Rate-limit values are never validated; `cost: 0` turns limiting off

- **Decision:** `FIX-CODE`, P2. Add write-time rate-limit validation.
- **What the docs say:**
  - [`F0006:135`](https://github.com/constructorfabric/gears-rust/blob/66da80e5715e10c08c6fafd5e9832137389a3355/gears/system/oagw/docs/features/0006-cpt-cf-oagw-feature-rate-limiting.md#L135)
    > 2. [x] - `p2` - Validate rate limit config: `sustained.rate` > 0, `burst.capacity` ≥ 1, `scope` is valid enum, `strategy` is valid enum - `inst-cfg-2`
  - [`ADR-0004:468`](https://github.com/constructorfabric/gears-rust/blob/66da80e5715e10c08c6fafd5e9832137389a3355/gears/system/oagw/docs/ADR/0004-rate-limiting.md#L468) (schema; `burst.capacity` and `cost` also have `minimum: 1`)
    > "rate": { "type": "integer", "minimum": 1 },
- **What the code does:**
  - `cost`, `rate` and `capacity` are bare `u32` with no checks ([`dto.rs:137-175`](https://github.com/constructorfabric/gears-rust/blob/66da80e5715e10c08c6fafd5e9832137389a3355/gears/system/oagw/oagw/src/api/rest/dto.rs#L137-L175)).
  - `cost=0` always passes ([`rate_limit.rs:79`](https://github.com/constructorfabric/gears-rust/blob/66da80e5715e10c08c6fafd5e9832137389a3355/gears/system/oagw/oagw/src/domain/rate_limit.rs#L79), [`rate_limit.rs:190`](https://github.com/constructorfabric/gears-rust/blob/66da80e5715e10c08c6fafd5e9832137389a3355/gears/system/oagw/oagw/src/domain/rate_limit.rs#L190)).
  - `rate=0` gives a fixed `Retry-After` of 60 ([`rate_limit.rs:88-90`](https://github.com/constructorfabric/gears-rust/blob/66da80e5715e10c08c6fafd5e9832137389a3355/gears/system/oagw/oagw/src/domain/rate_limit.rs#L88-L90)).

    ```rust
    if self.refill_rate <= 0.0 {
        return 60;
    }
    ```
  - A request with `cost > capacity` can never succeed.
  - The route's rate limit is never validated; `create_route` checks only CORS ([`mod.rs:335-337`](https://github.com/constructorfabric/gears-rust/blob/66da80e5715e10c08c6fafd5e9832137389a3355/gears/system/oagw/oagw/src/domain/services/management/mod.rs#L335-L337)).
- **Evidence of intent:** none. The `inst-cfg-2` box was ticked by the [bulk checkbox sync](#why-the-docs-drifted).
- **Why this decision:** the accepted ADR-0004 schema sets `minimum: 1`, and `F0006` claims the check exists.
- **Impact:** writes with `rate=0`, `capacity=0`, `cost=0` or `cost > capacity` now get 400. Breaking for clients that send such values. Follow-ups: 400 for those values ([`dto.rs:137-157`](https://github.com/constructorfabric/gears-rust/blob/66da80e5715e10c08c6fafd5e9832137389a3355/gears/system/oagw/oagw/src/api/rest/dto.rs#L137-L157) or domain validation); validate the route budget too. The schema alignment ([M-08](#m-08-the-json-schemas-in-docsschemas-do-not-match-what-the-api-accepts-and-returns)) then marks the rate-limit minimums as enforced.
- **Decided together with:** [M-08](#m-08-the-json-schemas-in-docsschemas-do-not-match-what-the-api-accepts-and-returns).

### R-13: The `min()` merge copies the whole config of the lower-rate side

- **Decision:** `FIX-CODE`, P2. `min()` merges per field and keeps the ancestor's scope.
- **What the docs say:**
  - [`F0006:191`](https://github.com/constructorfabric/gears-rust/blob/66da80e5715e10c08c6fafd5e9832137389a3355/gears/system/oagw/docs/features/0006-cpt-cf-oagw-feature-rate-limiting.md#L191)
    > 4. [x] - `p2` - Compute effective burst capacity: `min(own_burst, all_enforced_ancestor_bursts)` - `inst-merge-4`
  - [`ADR-0004:566-569`](https://github.com/constructorfabric/gears-rust/blob/66da80e5715e10c08c6fafd5e9832137389a3355/gears/system/oagw/docs/ADR/0004-rate-limiting.md#L566-L569) (Example 1)
    > - Sustained: `min(system:10000, partner:5000, tenant:1000)` = 1000/min … - Burst: `min(system:1000, partner:500, tenant:100)` = 100
- **What the code does:**
  - [`merge.rs:356-378`](https://github.com/constructorfabric/gears-rust/blob/66da80e5715e10c08c6fafd5e9832137389a3355/gears/system/oagw/oagw/src/domain/services/management/merge.rs#L356-L378): `min_rate_limit` compares only the normalised sustained rate and returns the whole winning config, including burst, scope, algorithm, cost, headers and budget.

    ```rust
    let a_rate = rate_per_second(a);
    let b_rate = rate_per_second(b);
    if b_rate < a_rate {
        let mut winner = b.clone();
    ```
  - A lower-rate child with a huge burst keeps its burst, and an ancestor's `scope: global` is lost.
- **Evidence of intent:** none.
- **Why this decision:** the accepted ADR-0004 Example 1 and `F0006` `inst-merge-4` take the minimum of each field separately.
- **Impact:** effective burst and scope change for merged limits, so some children get a smaller burst. Follow-ups: `min_rate_limit` ([`merge.rs:356-378`](https://github.com/constructorfabric/gears-rust/blob/66da80e5715e10c08c6fafd5e9832137389a3355/gears/system/oagw/oagw/src/domain/services/management/merge.rs#L356-L378)) takes a per-field min of sustained and burst, per ADR-0004 Example 1 and `F0006` `inst-merge-4`. Decide in the PR which side's algorithm, cost and headers win.

### R-14: A route cannot set only a `cost` that draws on the upstream budget

- **Decision:** `FIX-CODE`, P2. A route `rate_limit` may carry only `cost`, charged to the upstream bucket, as in ADR-0004 Example 3.
- **What the docs say:**
  - [`ADR-0004:587-610`](https://github.com/constructorfabric/gears-rust/blob/66da80e5715e10c08c6fafd5e9832137389a3355/gears/system/oagw/docs/ADR/0004-rate-limiting.md#L587-L610) (Example 3)
    > "rate_limit": { "cost": 10 } … Tenant with 1000 tokens/min can call:
  - [scenario 18.4](https://github.com/constructorfabric/gears-rust/blob/66da80e5715e10c08c6fafd5e9832137389a3355/gears/system/oagw/scenarios/rate-limiting/positive-18.4-weighted-cost-per-route.md#L5-L24)
    > - Route A: `cost=10` - Route B: `cost=1` … Ten calls to Route B consume the same budget.
  - [`F0006:413`](https://github.com/constructorfabric/gears-rust/blob/66da80e5715e10c08c6fafd5e9832137389a3355/gears/system/oagw/docs/features/0006-cpt-cf-oagw-feature-rate-limiting.md#L413)
    > - [x] Cost-based rate limiting deducts `cost` tokens per request (configurable per route)
- **What the code does:**
  - Route `rate_limit.sustained` is required ([`dto.rs:128`](https://github.com/constructorfabric/gears-rust/blob/66da80e5715e10c08c6fafd5e9832137389a3355/gears/system/oagw/oagw/src/api/rest/dto.rs#L128)), so `{"cost":10}` fails to deserialize.

    ```rust
    pub sustained: SustainedRate,
    ```
  - Route cost is charged only to the route's own bucket ([`service.rs:1465-1476`](https://github.com/constructorfabric/gears-rust/blob/66da80e5715e10c08c6fafd5e9832137389a3355/gears/system/oagw/oagw/src/infra/proxy/service.rs#L1465-L1476)).
- **Evidence of intent:** none. The e2e test [`test_weighted_cost`](https://github.com/constructorfabric/gears-rust/blob/66da80e5715e10c08c6fafd5e9832137389a3355/testing/e2e/suites/oagw/test_rate_limiting.py#L446-L484) puts the cost on the upstream, with a single route.
- **Why this decision:** the accepted ADR-0004 Example 3 and scenario 18.4 define per-route cost against one shared budget.
- **Impact:** a route body with only `cost` is now accepted, and route cost draws on the upstream bucket. Not breaking for existing configs. Follow-ups: make route `sustained` optional when only `cost` is given ([`dto.rs:128`](https://github.com/constructorfabric/gears-rust/blob/66da80e5715e10c08c6fafd5e9832137389a3355/gears/system/oagw/oagw/src/api/rest/dto.rs#L128)); charge the route cost to the upstream bucket ([`service.rs:1465-1476`](https://github.com/constructorfabric/gears-rust/blob/66da80e5715e10c08c6fafd5e9832137389a3355/gears/system/oagw/oagw/src/infra/proxy/service.rs#L1465-L1476)). This works together with [R-06](#r-06-requests-with-a-different-merged-limit-reset-the-shared-bucket-so-callers-bypass-limits) (separate upstream and route buckets). Add an e2e test for scenario 18.4 (two routes, cost 10 and cost 1, one upstream budget); fix `test_weighted_cost`. The fix PR re-ticks [`F0006:413`](https://github.com/constructorfabric/gears-rust/blob/66da80e5715e10c08c6fafd5e9832137389a3355/gears/system/oagw/docs/features/0006-cpt-cf-oagw-feature-rate-limiting.md#L413).
- **Decided together with:** [R-06](#r-06-requests-with-a-different-merged-limit-reset-the-shared-bucket-so-callers-bypass-limits).

### R-15: Allocated-budget checks count grandchildren against the top parent

- **Decision:** `FIX-CODE`, P2. Allocated-budget validation sums only direct children at each level.
- **What the docs say:**
  - [`ADR-0004:288`](https://github.com/constructorfabric/gears-rust/blob/66da80e5715e10c08c6fafd5e9832137389a3355/gears/system/oagw/docs/ADR/0004-rate-limiting.md#L288)
    > Sum of child allocations ≤ parent allocation (configurable overcommit ratio).
  - [`ADR-0004:527-569`](https://github.com/constructorfabric/gears-rust/blob/66da80e5715e10c08c6fafd5e9832137389a3355/gears/system/oagw/docs/ADR/0004-rate-limiting.md#L527-L569) (Example 1): the system allocates to the partner, and the partner allocates to its tenants.
    > **Partner level** (allocated 5000/min by system): … **Tenant level** (allocated 1000/min by partner):
- **What the code does:**
  - [`budget.rs:96-123`](https://github.com/constructorfabric/gears-rust/blob/66da80e5715e10c08c6fafd5e9832137389a3355/gears/system/oagw/oagw/src/domain/services/management/budget.rs#L96-L123): the check sums `rate_per_second` over every descendant in the ancestor's whole subtree that has the alias. The descendant query uses default options, which means unlimited depth ([`models.rs:292-293`](https://github.com/constructorfabric/gears-rust/blob/66da80e5715e10c08c6fafd5e9832137389a3355/gears/system/tenant-resolver/tenant-resolver-sdk/src/models.rs#L292-L293)). A partner's own children are therefore counted again against the system budget.
- **Evidence of intent:** none. The tests cover two levels only.
- **Why this decision:** the accepted ADR-0004 applies the sum rule at each level, parent to its direct children.
- **Impact:** some valid three-level allocations that are rejected today will be accepted. Not breaking. Follow-ups: [`budget.rs:96-123`](https://github.com/constructorfabric/gears-rust/blob/66da80e5715e10c08c6fafd5e9832137389a3355/gears/system/oagw/oagw/src/domain/services/management/budget.rs#L96-L123); add three-level tests.

### R-16: `F0005` bind and permission rules differ from the code

- **Decision:** `FIX-DOC`, no priority. The docs follow the refactored bind rules: `private` shadowing needs no permission, a missing permission is an explicit error, plugins under `enforce` need `add_plugins`, and create returns the stored row.
- **What the docs say:**
  - [`F0005:128-129`](https://github.com/constructorfabric/gears-rust/blob/66da80e5715e10c08c6fafd5e9832137389a3355/gears/system/oagw/docs/features/0005-cpt-cf-oagw-feature-tenant-hierarchy.md#L128-L129)
    > 2. [x] - `p2` - **IF** ancestor field sharing is `private` - `inst-bind-6b` … 1. [x] - `p2` - **RETURN** 400 ValidationError: field not visible (sharing: private) - `inst-bind-6b1`
  - [`F0005:126-127`](https://github.com/constructorfabric/gears-rust/blob/66da80e5715e10c08c6fafd5e9832137389a3355/gears/system/oagw/docs/features/0005-cpt-cf-oagw-feature-tenant-hierarchy.md#L126-L127)
    > 1. [x] - `p2` - **IF** ancestor field sharing is `enforce` - `inst-bind-6a` … 1. [x] - `p2` - **RETURN** 400 ValidationError: field cannot be overridden (sharing: enforce) - `inst-bind-6a1`
  - [`F0005:263`](https://github.com/constructorfabric/gears-rust/blob/66da80e5715e10c08c6fafd5e9832137389a3355/gears/system/oagw/docs/features/0005-cpt-cf-oagw-feature-tenant-hierarchy.md#L263) and [`F0005:267`](https://github.com/constructorfabric/gears-rust/blob/66da80e5715e10c08c6fafd5e9832137389a3355/gears/system/oagw/docs/features/0005-cpt-cf-oagw-feature-tenant-hierarchy.md#L267): the permission is checked only when the ancestor is `inherit`.
    > 1. [x] - `p2` - Check token has `oagw:upstream:add_plugins` AND ancestor plugins sharing is `inherit` - `inst-perm-5a`
  - [`F0005:339`](https://github.com/constructorfabric/gears-rust/blob/66da80e5715e10c08c6fafd5e9832137389a3355/gears/system/oagw/docs/features/0005-cpt-cf-oagw-feature-tenant-hierarchy.md#L339) (DoD)
    > Without appropriate permissions, descendants **MUST** use ancestor configuration as-is even with `sharing: inherit`.
  - [`DESIGN.md:565`](https://github.com/constructorfabric/gears-rust/blob/66da80e5715e10c08c6fafd5e9832137389a3355/gears/system/oagw/docs/DESIGN.md#L565)
    > Without appropriate permissions, descendant must use ancestor's configuration as-is (even with `sharing: inherit`).
  - [`F0005:150`](https://github.com/constructorfabric/gears-rust/blob/66da80e5715e10c08c6fafd5e9832137389a3355/gears/system/oagw/docs/features/0005-cpt-cf-oagw-feature-tenant-hierarchy.md#L150)
    > 13. [x] - `p2` - **RETURN** created upstream binding with effective configuration - `inst-bind-13`
- **What the code does:**
  - A `private` ancestor field can be shadowed with no permission ([`bind.rs:78-81`](https://github.com/constructorfabric/gears-rust/blob/66da80e5715e10c08c6fafd5e9832137389a3355/gears/system/oagw/oagw/src/domain/services/management/bind.rs#L78-L81), [`bind.rs:105`](https://github.com/constructorfabric/gears-rust/blob/66da80e5715e10c08c6fafd5e9832137389a3355/gears/system/oagw/oagw/src/domain/services/management/bind.rs#L105)).
  - The permission is also required when the ancestor field is absent ([`bind.rs:82-93`](https://github.com/constructorfabric/gears-rust/blob/66da80e5715e10c08c6fafd5e9832137389a3355/gears/system/oagw/oagw/src/domain/services/management/bind.rs#L82-L93)).
  - Plugins under `enforce` are allowed and need `add_plugins` ([`bind.rs:120-136`](https://github.com/constructorfabric/gears-rust/blob/66da80e5715e10c08c6fafd5e9832137389a3355/gears/system/oagw/oagw/src/domain/services/management/bind.rs#L120-L136)).
  - A missing permission is an error from the policy enforcer, not a silent fallback to the ancestor's config.
  - Only the closest ancestor with the alias is checked ([`bind.rs:218`](https://github.com/constructorfabric/gears-rust/blob/66da80e5715e10c08c6fafd5e9832137389a3355/gears/system/oagw/oagw/src/domain/services/management/bind.rs#L218)).
  - Create returns the stored row, not the effective config ([`mod.rs:153-156`](https://github.com/constructorfabric/gears-rust/blob/66da80e5715e10c08c6fafd5e9832137389a3355/gears/system/oagw/oagw/src/domain/services/management/mod.rs#L153-L156)).
- **Evidence of intent:** the refactor was deliberate: control-plane refactor [`c4de73b22`](https://github.com/constructorfabric/gears-rust/commit/c4de73b22add1528a7c73f99165f489b9548d7dc), merged in [`320f0c595`](https://github.com/constructorfabric/gears-rust/commit/320f0c595c261eca81fe267a2720a7656fe52c6c). The Rust tests [`bind_allows_rate_limit_override_on_private`](https://github.com/constructorfabric/gears-rust/blob/66da80e5715e10c08c6fafd5e9832137389a3355/gears/system/oagw/oagw/src/domain/services/management/tests.rs#L1698-L1725) and [`bind_allows_plugins_override_on_enforce`](https://github.com/constructorfabric/gears-rust/blob/66da80e5715e10c08c6fafd5e9832137389a3355/gears/system/oagw/oagw/src/domain/services/management/tests.rs#L1895-L1929) assert the code's rules. The `F0005` boxes were already `[x]` from the [bulk checkbox sync](#why-the-docs-drifted).
- **Why this decision:** the commit and tests show intent, and `F0005` is delivery tracking, so the doc is wrong. [`DESIGN.md:565`](https://github.com/constructorfabric/gears-rust/blob/66da80e5715e10c08c6fafd5e9832137389a3355/gears/system/oagw/docs/DESIGN.md#L565) is descriptive, not a principle.
- **Impact:** no client-visible change. Not breaking. Follow-ups: rewrite `F0005` `inst-bind-6a1`/`6b1`, `inst-perm-3a`/`5a`, [l.150](https://github.com/constructorfabric/gears-rust/blob/66da80e5715e10c08c6fafd5e9832137389a3355/gears/system/oagw/docs/features/0005-cpt-cf-oagw-feature-tenant-hierarchy.md#L150) and [`DESIGN.md:565`](https://github.com/constructorfabric/gears-rust/blob/66da80e5715e10c08c6fafd5e9832137389a3355/gears/system/oagw/docs/DESIGN.md#L565). The closest-ancestor-only check ([`bind.rs:218`](https://github.com/constructorfabric/gears-rust/blob/66da80e5715e10c08c6fafd5e9832137389a3355/gears/system/oagw/oagw/src/domain/services/management/bind.rs#L218)) is decided under [R-05](#r-05-descendants-and-routes-can-bypass-an-ancestors-enforced-plugins-and-rate-limit).
- **Decided together with:** [R-05](#r-05-descendants-and-routes-can-bypass-an-ancestors-enforced-plugins-and-rate-limit).

### R-17: Rate-limit headers do not follow the IETF draft the docs cite

- **Decision:** `FIX-CODE`, P2. Adopt the IETF `RateLimit` / `RateLimit-Policy` headers (draft-ietf-httpapi-ratelimit-headers) instead of `x-ratelimit-*`.
- **What the docs say:**
  - [`F0006:173`](https://github.com/constructorfabric/gears-rust/blob/66da80e5715e10c08c6fafd5e9832137389a3355/gears/system/oagw/docs/features/0006-cpt-cf-oagw-feature-rate-limiting.md#L173) (`inst-tb-5b`)
    > **RETURN** Deny with `retry_after`, `limit = capacity`, `remaining = 0`,
  - [`F0006:346`](https://github.com/constructorfabric/gears-rust/blob/66da80e5715e10c08c6fafd5e9832137389a3355/gears/system/oagw/docs/features/0006-cpt-cf-oagw-feature-rate-limiting.md#L346) (DoD)
    > The system **MUST** include `X-RateLimit-Limit`, `X-RateLimit-Remaining`, and `X-RateLimit-Reset` response headers on all proxy responses when `response_headers: true` in rate limit config (default: true) per RFC 6585 / draft-ietf-httpapi-ratelimit-headers.
  - [`ADR-0004:664-670`](https://github.com/constructorfabric/gears-rust/blob/66da80e5715e10c08c6fafd5e9832137389a3355/gears/system/oagw/docs/ADR/0004-rate-limiting.md#L664-L670)
    > Response headers follow RFC 6585 / draft-ietf-httpapi-ratelimit-headers: … X-RateLimit-Reset: 1706626800
- **What the code does:**
  - On deny, `remaining` is `floor(tokens)` ([`rate_limit.rs:437`](https://github.com/constructorfabric/gears-rust/blob/66da80e5715e10c08c6fafd5e9832137389a3355/gears/system/oagw/oagw/src/domain/rate_limit.rs#L437)), which is non-zero on a 429 when `cost > 1`.

    ```rust
    let remaining = bucket.tokens.floor().max(0.0) as u64;
    ```
  - The headers are `x-`-prefixed with an epoch reset, matching the ADR example but not the draft ([`service.rs:207-213`](https://github.com/constructorfabric/gears-rust/blob/66da80e5715e10c08c6fafd5e9832137389a3355/gears/system/oagw/oagw/src/infra/proxy/service.rs#L207-L213)).
  - Headers appear only on a success that passed a limit ([`service.rs:207-213`](https://github.com/constructorfabric/gears-rust/blob/66da80e5715e10c08c6fafd5e9832137389a3355/gears/system/oagw/oagw/src/infra/proxy/service.rs#L207-L213)) and on 429 ([`error.rs:522-542`](https://github.com/constructorfabric/gears-rust/blob/66da80e5715e10c08c6fafd5e9832137389a3355/gears/system/oagw/oagw/src/api/rest/error.rs#L522-L542)), not on all proxy responses.
- **Evidence of intent:** today's names are locked by tests, for example [`e2e_rate_limit_429_includes_all_headers`](https://github.com/constructorfabric/gears-rust/blob/66da80e5715e10c08c6fafd5e9832137389a3355/gears/system/oagw/oagw/tests/e2e_smoke_test.rs#L905-L909) and e2e [`test_token_bucket_burst_capacity_and_headers`](https://github.com/constructorfabric/gears-rust/blob/66da80e5715e10c08c6fafd5e9832137389a3355/testing/e2e/suites/oagw/test_rate_limiting.py#L122-L124).
- **Why this decision:** ADR-0004 and `F0006` both cite the IETF draft as the target; the code follows only the ADR's example.
- **Impact:** **Breaking** for clients reading `x-ratelimit-*` ([`service.rs:208-213`](https://github.com/constructorfabric/gears-rust/blob/66da80e5715e10c08c6fafd5e9832137389a3355/gears/system/oagw/oagw/src/infra/proxy/service.rs#L208-L213)); decide in the PR whether to emit both for a deprecation period. Semantics follow the draft: remaining = 0 on deny (fixing [`rate_limit.rs:437`](https://github.com/constructorfabric/gears-rust/blob/66da80e5715e10c08c6fafd5e9832137389a3355/gears/system/oagw/oagw/src/domain/rate_limit.rs#L437)) and reset as delta-seconds, not an epoch. Decide in the PR whether headers go on all proxy responses (DoD) or only on limited routes. Update ADR-0004's example, `F0006` `inst-tb-5b` and the scenarios; update the `response_headers` config if it names headers.

## 7. Streaming protocols and the Rust SDK

This area covers what SSE, WebSocket, gRPC and WebTransport callers see on the proxy path, and how the Rust SDK (`oagw-sdk`) matches it.

Mechanical doc fixes in this area: [STR-03](#other-doc-only-fixes-streaming-sdk-and-observability), [STR-04](#status-markers-checkbox-sync), [STR-11](#other-doc-only-fixes-streaming-sdk-and-observability), [STR-12](#config-reference) (see [Mechanical doc fixes](#mechanical-doc-fixes)).

### STR-01: A mid-stream upstream failure appends Problem JSON to a 200 SSE stream (*Likely*)

- **Decision:** `FIX-CODE`, P1. Once the response has started, `fail_to_proxy` must not write a body; it aborts the stream instead.
- **What the docs say:**
  - [`F0007:107`](https://github.com/constructorfabric/gears-rust/blob/66da80e5715e10c08c6fafd5e9832137389a3355/gears/system/oagw/docs/features/0007-cpt-cf-oagw-feature-streaming.md#L107)
    > [x] - `p1` - **RETURN** 502 StreamAborted with `X-OAGW-Error-Source: gateway` - `inst-sse-11a`
  - [`F0007:312`](https://github.com/constructorfabric/gears-rust/blob/66da80e5715e10c08c6fafd5e9832137389a3355/gears/system/oagw/docs/features/0007-cpt-cf-oagw-feature-streaming.md#L312) (acceptance criterion)
    > [x] Unexpected upstream drop during SSE streaming returns 502 StreamAborted with `X-OAGW-Error-Source: gateway`
- **What the code does:**
  - [Pingora `lib.rs:928`](https://github.com/cloudflare/pingora/blob/faac65b0c2a0bfdbfdc5f13a1591f53f3c15321a/pingora-proxy/src/lib.rs#L928): after the headers are sent, Pingora still calls the gateway's `fail_to_proxy` hook on an upstream read error, read timeout or write error.

    ```rust
    let res = self.inner.fail_to_proxy(&mut session, e, &mut ctx).await;
    ```
  - [`pingora_proxy.rs:867-871`](https://github.com/constructorfabric/gears-rust/blob/66da80e5715e10c08c6fafd5e9832137389a3355/gears/system/oagw/oagw/src/infra/proxy/pingora_proxy.rs#L867-L871): OAGW's `fail_to_proxy` writes a Problem response header and then the body.

    ```rust
    if let Ok(mut resp) = ResponseHeader::build(status, Some(body_bytes.len())) {
        let _ = resp.insert_header("content-type", "application/problem+json");
        let _ = resp.insert_header("x-oagw-error-source", "gateway");
        let _ = session.write_response_header(Box::new(resp), false).await;
        let _ = session.write_response_body(Some(body_bytes), true).await;
    ```
  - [Pingora `v1/server.rs:503-507`](https://github.com/cloudflare/pingora/blob/faac65b0c2a0bfdbfdc5f13a1591f53f3c15321a/pingora-core/src/protocols/http/v1/server.rs#L503-L507): the second header write is dropped with the warning "Respond header is already sent", but the body write is not guarded. The caller sees Problem JSON bytes (`"status":503` or `504`) appended to the event stream, followed by a clean EOF.
  - [`pingora_proxy.rs:777-880`](https://github.com/constructorfabric/gears-rust/blob/66da80e5715e10c08c6fafd5e9832137389a3355/gears/system/oagw/oagw/src/infra/proxy/pingora_proxy.rs#L777-L880): `fail_to_proxy` never checks `session.response_written()`.
- **Evidence of intent:** [`3bf316a33`](https://github.com/constructorfabric/gears-rust/commit/3bf316a33ffb7ee3340d0490f92f684bded83df4) ticked inst-sse-11a and the acceptance criterion without code for the post-header case. No test covers a mid-stream failure.
- **Why this decision:** Writing JSON into a 200 event stream corrupts the client's data, and no document allows it. The feature doc `F0007` asks for a gateway-classified abort; a 502 is impossible once a 200 has gone out, so the code aborts and the doc drops the status.
- **Impact:** SSE clients (for example LLM token streams) see the connection close instead of stray JSON inside the stream. Not breaking. Follow-ups: check `response_written()` in [`pingora_proxy.rs:777-880`](https://github.com/constructorfabric/gears-rust/blob/66da80e5715e10c08c6fafd5e9832137389a3355/gears/system/oagw/oagw/src/infra/proxy/pingora_proxy.rs#L777-L880) (the writes are at [l.870-871](https://github.com/constructorfabric/gears-rust/blob/66da80e5715e10c08c6fafd5e9832137389a3355/gears/system/oagw/oagw/src/infra/proxy/pingora_proxy.rs#L870-L871)); for SSE, close the downstream and classify the abort as `StreamAborted` (see [STR-08](#str-08-stream-aborts-are-never-counted-as-streamaborted-and-streaming-metrics-stop-when-the-headers-arrive)); add a mid-stream RST/timeout test; fix `F0007` [inst-sse-11a](https://github.com/constructorfabric/gears-rust/blob/66da80e5715e10c08c6fafd5e9832137389a3355/gears/system/oagw/docs/features/0007-cpt-cf-oagw-feature-streaming.md#L107) and [AC l.312](https://github.com/constructorfabric/gears-rust/blob/66da80e5715e10c08c6fafd5e9832137389a3355/gears/system/oagw/docs/features/0007-cpt-cf-oagw-feature-streaming.md#L312) (no 502 after a 200).
- **Decided together with:** [STR-07](#str-07-an-abnormal-upstream-websocket-drop-writes-problem-json-bytes-into-the-open-tunnel-likely) (same change), [STR-08](#str-08-stream-aborts-are-never-counted-as-streamaborted-and-streaming-metrics-stop-when-the-headers-arrive). Related: [STR-02](#str-02-sse-and-websocket-streams-are-cut-after-30-s-of-upstream-silence-not-after-the-300-s-idle-timeouts-likely) (the 30 s read timeout is one trigger).

### STR-02: SSE and WebSocket streams are cut after 30 s of upstream silence, not after the 300 s idle timeouts (*Likely*)

- **Decision:** `FIX-CODE`, P1. SSE responses and WebSocket upgrades use the streaming/WS idle timeout as the upstream read timeout.
- **What the docs say:**
  - [`config.rs:43-45`](https://github.com/constructorfabric/gears-rust/blob/66da80e5715e10c08c6fafd5e9832137389a3355/gears/system/oagw/oagw/src/config.rs#L43-L45) (config reference comment)
    > Idle timeout in seconds for SSE streaming connections. … A connection with no data received from upstream for this duration … will be closed. Must be > 0. Default: 300 (5 minutes).
  - [`config.rs:33-35`](https://github.com/constructorfabric/gears-rust/blob/66da80e5715e10c08c6fafd5e9832137389a3355/gears/system/oagw/oagw/src/config.rs#L33-L35)
    > Idle timeout in seconds for WebSocket streaming connections. … A connection with no data in either direction for this duration … will be torn down. Must be > 0. Default: 300 (5 minutes).
  - [`F0007:150`](https://github.com/constructorfabric/gears-rust/blob/66da80e5715e10c08c6fafd5e9832137389a3355/gears/system/oagw/docs/features/0007-cpt-cf-oagw-feature-streaming.md#L150) (inst-ws-11a)
    > Send Close frame (1001 Going Away) to both sides, each announcement bounded independently by `websocket_close_timeout_secs`
  - [scenario 14.4](https://github.com/constructorfabric/gears-rust/blob/66da80e5715e10c08c6fafd5e9832137389a3355/gears/system/oagw/scenarios/protocols/websocket/negative-14.4-ws-connection-idle-timeout-enforced.md#L14-L15)
    > Gateway closes the connection. … Close frame/code is consistent (lock expected behavior).
- **What the code does:**
  - [`gear.rs:113`](https://github.com/constructorfabric/gears-rust/blob/66da80e5715e10c08c6fafd5e9832137389a3355/gears/system/oagw/oagw/src/gear.rs#L113) passes `proxy_timeout_secs` (default 30 s, [`config.rs:117-118`](https://github.com/constructorfabric/gears-rust/blob/66da80e5715e10c08c6fafd5e9832137389a3355/gears/system/oagw/oagw/src/config.rs#L117-L118)) to the proxy, and [`pingora_proxy.rs:643`](https://github.com/constructorfabric/gears-rust/blob/66da80e5715e10c08c6fafd5e9832137389a3355/gears/system/oagw/oagw/src/infra/proxy/pingora_proxy.rs#L643) sets it as the read timeout of every upstream peer.

    ```rust
    peer.options.read_timeout = Some(self.read_timeout);
    ```
  - [Pingora `proxy_h1.rs:39`](https://github.com/cloudflare/pingora/blob/faac65b0c2a0bfdbfdc5f13a1591f53f3c15321a/pingora-proxy/src/proxy_h1.rs#L39) copies that timeout onto the upstream session, and [Pingora `v1/client.rs:438-441`](https://github.com/cloudflare/pingora/blob/faac65b0c2a0bfdbfdc5f13a1591f53f3c15321a/pingora-core/src/protocols/http/v1/client.rs#L438-L441) wraps every upstream body read in it, which includes SSE bodies and upgraded WS bodies.
  - More than 30 s of upstream silence therefore raises `ReadTimedout` and tears the session down, with Problem JSON injected ([STR-01](#str-01-a-mid-stream-upstream-failure-appends-problem-json-to-a-200-sse-stream-likely) for SSE, [STR-07](#str-07-an-abnormal-upstream-websocket-drop-writes-problem-json-bytes-into-the-open-tunnel-likely) for WS). An LLM that pauses more than 30 s between tokens has its SSE stream cut.
- **Evidence of intent:** none. The tests hide the problem: the harness uses a 30 s read timeout ([`test_support.rs:620-625`](https://github.com/constructorfabric/gears-rust/blob/66da80e5715e10c08c6fafd5e9832137389a3355/gears/system/oagw/oagw/src/domain/test_support.rs#L620-L625)) with a 1 s WS idle timeout ([`proxy_integration.rs:2113-2116`](https://github.com/constructorfabric/gears-rust/blob/66da80e5715e10c08c6fafd5e9832137389a3355/gears/system/oagw/oagw/tests/proxy_integration.rs#L2113-L2116)), so the idle timer always fires first. [`proxy_websocket_idle_timeout_closes_connection`](https://github.com/constructorfabric/gears-rust/blob/66da80e5715e10c08c6fafd5e9832137389a3355/gears/system/oagw/oagw/tests/proxy_integration.rs#L2107-L2139) and [`proxy_websocket_idle_timeout_sends_1001`](https://github.com/constructorfabric/gears-rust/blob/66da80e5715e10c08c6fafd5e9832137389a3355/gears/system/oagw/oagw/tests/proxy_integration.rs#L4413-L4456) also count a bare EOF as a pass.
- **Why this decision:** The config reference and `F0007` promise a 300 s idle limit for streams; `proxy_timeout_secs` is a request/response timeout and should not bound a long-lived stream.
- **Impact:** SSE and WS clients with long quiet periods stop being disconnected at 30 s. Not breaking. Follow-ups: confirm with a test first (*Likely*); set the read timeout per request/phase instead of once per peer ([`pingora_proxy.rs:643`](https://github.com/constructorfabric/gears-rust/blob/66da80e5715e10c08c6fafd5e9832137389a3355/gears/system/oagw/oagw/src/infra/proxy/pingora_proxy.rs#L643)); fix the masking tests ([`test_support.rs:620-625`](https://github.com/constructorfabric/gears-rust/blob/66da80e5715e10c08c6fafd5e9832137389a3355/gears/system/oagw/oagw/src/domain/test_support.rs#L620-L625), [`proxy_integration.rs:2107`](https://github.com/constructorfabric/gears-rust/blob/66da80e5715e10c08c6fafd5e9832137389a3355/gears/system/oagw/oagw/tests/proxy_integration.rs#L2107), [`proxy_integration.rs:4413`](https://github.com/constructorfabric/gears-rust/blob/66da80e5715e10c08c6fafd5e9832137389a3355/gears/system/oagw/oagw/tests/proxy_integration.rs#L4413): 1 s idle vs 30 s, and EOF counted as a pass).
- **Decided together with:** related to [STR-01](#str-01-a-mid-stream-upstream-failure-appends-problem-json-to-a-200-sse-stream-likely) and [STR-07](#str-07-an-abnormal-upstream-websocket-drop-writes-problem-json-bytes-into-the-open-tunnel-likely) (what happens when the timeout fires) and [STR-14](#str-14-streaming-tests-pass-without-checking-the-behaviour-they-are-named-after) (weak idle tests).

### STR-05: A WebSocket upgrade to an `https` upstream fails whenever TLS negotiation picks HTTP/2 (*Likely*)

- **Decision:** `FIX-CODE`, P1. Force HTTP/1.1 ALPN for upgrade requests, whatever the scheme.
- **What the docs say:**
  - [`F0007:131`](https://github.com/constructorfabric/gears-rust/blob/66da80e5715e10c08c6fafd5e9832137389a3355/gears/system/oagw/docs/features/0007-cpt-cf-oagw-feature-streaming.md#L131) (inst-ws-3) presents `https` as valid for WebSocket:
    > Initiate WebSocket upgrade handshake with upstream endpoint (HTTPS/WSS-only)
  - [`DESIGN.md:242`](https://github.com/constructorfabric/gears-rust/blob/66da80e5715e10c08c6fafd5e9832137389a3355/gears/system/oagw/docs/DESIGN.md#L242)
    > Tenant-scoped root configuration object representing an external service. Unique per `(tenant_id, alias)`.
- **What the code does:**
  - [`pingora_proxy.rs:170-181`](https://github.com/constructorfabric/gears-rust/blob/66da80e5715e10c08c6fafd5e9832137389a3355/gears/system/oagw/oagw/src/infra/proxy/pingora_proxy.rs#L170-L181): only `wss` is forced to HTTP/1.1. For `https` the gateway offers h2+h1, or h2 only when the protocol cache says so; earlier plain HTTP calls to the same host fill that cache.

    ```rust
    let tls = matches!(ep.scheme, Scheme::Https | Scheme::Wss | Scheme::Wt);
    if tls && !matches!(ep.scheme, Scheme::Wss) {
    // …
                None => pingora_core::protocols::tls::ALPN::H2H1,
    ```
  - [Pingora `proxy_h2.rs:92-99`](https://github.com/cloudflare/pingora/blob/faac65b0c2a0bfdbfdc5f13a1591f53f3c15321a/pingora-proxy/src/proxy_h2.rs#L92-L99): over h2, Pingora strips the headers the upgrade needs, so the upstream never answers 101 and the caller gets a `ProtocolError`.

    ```rust
    req.remove_header(&http::header::CONNECTION);
    req.remove_header(&http::header::UPGRADE);
    ```
  - [`alias.rs:151-166`](https://github.com/constructorfabric/gears-rust/blob/66da80e5715e10c08c6fafd5e9832137389a3355/gears/system/oagw/oagw/src/domain/services/management/alias.rs#L151-L166): for a hostname endpoint the alias is always derived from the host (an explicit alias is rejected), and it is unique per tenant, so one host cannot have both an `https` and a `wss` upstream. A client cannot work around the problem by adding a second upstream.
- **Evidence of intent:** none. All WS tests use cleartext `http` upstreams (for example [`setup_ws_upstream`](https://github.com/constructorfabric/gears-rust/blob/66da80e5715e10c08c6fafd5e9832137389a3355/gears/system/oagw/oagw/tests/proxy_integration.rs#L4308-L4320)).
- **Why this decision:** `F0007` presents `https` as a valid WS scheme, and HTTP/2 cannot carry an HTTP/1.1 `Upgrade`, so every upgrade must negotiate HTTP/1.1.
- **Impact:** WS clients whose upstream is `https` and h2-capable get working upgrades. Not breaking. Follow-ups: change `select_alpn` ([`pingora_proxy.rs:170-181`](https://github.com/constructorfabric/gears-rust/blob/66da80e5715e10c08c6fafd5e9832137389a3355/gears/system/oagw/oagw/src/infra/proxy/pingora_proxy.rs#L170-L181)); add an e2e/integration test with an h2-capable TLS upstream (all current WS tests use cleartext).
- **Decided together with:** related to [STR-14](#str-14-streaming-tests-pass-without-checking-the-behaviour-they-are-named-after) (no WS-over-TLS coverage).

### STR-06: Every rejected WebSocket upgrade returns the same gateway error; the upstream's own 401/403/404 is lost

- **Decision:** `FIX-CODE`, P2. Pass through an upstream's non-101 answer to a WS upgrade (status and body) as `ErrorSource::Upstream`; Pingora-synthesised connect/TLS/timeout failures stay gateway errors.
- **What the docs say:**
  - [`F0007:135`](https://github.com/constructorfabric/gears-rust/blob/66da80e5715e10c08c6fafd5e9832137389a3355/gears/system/oagw/docs/features/0007-cpt-cf-oagw-feature-streaming.md#L135) (inst-ws-5a)
    > **RETURN** 502 ProtocolError with `X-OAGW-Error-Source: gateway` — upstream rejected WebSocket upgrade
  - [`F0007:321`](https://github.com/constructorfabric/gears-rust/blob/66da80e5715e10c08c6fafd5e9832137389a3355/gears/system/oagw/docs/features/0007-cpt-cf-oagw-feature-streaming.md#L321) (acceptance criterion)
    > [x] Upstream rejection of WebSocket upgrade returns 502 ProtocolError with `X-OAGW-Error-Source: gateway`
- **What the code does:**
  - [`service.rs:947-952`](https://github.com/constructorfabric/gears-rust/blob/66da80e5715e10c08c6fafd5e9832137389a3355/gears/system/oagw/oagw/src/infra/proxy/service.rs#L947-L952): any status other than 101 becomes a gateway `ProtocolError`. This includes the 503 (connect refused), 504 and TLS failures that Pingora itself writes into the bridge, and the upstream's own 401/403/404; its status and body are discarded.

    ```rust
    if status != http::StatusCode::SWITCHING_PROTOCOLS {
        return Err(DomainError::ProtocolError {
            detail: format!("upstream rejected WebSocket upgrade with status {status}"),
    ```
  - [`error.rs:250-254`](https://github.com/constructorfabric/gears-rust/blob/66da80e5715e10c08c6fafd5e9832137389a3355/gears/system/oagw/oagw/src/api/rest/error.rs#L250-L254): `ProtocolError` maps to 503 today; [PR #4911](https://github.com/constructorfabric/gears-rust/pull/4911) realigns protocol errors to 502.
- **Evidence of intent:** [`proxy_websocket_upgrade_rejected_returns_503_protocol_error`](https://github.com/constructorfabric/gears-rust/blob/66da80e5715e10c08c6fafd5e9832137389a3355/gears/system/oagw/oagw/tests/proxy_integration.rs#L1781-L1849) pins the 503 (`CanonicalError::ServiceUnavailable`). The e2e [`test_websocket_upgrade_rejected_by_upstream`](https://github.com/constructorfabric/gears-rust/blob/66da80e5715e10c08c6fafd5e9832137389a3355/testing/e2e/suites/oagw/test_websocket.py#L96-L127) only asserts that the status is not 101.
- **Why this decision:** The PRD scope item [`PRD.md:178`](https://github.com/constructorfabric/gears-rust/blob/66da80e5715e10c08c6fafd5e9832137389a3355/gears/system/oagw/docs/PRD.md#L178) “Error source distinction (gateway errors vs upstream errors)” outranks the feature doc: an upstream's refusal is an upstream answer, and a client needs its 401/403 to fix credentials.
- **Impact:** Breaking for clients: when the upstream refuses the upgrade, the caller gets the upstream's status and body with `X-OAGW-Error-Source: upstream` instead of a gateway 503 (502 after #4911). Follow-ups: FIX-DOC `F0007` [inst-ws-5a](https://github.com/constructorfabric/gears-rust/blob/66da80e5715e10c08c6fafd5e9832137389a3355/gears/system/oagw/docs/features/0007-cpt-cf-oagw-feature-streaming.md#L135) and [AC l.321](https://github.com/constructorfabric/gears-rust/blob/66da80e5715e10c08c6fafd5e9832137389a3355/gears/system/oagw/docs/features/0007-cpt-cf-oagw-feature-streaming.md#L321); tighten [`test_websocket.py:96-130`](https://github.com/constructorfabric/gears-rust/blob/66da80e5715e10c08c6fafd5e9832137389a3355/testing/e2e/suites/oagw/test_websocket.py#L96-L130) (asserts only `!= 101`) and the Rust test that pins 503.

### STR-07: An abnormal upstream WebSocket drop writes Problem JSON bytes into the open tunnel (*Likely*)

- **Decision:** `FIX-CODE`, P1. The same guard applies to upgraded WS tunnels: send a Close frame or drop the connection, never raw JSON.
- **What the docs say:**
  - [`F0007:144`](https://github.com/constructorfabric/gears-rust/blob/66da80e5715e10c08c6fafd5e9832137389a3355/gears/system/oagw/docs/features/0007-cpt-cf-oagw-feature-streaming.md#L144) (inst-ws-9a)
    > Half-close the caller's leg so it observes EOF with no Close frame; the caller's own stack reports 1006 Abnormal Closure, which the gateway does not synthesise
  - [`F0007:318`](https://github.com/constructorfabric/gears-rust/blob/66da80e5715e10c08c6fafd5e9832137389a3355/gears/system/oagw/docs/features/0007-cpt-cf-oagw-feature-streaming.md#L318) (acceptance criterion)
    > [x] Upstream WebSocket drop half-closes the caller's leg, so the caller sees EOF and reports 1006 Abnormal Closure itself
- **What the code does:**
  - A clean FIN from the upstream behaves as documented.
  - An RST, a read/write error, or the 30 s read timeout ([STR-02](#str-02-sse-and-websocket-streams-are-cut-after-30-s-of-upstream-silence-not-after-the-300-s-idle-timeouts-likely)) reaches the same `fail_to_proxy` as in [STR-01](#str-01-a-mid-stream-upstream-failure-appends-problem-json-to-a-200-sse-stream-likely), which maps it to a Problem ([`pingora_proxy.rs:790-825`](https://github.com/constructorfabric/gears-rust/blob/66da80e5715e10c08c6fafd5e9832137389a3355/gears/system/oagw/oagw/src/infra/proxy/pingora_proxy.rs#L790-L825)) and writes the JSON raw into the upgraded stream ([`pingora_proxy.rs:867-871`](https://github.com/constructorfabric/gears-rust/blob/66da80e5715e10c08c6fafd5e9832137389a3355/gears/system/oagw/oagw/src/infra/proxy/pingora_proxy.rs#L867-L871)). The caller's WebSocket parser then fails with 1002 instead of seeing EOF.
- **Evidence of intent:** the opaque relay rewrite [`9dc35c008`](https://github.com/constructorfabric/gears-rust/commit/9dc35c008690d8092c475a878545f30e43ec2fa5) did not audit Pingora's error path. No test covers an abnormal upstream drop.
- **Why this decision:** `F0007` inst-ws-9a is explicit that the caller sees EOF with no synthesised bytes, and nothing contradicts it.
- **Impact:** WS clients see a clean 1006 instead of a protocol error on upstream failure. Not breaking. Follow-ups: same change as STR-01; add a test for an abnormal upstream drop (RST) that expects no garbage bytes.
- **Decided together with:** [STR-01](#str-01-a-mid-stream-upstream-failure-appends-problem-json-to-a-200-sse-stream-likely) (same change), [STR-08](#str-08-stream-aborts-are-never-counted-as-streamaborted-and-streaming-metrics-stop-when-the-headers-arrive).

### STR-08: Stream aborts are never counted as `StreamAborted`, and streaming metrics stop when the headers arrive

- **Decision:** `FIX-CODE`, P2. Streaming metrics complete at stream end; aborts are classified as `StreamAborted`.
- **What the docs say:**
  - [INDEX:757-759](https://github.com/constructorfabric/gears-rust/blob/66da80e5715e10c08c6fafd5e9832137389a3355/gears/system/oagw/scenarios/INDEX.md#L757-L759)
    > Stream aborted classified distinctly … Abort SSE/WS mid-flight → classified as `StreamAborted`.
  - [scenario 13.2](https://github.com/constructorfabric/gears-rust/blob/66da80e5715e10c08c6fafd5e9832137389a3355/gears/system/oagw/scenarios/protocols/sse/positive-13.2-client-disconnect-aborts-upstream-stream.md#L14-L18)
    > Gateway closes/cancels upstream stream. … in-flight metrics decrease … If an error is produced, it is classified as `StreamAborted`.
- **What the code does:**
  - [`service.rs:881-886`](https://github.com/constructorfabric/gears-rust/blob/66da80e5715e10c08c6fafd5e9832137389a3355/gears/system/oagw/oagw/src/infra/proxy/service.rs#L881-L886): the request metrics guard records success as soon as `proxy_request` returns the response headers.

    ```rust
    match &result {
        Ok(resp) => guard.set_success(resp.status().as_u16()),
        Err(err) => guard.set_error(domain_error_type_name(err)),
    }
    ```
  - [`service.rs:495-519`](https://github.com/constructorfabric/gears-rust/blob/66da80e5715e10c08c6fafd5e9832137389a3355/gears/system/oagw/oagw/src/infra/proxy/service.rs#L495-L519): the guard's `Drop` then decrements the in-flight gauge and records the duration, while the SSE body is still streaming.
  - [`websocket.rs:579-586`](https://github.com/constructorfabric/gears-rust/blob/66da80e5715e10c08c6fafd5e9832137389a3355/gears/system/oagw/oagw/src/infra/proxy/websocket.rs#L579-L586): an unexpected upstream WS drop is only a `warn!` log line; no metric or error class records it.
- **Evidence of intent:** none.
- **Why this decision:** The scenario and INDEX define the expected observability, and no higher document disagrees; metrics that end at header time misreport every long stream.
- **Impact:** Operators see SSE request durations and the in-flight gauge cover the whole stream, and aborts show up as `StreamAborted` errors; dashboards built on header-time durations will shift. Not breaking for clients. Follow-ups: change `RequestMetricsGuard` ([`service.rs:456-519`](https://github.com/constructorfabric/gears-rust/blob/66da80e5715e10c08c6fafd5e9832137389a3355/gears/system/oagw/oagw/src/infra/proxy/service.rs#L456-L519), [`service.rs:881-886`](https://github.com/constructorfabric/gears-rust/blob/66da80e5715e10c08c6fafd5e9832137389a3355/gears/system/oagw/oagw/src/infra/proxy/service.rs#L881-L886)) and the WS abort path ([`websocket.rs:579-586`](https://github.com/constructorfabric/gears-rust/blob/66da80e5715e10c08c6fafd5e9832137389a3355/gears/system/oagw/oagw/src/infra/proxy/websocket.rs#L579-L586)); [scenario 13.2](https://github.com/constructorfabric/gears-rust/blob/66da80e5715e10c08c6fafd5e9832137389a3355/gears/system/oagw/scenarios/protocols/sse/positive-13.2-client-disconnect-aborts-upstream-stream.md#L14-L18) and [INDEX:757-759](https://github.com/constructorfabric/gears-rust/blob/66da80e5715e10c08c6fafd5e9832137389a3355/gears/system/oagw/scenarios/INDEX.md#L757-L759) are the acceptance tests.
- **Decided together with:** [STR-01](#str-01-a-mid-stream-upstream-failure-appends-problem-json-to-a-200-sse-stream-likely), [STR-07](#str-07-an-abnormal-upstream-websocket-drop-writes-problem-json-bytes-into-the-open-tunnel-likely).

### STR-09: gRPC and WebTransport configs are accepted, but gRPC traffic never works and WebTransport is proxied as plain HTTPS

- **Decision:** `FUTURE` + `FIX-CODE`, P2. gRPC and WebTransport are planned. Until then, the API rejects their config at write time.
- **What the docs say:**
  - [INDEX:44](https://github.com/constructorfabric/gears-rust/blob/66da80e5715e10c08c6fafd5e9832137389a3355/gears/system/oagw/scenarios/INDEX.md#L44) (How-To, step 2)
    > For gRPC: [Create gRPC route by service+method]
  - [INDEX:343-345](https://github.com/constructorfabric/gears-rust/blob/66da80e5715e10c08c6fafd5e9832137389a3355/gears/system/oagw/scenarios/INDEX.md#L343-L345)
    > gRPC unary request proxied (native) … `content-type: application/grpc*` detection routes to gRPC handler. Metadata headers preserved.
  - [scenario 17.1](https://github.com/constructorfabric/gears-rust/blob/66da80e5715e10c08c6fafd5e9832137389a3355/gears/system/oagw/scenarios/protocols/webtransport/positive-17.1-wt-session-establishment-auth.md#L23-L25)
    > If WebTransport is not supported: … Gateway returns `502 ProtocolError` (or equivalent), `application/problem+json`, `X-OAGW-Error-Source: gateway`. … Failure mode is stable and documented.
  - [INDEX:361](https://github.com/constructorfabric/gears-rust/blob/66da80e5715e10c08c6fafd5e9832137389a3355/gears/system/oagw/scenarios/INDEX.md#L361)
    > Upstream scheme `wt` accepted. Future-facing — if not implemented yet, `502 ProtocolError` with `ESrc=gateway`.
  - Also scenarios [3.8](https://github.com/constructorfabric/gears-rust/blob/66da80e5715e10c08c6fafd5e9832137389a3355/gears/system/oagw/scenarios/management-api/routes/positive-3.8-create-grpc-route-service-method.md), [15.1](https://github.com/constructorfabric/gears-rust/blob/66da80e5715e10c08c6fafd5e9832137389a3355/gears/system/oagw/scenarios/protocols/grpc/positive-15.1-grpc-unary-request-proxied.md)-[15.4](https://github.com/constructorfabric/gears-rust/blob/66da80e5715e10c08c6fafd5e9832137389a3355/gears/system/oagw/scenarios/protocols/grpc/negative-15.4-grpc-status-mapping-error-source.md) and [`grpc/examples/positive-example-04`](https://github.com/constructorfabric/gears-rust/blob/66da80e5715e10c08c6fafd5e9832137389a3355/gears/system/oagw/scenarios/protocols/grpc/examples/positive-example-04-grpc-unary-proxy.md).
  - Consistent docs: [`ADR-0014:57`](https://github.com/constructorfabric/gears-rust/blob/66da80e5715e10c08c6fafd5e9832137389a3355/gears/system/oagw/docs/ADR/0014-grpc-support.md#L57) (status `proposed`)
    > **Note:** gRPC proxy support is not implemented yet and is targeted for Phase 3; current OAGW proxy paths do not route gRPC.
  - [`DESIGN.md:243`](https://github.com/constructorfabric/gears-rust/blob/66da80e5715e10c08c6fafd5e9832137389a3355/gears/system/oagw/docs/DESIGN.md#L243) (also l.706 and l.866)
    > gRPC service/method matching is planned for Phase 3 — no gRPC proxy code path is currently implemented or reachable
  - [`F0007:155`](https://github.com/constructorfabric/gears-rust/blob/66da80e5715e10c08c6fafd5e9832137389a3355/gears/system/oagw/docs/features/0007-cpt-cf-oagw-feature-streaming.md#L155): the WebTransport flow is `p2` and unticked.
    > - [ ] `p2` - **ID**: `cpt-cf-oagw-flow-streaming-webtransport`
  - [`PRD.md:187`](https://github.com/constructorfabric/gears-rust/blob/66da80e5715e10c08c6fafd5e9832137389a3355/gears/system/oagw/docs/PRD.md#L187) (out of scope)
    > gRPC proxying (planned for phase 4)
  - [`PRD.md:306`](https://github.com/constructorfabric/gears-rust/blob/66da80e5715e10c08c6fafd5e9832137389a3355/gears/system/oagw/docs/PRD.md#L306) (`p1`)
    > The system **MUST** support WebSocket and WebTransport session flows.
- **What the code does:**
  - [`validation.rs:11-23`](https://github.com/constructorfabric/gears-rust/blob/66da80e5715e10c08c6fafd5e9832137389a3355/gears/system/oagw/oagw/src/domain/services/management/validation.rs#L11-L23): a route with `grpc` match rules is accepted at create time.
  - [`route_repo.rs:131-134`](https://github.com/constructorfabric/gears-rust/blob/66da80e5715e10c08c6fafd5e9832137389a3355/gears/system/oagw/oagw/src/infra/storage/route_repo.rs#L131-L134): matching skips every route without HTTP rules, so gRPC routes always answer 404.

    ```rust
    let Some(http_match) = &route.match_rules.http else {
        continue;
    };
    ```
  - [`service.rs:775-789`](https://github.com/constructorfabric/gears-rust/blob/66da80e5715e10c08c6fafd5e9832137389a3355/gears/system/oagw/oagw/src/infra/proxy/service.rs#L775-L789): the upstream URL is built only after the auth plugin and rate limiting have run.
  - [`request_builder.rs:20-28`](https://github.com/constructorfabric/gears-rust/blob/66da80e5715e10c08c6fafd5e9832137389a3355/gears/system/oagw/oagw/src/infra/proxy/request_builder.rs#L20-L28): an endpoint with `scheme=grpc` then fails with 400 `UNSUPPORTED_SCHEME`, and `scheme=wt` is proxied as plain `https`, with no CONNECT and no 502.

    ```rust
    Scheme::Wt => "https",
    Scheme::Grpc => {
        return Err(DomainError::Validation {
            field: "endpoint.scheme",
            reason: field::UNSUPPORTED_SCHEME,
    ```
- **Evidence of intent:** [`f9bc81e32`](https://github.com/constructorfabric/gears-rust/commit/f9bc81e323c3f2cb0efcbe369b70f85ebaf3a1ad) added the "not yet implemented / Phase 3" notes to ADR-0014 and DESIGN. Nothing tests gRPC or WebTransport traffic.
- **Why this decision:** The PRD puts gRPC out of scope ("planned for phase 4") and ADR-0014 is still `proposed`, so gRPC is future work. For WebTransport the PRD `p1` MUST at l.306 still applies, so the FUTURE marker needs product sign-off. Rejecting the config at write time turns silent misbehaviour into a clear error.
- **Impact:** Breaking for clients: creating or updating a route with `grpc` match rules, or an upstream with a `grpc` or `wt` endpoint scheme, returns 400 "not supported" (today it is accepted, then fails with 404 at match time, 400 after auth and rate limiting, or `wt` is proxied as `https`). Follow-ups: FIX-CODE 400 "not supported" for gRPC match rules ([`validation.rs:11-23`](https://github.com/constructorfabric/gears-rust/blob/66da80e5715e10c08c6fafd5e9832137389a3355/gears/system/oagw/oagw/src/domain/services/management/validation.rs#L11-L23)) and `grpc`/`wt` endpoint schemes on create/update; FIX-DOC future-facing markers on scenarios [15.1](https://github.com/constructorfabric/gears-rust/blob/66da80e5715e10c08c6fafd5e9832137389a3355/gears/system/oagw/scenarios/protocols/grpc/positive-15.1-grpc-unary-request-proxied.md), [15.2](https://github.com/constructorfabric/gears-rust/blob/66da80e5715e10c08c6fafd5e9832137389a3355/gears/system/oagw/scenarios/protocols/grpc/positive-15.2-grpc-server-streaming-proxied.md), [15.3](https://github.com/constructorfabric/gears-rust/blob/66da80e5715e10c08c6fafd5e9832137389a3355/gears/system/oagw/scenarios/protocols/grpc/positive-15.3-grpc-json-transcoding-http-clients.md), [15.4](https://github.com/constructorfabric/gears-rust/blob/66da80e5715e10c08c6fafd5e9832137389a3355/gears/system/oagw/scenarios/protocols/grpc/negative-15.4-grpc-status-mapping-error-source.md), [3.8](https://github.com/constructorfabric/gears-rust/blob/66da80e5715e10c08c6fafd5e9832137389a3355/gears/system/oagw/scenarios/management-api/routes/positive-3.8-create-grpc-route-service-method.md), [17.1](https://github.com/constructorfabric/gears-rust/blob/66da80e5715e10c08c6fafd5e9832137389a3355/gears/system/oagw/scenarios/protocols/webtransport/positive-17.1-wt-session-establishment-auth.md), [`grpc/examples/positive-example-04`](https://github.com/constructorfabric/gears-rust/blob/66da80e5715e10c08c6fafd5e9832137389a3355/gears/system/oagw/scenarios/protocols/grpc/examples/positive-example-04-grpc-unary-proxy.md) and [INDEX:44](https://github.com/constructorfabric/gears-rust/blob/66da80e5715e10c08c6fafd5e9832137389a3355/gears/system/oagw/scenarios/INDEX.md#L44), [INDEX:341-361](https://github.com/constructorfabric/gears-rust/blob/66da80e5715e10c08c6fafd5e9832137389a3355/gears/system/oagw/scenarios/INDEX.md#L341-L361). See [M-04](#m-04-the-upstream-protocol-field-accepts-any-string-and-is-never-used) (`protocol` validation).
- **Decided together with:** [M-04](#m-04-the-upstream-protocol-field-accepts-any-string-and-is-never-used).

### STR-10: ADR-0015 describes a Rust client library that does not exist

- **Decision:** `ADR` → `FIX-DOC`, no priority. ADR-0015 is superseded; a new short ADR records the SDK as built.
- **What the docs say:**
  - [`ADR-0015:2`](https://github.com/constructorfabric/gears-rust/blob/66da80e5715e10c08c6fafd5e9832137389a3355/gears/system/oagw/docs/ADR/0015-rust-abi-client-library.md#L2)
    > status: proposed
  - [`ADR-0015:108-115`](https://github.com/constructorfabric/gears-rust/blob/66da80e5715e10c08c6fafd5e9832137389a3355/gears/system/oagw/docs/ADR/0015-rust-abi-client-library.md#L108-L115) (the core API)

    ```rust
    pub struct OagwClient { inner: OagwClientImpl }
    impl OagwClient {
        pub fn from_config(config: OagwClientConfig) -> Result<Self, ClientError>;
        pub async fn execute(&self, alias: &str, req: Request) -> Result<Response, ClientError>;
        pub async fn websocket(&self, alias: &str, req: Request) -> Result<WebSocketConn, ClientError>;
        pub fn execute_blocking(&self, alias: &str, req: Request) -> Result<Response, ClientError>;
    ```
  - [`ADR-0015:143`](https://github.com/constructorfabric/gears-rust/blob/66da80e5715e10c08c6fafd5e9832137389a3355/gears/system/oagw/docs/ADR/0015-rust-abi-client-library.md#L143)
    > Good, because type-safe — single concrete type, no trait objects
  - [`ADR-0015:1605`](https://github.com/constructorfabric/gears-rust/blob/66da80e5715e10c08c6fafd5e9832137389a3355/gears/system/oagw/docs/ADR/0015-rust-abi-client-library.md#L1605) (its own traceability line contradicts that)
    > `cpt-cf-oagw-interface-sdk-client` — Public Rust trait for inter-gear communication
  - Other types the ADR describes that do not exist: `OagwClientConfig::from_env` with `OAGW_MODE`/`OAGW_BASE_URL` ([l.546-552](https://github.com/constructorfabric/gears-rust/blob/66da80e5715e10c08c6fafd5e9832137389a3355/gears/system/oagw/docs/ADR/0015-rust-abi-client-library.md#L546-L552), [l.893-916](https://github.com/constructorfabric/gears-rust/blob/66da80e5715e10c08c6fafd5e9832137389a3355/gears/system/oagw/docs/ADR/0015-rust-abi-client-library.md#L893-L916)), `RemoteProxyClient` over reqwest ([l.397-400](https://github.com/constructorfabric/gears-rust/blob/66da80e5715e10c08c6fafd5e9832137389a3355/gears/system/oagw/docs/ADR/0015-rust-abi-client-library.md#L397-L400)), `BlockingClient` ([l.441](https://github.com/constructorfabric/gears-rust/blob/66da80e5715e10c08c6fafd5e9832137389a3355/gears/system/oagw/docs/ADR/0015-rust-abi-client-library.md#L441)), `Request`/`RequestBuilder` ([l.560-587](https://github.com/constructorfabric/gears-rust/blob/66da80e5715e10c08c6fafd5e9832137389a3355/gears/system/oagw/docs/ADR/0015-rust-abi-client-library.md#L560-L587)), `Response.bytes/json/text/into_sse_stream` ([l.125](https://github.com/constructorfabric/gears-rust/blob/66da80e5715e10c08c6fafd5e9832137389a3355/gears/system/oagw/docs/ADR/0015-rust-abi-client-library.md#L125)) and `SseEventStream` ([l.654-659](https://github.com/constructorfabric/gears-rust/blob/66da80e5715e10c08c6fafd5e9832137389a3355/gears/system/oagw/docs/ADR/0015-rust-abi-client-library.md#L654-L659)), `ErrorSource::Unknown` ([l.732](https://github.com/constructorfabric/gears-rust/blob/66da80e5715e10c08c6fafd5e9832137389a3355/gears/system/oagw/docs/ADR/0015-rust-abi-client-library.md#L732)), `WebSocketConn`/`WsMessage` ([l.735-737](https://github.com/constructorfabric/gears-rust/blob/66da80e5715e10c08c6fafd5e9832137389a3355/gears/system/oagw/docs/ADR/0015-rust-abi-client-library.md#L735-L737)), `ClientError` ([l.810](https://github.com/constructorfabric/gears-rust/blob/66da80e5715e10c08c6fafd5e9832137389a3355/gears/system/oagw/docs/ADR/0015-rust-abi-client-library.md#L810)) and a mock client ([l.54](https://github.com/constructorfabric/gears-rust/blob/66da80e5715e10c08c6fafd5e9832137389a3355/gears/system/oagw/docs/ADR/0015-rust-abi-client-library.md#L54)).
  - [`DESIGN.md:946`](https://github.com/constructorfabric/gears-rust/blob/66da80e5715e10c08c6fafd5e9832137389a3355/gears/system/oagw/docs/DESIGN.md#L946) still lists the ADR under "Review":
    > ADR: Rust ABI / Client Libraries … HTTP client abstractions, streaming support, plugin development APIs
- **What the code does:**
  - [`api.rs:62-63`](https://github.com/constructorfabric/gears-rust/blob/66da80e5715e10c08c6fafd5e9832137389a3355/gears/system/oagw/oagw-sdk/src/api.rs#L62-L63): the SDK is the `ServiceGatewayClientV1` trait, and every method returns `CanonicalError` on failure.

    ```rust
    #[async_trait]
    pub trait ServiceGatewayClientV1: Send + Sync {
    ```
  - [`api.rs:152-156`](https://github.com/constructorfabric/gears-rust/blob/66da80e5715e10c08c6fafd5e9832137389a3355/gears/system/oagw/oagw-sdk/src/api.rs#L152-L156): proxying takes and returns `http::Request/Response<oagw_sdk::Body>`.
  - Streaming and helpers: [`ServerEventsStream`](https://github.com/constructorfabric/gears-rust/blob/66da80e5715e10c08c6fafd5e9832137389a3355/gears/system/oagw/oagw-sdk/src/sse/stream.rs#L74)/`ServerEvent`, [`WebSocketStream`](https://github.com/constructorfabric/gears-rust/blob/66da80e5715e10c08c6fafd5e9832137389a3355/gears/system/oagw/oagw-sdk/src/ws/stream.rs#L78), [`ServiceGatewayError`](https://github.com/constructorfabric/gears-rust/blob/66da80e5715e10c08c6fafd5e9832137389a3355/gears/system/oagw/oagw-sdk/src/error.rs#L86)/[`StreamingError`](https://github.com/constructorfabric/gears-rust/blob/66da80e5715e10c08c6fafd5e9832137389a3355/gears/system/oagw/oagw-sdk/src/error.rs#L366), the [`Json`](https://github.com/constructorfabric/gears-rust/blob/66da80e5715e10c08c6fafd5e9832137389a3355/gears/system/oagw/oagw-sdk/src/codec.rs#L42) codec, [`MultipartBody`](https://github.com/constructorfabric/gears-rust/blob/66da80e5715e10c08c6fafd5e9832137389a3355/gears/system/oagw/oagw-sdk/src/multipart.rs#L161) and the [`axum` feature](https://github.com/constructorfabric/gears-rust/blob/66da80e5715e10c08c6fafd5e9832137389a3355/gears/system/oagw/oagw-sdk/Cargo.toml#L21). [`ErrorSource`](https://github.com/constructorfabric/gears-rust/blob/66da80e5715e10c08c6fafd5e9832137389a3355/gears/system/oagw/oagw-sdk/src/api.rs#L20-L23) has only `Gateway` and `Upstream`.
- **Evidence of intent:** the ADR comes from the pre-implementation design proposal (commits [`14ef3e26e`](https://github.com/constructorfabric/gears-rust/commit/14ef3e26e3d0660cccbe8f7e8463d5fa3254c0e3), [`2078ff1da`](https://github.com/constructorfabric/gears-rust/commit/2078ff1da266a327572515c50cd9a51e8dbf4744), [`141f08c64`](https://github.com/constructorfabric/gears-rust/commit/141f08c649b048bdf4b4c9e7add110efb09054c6)); the SDK was built differently in [`1d3778927`](https://github.com/constructorfabric/gears-rust/commit/1d377892739a97e1c853ddb75a27f1670b630152) and the ADR was never marked superseded ([design-era docs](#why-the-docs-drifted)).
- **Why this decision:** ADR-0015 is `proposed`, and a proposed ADR that disagrees with the code is stale; the built SDK is the decision that was actually taken, so it gets its own short ADR.
- **Impact:** SDK consumers and reviewers get an ADR that matches the crate. Not breaking.
