### Not verified

These claims are kept as the original states them. Reviewers should know they rest on reading, not on a run.

- The [U-1](#other-doc-only-fixes-proxy) and [U-2](#u-2-a-large-request-whose-upstream-answers-early-eg-413-before-reading-the-body-gets-a-gateway-503-instead-of-the-upstreams-response) probe results come from a throwaway probe test that is not in the repo. That includes status, header and body preservation for 400–504, and the early-413 threshold between 200 KiB and 1 MiB.
- Whether a client that is still sending gets a connection reset after the Content-Length precheck rejects its request ([U-2](#u-2-a-large-request-whose-upstream-answers-early-eg-413-before-reading-the-body-gets-a-gateway-503-instead-of-the-upstreams-response), [P-06](#p-06-an-oversized-body-gets-400-out_of_range-from-oagw-or-a-plain-text-413-from-the-api-gateway)).
- *Likely* items were traced statically, not run: [P-02](#p-02-percent-encoded--in-the-proxy-path-reaches-the-upstream-unchanged) (upstreams decode `%2e%2e`), [P-17](#other-doc-only-fixes-proxy), [PLG-17](#plg-17-headers-written-by-auth-and-transform-plugins-are-not-re-checked-for-hop-by-hop-names-likely), [R-04](#r-04-an-ancestors-private-auth-rate-limit-and-plugins-reach-descendants-that-omit-the-field) (credstore refusing the ancestor's secret), [STR-01](#str-01-a-mid-stream-upstream-failure-appends-problem-json-to-a-200-sse-stream-likely), [STR-02](#str-02-sse-and-websocket-streams-are-cut-after-30-s-of-upstream-silence-not-after-the-300-s-idle-timeouts-likely), [STR-05](#str-05-a-websocket-upgrade-to-an-https-upstream-fails-whenever-tls-negotiation-picks-http2-likely), [STR-07](#str-07-an-abnormal-upstream-websocket-drop-writes-problem-json-bytes-into-the-open-tunnel-likely), [M-04](#m-04-the-upstream-protocol-field-accepts-any-string-and-is-never-used) (grpc over https proxied as plain HTTP) and [SCH-01](#sch-01-types-registry-holds-empty-placeholder-schemas-for-the-oagw-types-so-it-checks-nothing-about-upstreams-routes-or-plugins) (one bad provisioned instance fails startup; the call chain is now linked).
- [A-01](#a-01-the-docs-describe-database-persistence-but-all-config-lives-in-memory-and-restsdk-created-config-is-lost-on-restart): the count of about 55 checkboxes to untick, and its per-file breakdown, were not recounted.
- [O-06](#status-markers-checkbox-sync): each step in the untick list was checked to exist and be `[x]`, but not traced one by one in the code.
- [M-01](#m-01-any-authenticated-caller-can-create-change-and-delete-its-tenants-upstreams-and-routes): how many existing tests the new permission checks will break depends on the mock authz resolver, which was not traced.
- [M-13](#m-13-changes-made-through-the-sdk-leave-the-load-balancer-and-rate-limit-state-stale): "keeps routing to the old IPs until restart" is inferred from the load-balancer cache code; no test exercises it.
- [STR-06](#str-06-every-rejected-websocket-upgrade-returns-the-same-gateway-error-the-upstreams-own-401403404-is-lost): whether Pingora's own connect, TLS and timeout failures reach the upgrade parser as 503/504 was not traced end to end.
- [P-06](#p-06-an-oversized-body-gets-400-out_of_range-from-oagw-or-a-plain-text-413-from-the-api-gateway), [P-08](#p-08-unreachable-tls-failed-and-http-only-violating-upstreams-get-different-statuses-than-documented), [STR-06](#str-06-every-rejected-websocket-upgrade-returns-the-same-gateway-error-the-upstreams-own-401403404-is-lost): what PR [#4911](https://github.com/constructorfabric/gears-rust/pull/4911) changes is taken from the decision log. Its diff was not inspected.
- [CFG-VAL](#cfg-val-oagw-starts-with-a-zero-proxy-timeout-body-limit-or-token-cache-size-instead-of-refusing-the-config): that `proxy_timeout_secs: 0` or `max_body_size_bytes: 0` breaks every request, and what `token_cache_capacity: 0` does at runtime.
- [A-05](#config-reference): which api-gateway layer enforces the 30 s request timeout was not traced to one line.
- The "Code follows" column of §10 and the Implemented column of the appendix are taken from the item findings, not re-derived.

## Questions for the author

No decision was changed. Each question is about a fact a decision may rest on, or about a gap in a follow-up.

### Facts a decision may rest on

- [PLG-02](#plg-02-route-plugins-with-the-default-sharing-private-replace-the-upstreams-plugins-instead-of-running-after-them): the decision fixes the route merge. The scenario 4.6-A e2e test it asks for goes through the tenant-layer merge, which still replaces the parent's plugins under default `private`. Is the tenant-layer merge in scope, or should the scenario set `sharing: inherit`?
- [PLG-03](#plg-03-an-unknown-plugin-id-gives-401-auth-500-guard-or-is-silently-skipped-transform-not-the-documented-503): the decision says 503 `plugin_not_found`, but the existing `PluginNotFound` error maps to 404 and a unit test asserts that. Change the mapping, or add a separate proxy-time error?
- [M-12](#m-12-a-disabled-upstream-silently-falls-through-to-an-ancestors-upstream-instead-of-returning-503): [`PRD.md:217`](https://github.com/constructorfabric/gears-rust/blob/66da80e5715e10c08c6fafd5e9832137389a3355/gears/system/oagw/docs/PRD.md#L217) says “If an ancestor tenant disables an upstream, it **MUST** be disabled for all descendants”. The decision lets a descendant's own upstream under the same alias keep working. Is that the intended reading, or does the PRD need a clarifying edit?
- [M-04](#m-04-the-upstream-protocol-field-accepts-any-string-and-is-never-used): the decision allows only "a registered protocol GTS id (http only today)", but the catalog also registers the gRPC protocol. Should gRPC be rejected as "not supported" (per [STR-09](#str-09-grpc-and-webtransport-configs-are-accepted-but-grpc-traffic-never-works-and-webtransport-is-proxied-as-plain-https)) rather than as unregistered?
- [M-10](#m-10-credential-references-are-resolved-at-write-time-for-secret_ref-only-and-the-docs-describe-a-different-check): the decision cites the credential-isolation principle for "fail closed", but the principle text does not say that. The decision still follows from the code and F0005.
- [R-03](#r-03-under-an-inherit-ancestor-a-child-can-raise-its-own-limit-above-the-ancestors): [`PRD.md:336`](https://github.com/constructorfabric/gears-rust/blob/66da80e5715e10c08c6fafd5e9832137389a3355/gears/system/oagw/docs/PRD.md#L336) opens with “Descendant can only be stricter”, which, read alone, contradicts letting an `inherit` child raise its limit. Should that sentence be clarified, with product sign-off?
- [STR-13](#str-13-the-sdk-sse-parser-does-not-follow-the-w3c-rules-it-claims-to-follow): two parser tests record deliberate choices that the W3C decision reverses: yield metadata-only blocks ("consumers decide"), and flush a final event without the trailing blank line ("some servers don't send" it). Should W3C override both?
- [P-16](#p-16-there-is-no-per-route-timeout-and-oagw-sets-no-timeout-on-plugin-calls): OAuth2 token calls already have a 30 s per-attempt timeout, but with retries and no total bound the worst case is over 2 minutes. Should the fix be a total bound under the api-gateway's 30 s cap?
- [P-02](#p-02-percent-encoded--in-the-proxy-path-reaches-the-upstream-unchanged): rejecting every encoded `/` also blocks upstreams that carry `%2F` inside a segment on purpose (for example GitLab-style `group%2Fname`). Is that scope intended?
- [P-03](#p-03-a-common-suffix-pool-called-without-x-oagw-target-host-round-robins-instead-of-returning-400): the model stores only the alias, so "derived common-suffix alias" has to be recomputed. What should happen when an operator sets an explicit alias equal to the common suffix?
- [P-05](#p-05-over-rest-hyper-answers-a-bad-content-length-or-transfer-encoding-with-a-bare-400-before-oagw-runs): scenario 7.4-B declares a Content-Length larger than the body. hyper waits for the missing bytes and sends no 400, so the doc fix needs the real client-visible result.
- [U-3](#u-3-a-response-phase-guard-that-rejects-with-5xx-makes-the-client-get-503-instead-of-the-documented-502): guard rejections carry no phase. Should a request-phase guard's 5xx stay 503 while the response-phase one becomes 502?
- [U-2](#u-2-a-large-request-whose-upstream-answers-early-eg-413-before-reading-the-body-gets-a-gateway-503-instead-of-the-upstreams-response): on the SDK streaming path the failure comes through the `abort_rx` branch, which the biased select prefers even when a response is ready. Should the fix cover that branch explicitly?
- [CFG-HTTP](#cfg-http-the-mini-chat-helm-chart-turns-on-plaintext-http-upstreams-against-the-https-only-constraint): the chart line came in with a mini-chat provider commit that gives no reason for plaintext upstreams. The documentation note needs that reason. Should DESIGN's HTTPS-only text change, or only get a note next to it?
- [CFG-VAL](#cfg-val-oagw-starts-with-a-zero-proxy-timeout-body-limit-or-token-cache-size-instead-of-refusing-the-config): `token_cache_capacity: 0` may only disable token caching rather than break requests. Is rejecting it intended?
- [A-06](#a-06-docs-put-every-oagw-path-under-apioagwv1-but-the-gear-serves-oagwv1-and-error-instance-values-are-built-inconsistently): 289 lines contain `/api/oagw/v1`, not 286. Which set was meant?
- [C-05](#scenario-catalogue): the comment-only change covers 14 test files, not 18. Please confirm the scope.

### Gaps in follow-ups

- Product sign-off is needed but not recorded for [O-01](#o-01-operators-get-6-of-the-12-documented-metrics-pushed-over-otlp-there-is-no-metrics-endpoint) (PRD `/metrics`), [O-04](#o-04-no-audit-log-config-change-log-or-log-sampling-and-the-trace-breaks-at-oagw) (PRD audit and logging MUST), [STR-09](#str-09-grpc-and-webtransport-configs-are-accepted-but-grpc-traffic-never-works-and-webtransport-is-proxied-as-plain-https) (PRD WebTransport p1 MUST) and [PLG-10](#plg-10-a-missing-secret-gives-500-while-an-unreachable-credential-store-or-idp-gives-401-and-the-status-differs-by-plugin) (PRD "any auth failure → 401"). None of these outcomes includes `PRD`.
- [R-10](#r-10-strategy-queue-and-degrade-are-accepted-but-behave-as-reject-the-queue-block-is-dropped): the follow-ups mark only F0006 and the scenarios as planned. Do [`PRD.md:246`](https://github.com/constructorfabric/gears-rust/blob/66da80e5715e10c08c6fafd5e9832137389a3355/gears/system/oagw/docs/PRD.md#L246) and ADR-0004's strategy enum ([`ADR-0004:282`](https://github.com/constructorfabric/gears-rust/blob/66da80e5715e10c08c6fafd5e9832137389a3355/gears/system/oagw/docs/ADR/0004-rate-limiting.md#L282)) also need a planned marker?
- [PLG-13](#plg-13-route-level-cors-is-ignored-unless-sharing-inherit-and-a-descendants-own-private-or-enforce-cors-is-dropped): the doc list misses [`PRD.md:290`](https://github.com/constructorfabric/gears-rust/blob/66da80e5715e10c08c6fafd5e9832137389a3355/gears/system/oagw/docs/PRD.md#L290) and [`ADR-0003:130`](https://github.com/constructorfabric/gears-rust/blob/66da80e5715e10c08c6fafd5e9832137389a3355/gears/system/oagw/docs/ADR/0003-plugin-system.md#L130), which both describe `Upstream.cors`. It also doesn't say what happens to the SDK's public CORS types and to the `cors` properties in the OpenAPI file.
- [PLG-17](#plg-17-headers-written-by-auth-and-transform-plugins-are-not-re-checked-for-hop-by-hop-names-likely): operator header rules (`set`/`add`) also run after the hop-by-hop strip. Should the re-strip cover them?
- [PLG-06](#other-doc-only-fixes-plugins): the `path` field's "Mutable" rustdoc is as misleading as the `detail` one. Fix both?
- [P-08](#p-08-unreachable-tls-failed-and-http-only-violating-upstreams-get-different-statuses-than-documented), [P-19](#p-19-x-forwarded--and-x-real-ip-reach-the-upstream-under-passthrough-all): P-08 hands the SSRF DNS-block status (503 today, 400 in F0008) to P-19, but P-19 only covers header stripping and status markers. Nothing decides that status.
- [O-04](#o-04-no-audit-log-config-change-log-or-log-sampling-and-the-trace-breaks-at-oagw): the completion log being extended runs only for requests that reach Pingora (*Likely*). Requests rejected earlier (401, 403, 429, 400) would still have no log line, so this alone may not meet "100% of requests logged".
- [O-06](#status-markers-checkbox-sync): the F0008 SSRF algorithm includes the unbuilt header-strip steps (P-19). Should its ID line stay `[ ]` until P-19 lands?
- [A-02](#a-02-the-documented-multi-layer-config-cache-l1l2redis-does-not-exist): the follow-up says to untick F0008's cache steps and DoD, but they are already `[ ]`. Is only the future-facing marker meant?
- [M-11](#m-11-unknown-or-wrong-type-plugin-ids-are-accepted-on-write-and-only-fail-or-are-silently-skipped-at-proxy-time): write-time plugin validation will also break management unit tests that bind short plugin IDs such as `"noop"`. This widens the fix PR.
- [R-09](#r-09-a-shared-budget-with-scopetenant-is-not-shared-and-budgettotal-is-ignored-at-runtime): scenario 18.7-B's JSON has no `sharing` field, so the parent is invisible to descendants. The new e2e test probably needs `sharing: inherit`.
- [R-05](#r-05-descendants-and-routes-can-bypass-an-ancestors-enforced-plugins-and-rate-limit), [R-16](#r-16-f0005-bind-and-permission-rules-differ-from-the-code): bind must reject only removal or reconfiguration of enforced plugins, so `bind_allows_plugins_override_on_enforce` stays green.
- [SCH-01](#sch-01-types-registry-holds-empty-placeholder-schemas-for-the-oagw-types-so-it-checks-nothing-about-upstreams-routes-or-plugins): once real schemas are registered, OAGW's 14 built-in instances must also validate, or OAGW init fails. The test should cover them as well as provisioned samples.
- [STR-09](#str-09-grpc-and-webtransport-configs-are-accepted-but-grpc-traffic-never-works-and-webtransport-is-proxied-as-plain-https): PRD says gRPC is phase 4, and DESIGN and ADR-0014 say Phase 3. Which phase should the markers name?
- [STR-06](#str-06-every-rejected-websocket-upgrade-returns-the-same-gateway-error-the-upstreams-own-401403404-is-lost): the decision log gives no rationale. The item cites the PRD error-source scope; please confirm.
- §10 rows that no item decides: plugin-system priority (p1 vs p2), whether a tenant can list its ancestors' upstreams ([`DESIGN.md:655`](https://github.com/constructorfabric/gears-rust/blob/66da80e5715e10c08c6fafd5e9832137389a3355/gears/system/oagw/docs/DESIGN.md#L655) vs [`DESIGN.md:865`](https://github.com/constructorfabric/gears-rust/blob/66da80e5715e10c08c6fafd5e9832137389a3355/gears/system/oagw/docs/DESIGN.md#L865)), where the queue config lives (`concurrency_limit` vs `rate_limit`), log levels vs events for plugin execution, the ADR-0005 error body format, 429 vs 503 for a rejecting limit (ADR-0004 vs ADR-0012), and whether DESIGN's error table should list the concurrency and queue errors as future.
- §10 rows that their item settles only in part: merge order (the docs' described order), WebSocket idle ([`F0007:125`](https://github.com/constructorfabric/gears-rust/blob/66da80e5715e10c08c6fafd5e9832137389a3355/gears/system/oagw/docs/features/0007-cpt-cf-oagw-feature-streaming.md#L125) still says 504) and the audit log field list (`trace_id`).
- Appendix: `test_websocket_echo_large_payload` is mapped to 14.6 and also listed as having no scenario. The 10.2 row counts all 7 CORS tests, though 2 have no scenario. The 3.5 row says "indirect" without naming a test. The 13 custom-header-routing scenarios and the 4 flow docs have no rows.
